# Whole-Body (Capability) — Five-Perspective Review

> [!abstract] What this is
> A five-perspective adversarial review of every **cluster** and **research direction** in this doc, generated 2026-06-20.
> Each unit was put through a fixed 4-prompt lens by an independent analyst then an independent peer reviewer:
> **P1** five expert perspectives (Practitioner / Academic / Skeptic / Economist / Historian) · **P2** contradiction map ·
> **P3** synthesis briefing (for a researcher deciding whether to *commit* to the bet) · **P4** independent peer review (confidence 1-10 + Stanford-prof grade).
> Every novelty verdict was stress-tested against the live literature via the alphaxiv MCP. Cited paper ids are real (vault KH notes or alphaxiv hits).

---

## Whole-Body — doc-level synthesis

### Capsule

Whole-Body is the most internally-coherent and the most physically-grounded doc of the Capability axis: four clusters that all attack the same underlying object, the dynamic coupling between a humanoid's base/legs and its arms, from four angles, A) make the arm->base inertial reaction an explicit learned quantity, B) factor base+arm coordination (and base-mounted memory) for mobile-manipulation, C) certify balance-under-load with a wrench-aware safety barrier, D) transfer/retarget whole-body loco-manipulation across embodiments and measure when contact-fidelity predicts downstream success. Novelty is genuinely high where the bet is a *formulation* nobody has written down (A1 explicit arm->base reaction, B2 active-gaze for dynamic relocation, C2 the payload-parameterized safe set, D1 the contact-fidelity->SR regression) and thins to "analysis-paper" or "pre-empted" where the bet is a stratification/diagnosis of a settled main effect (B1, B3, A2, A4). The value concentrates not in any single method but in three durable *artifacts* the doc keeps rediscovering independently: a crossover/scaling law (D3), a certified envelope/safe-set formulation (C, C2), and a graded measurement protocol (A3, B1, D1) that survives the next foundation model. Graded A-/B+ in the strong clusters (A, C) and B in the diagnosis-heavy ones (A2-A4, B1-B3). The pervasive weakness is evidentiary, not conceptual: roughly half the load-bearing numbers are sourced to the card, not to the vault note they cite.

### Cross-direction pattern

The recurring bottleneck is that **every cluster formalizes the coupling as a rigid-body / kinematic object and is blind to the contact-and-actuation channel that actually destabilizes a legged humanoid.** A frames it as the inertial term M_base,arm; B as base-travel distance / factoring order; C as a CoM-in-fixed-support-polygon barrier; D as kinematic phase / morphology distance. In all four, the missing-sixth perspective is the same control-theorist/hardware-dynamicist who points out the destabilizing physics lives elsewhere, the contact-wrench and support-polygon transition (A1, C2), actuator torque-density and step-response (C1, D3), force/momentum coupling on a balancing base (D2), the QP/control-loop bandwidth in the first 100 ms (C1). The doc's "whole-body" object is, almost everywhere, secretly a floating-base-manipulator object with the legs assumed away. Compounding this is a second shared pattern, the doc's strongest novelty is consistently a *benchmark or measurement protocol*, not a method, and four independent clusters (A3 "protocol over controller," B1 "diagnosis not method," D1/D3 "the benchmark is the durable asset") arrive at that exit from completely different premises.

### Sharpest intra-doc contradiction

**A1 vs D3 on whether structured priors beat scale, stated as a direct collision of theses.** A1's whole bet is that pushing *back* toward explicit structure (an analytic + learned arm->base coupling term) beats HEX's implicit end-to-end MoE on fixed data, a low-data, structured-prior wins. D3's own Historian/Skeptic spine argues the opposite for the sibling problem: every "cheap structured transfer beats brute scale" bet (LoRA, modular SLAM, hand features) won briefly on a compute budget and then lost to the foundation model, and XHugWBC's +10% fine-tune already shows the generalist is the cheap-transfer substrate. A1 commits to the structured-prior side of the analytic-vs-end-to-end pendulum at limb scale; D3 concludes the structured side is a transient-regime artifact at body scale and tells the researcher to publish the regime boundary instead of the structure. Both cannot be the durable bet; the doc never reconciles whether explicit structure is the asset or the expiring move. A sharper, narrower collision also sits inside D itself: D1 and D4 both claim ownership of the contact-fidelity->trainability whitespace over the same anchor paper (OmniRetarget), and D4's H4 treats it as pre-empting exactly what D1 calls "the surviving D1 core" (verdict topFix for D4).

### Recurring blind spots

- **Contact / underactuation as the real coupling channel.** A, C, and D2 all model coupling as inertial/kinematic; none owns the foot-ground contact wrench, support-polygon transitions, or made/broken contact sets that distinguish a humanoid from a floating-base arm. C2's safe set may not even be well-posed when the robot braces or steps to recover.
- **Actuation/dynamic heterogeneity.** C1 (actuator rise-time can physically forbid the 100 ms feedforward), D3 (the morphology cliff may be a torque/bandwidth cliff mislabeled as kinematic), and D2 (force/momentum coupling) all surface this; no hypothesis anywhere tests whether the governing distance is kinematic or dynamic.
- **Wheeled->legged transfer assumed, not tested.** B1, B2, B3, and D2 cite almost exclusively wheeled-base evidence (R1, TidyBot++, Fetch, HSR, EMMA) inside a whole-body-humanoid doc; the base-as-balance-constrained-system changes the entire premise and is never validated on legs.
- **Sim-to-real of the proposed mechanism.** B2 (gaze policy under real head-eye latency/motion-blur/rolling-shutter), D1/D4 (every fidelity->SR signal is sim downstream RL), and A1 (biased analytic M_base,arm as a new sim-to-real liability) all carry a clean-sim result that could greenlight a direction that dies on hardware.
- **Failure recovery / correlated-failure under the coupling.** B cluster notes the joint base+arm factoring makes failures correlated by construction, yet no direction owns detect-and-recover or runtime monitoring after a violation; D4 notes feasibility-enforced generators under-sample the recovery/near-failure tail.
- **Evidentiary sourcing.** Pervasive across A1, A2, A4, B, B3, C2, D2, D4: the keystone number (HEX 11/12->6/12, X-Loco 94.8%, WholeBodyVLA 66.0%, FAME's estimator equation, DreamGen >90% Pearson, 2509.03222 80%-vs-0%) is in the card but absent or only qualitative in the KH note it cites.

### Commit picks

- **C cluster + C2 (wrench-aware certified safe set):** still-whitespace, A-/B+, the payload-parameterized balance envelope is the single least-commoditizable, most-fundable asset in the doc, and its falsifier (FAME) sharpens rather than kills it by forcing a guarantee-FAME-cannot-emit framing.
- **A cluster + A1 (explicit arm->base reaction):** still-whitespace, A-/B+, solo-buildable on fixed data, HEX's UPP-removal ablation is the strongest single piece of evidence that coupling-representation is the right axis; commit gated on HEX reproducibility and contact-vs-inertial instrumentation.
- **D1 (contact-fidelity -> downstream-SR regression):** still-whitespace, B+, highest mean confidence (7.8) in the doc; a cheap, decisive regression nobody has plotted, provided the OmniRetarget collision with D4 is resolved in D1's favor.
- **B2 (active gaze for in-view dynamic relocation):** still-whitespace, B+, the one B-cluster bet that is a formulation rather than a re-plot; commit gated on quantifying the in-view-dynamic-relocation regime size first.

### Reshape picks

- **A2 (frozen-primitive convex-hull feasibility):** threat, the "convex blend stays feasible" first principle is mathematically false under contact/underactuation (a blend of two balance-feasible trajectories need not be balance-feasible), and the keystone X-Loco 94.8% number is unsourced; the feasibility-by-construction thesis can void outright.
- **B3 (multi-room out-of-view memory scaling law):** threat, likely pre-empted from a field it never searched (lifelong/dynamic-scene SLAM), with SOMA as an un-read pre-emptor and the "scaling law" possibly being an error-accumulation curve; lowest mean confidence (6.2) in the doc.
- **A4 (manip-vs-loco latent split):** threat, the de-risk thesis rests on WholeBodyVLA quotes the vault cannot produce, and the split may be justified by plant-dynamics timescale separation (which survives) rather than the card's camera-motion statistics (which may falsify flat), inverting its own conclusion.
- **D2 (egocentric whole-body capture):** threat, the keystone 2509.03222 "80% vs 0%" is an underpowered side-result in an operator-workload user study, and every verified H1 datapoint is wheeled/statically-stable, so the legged force-coupling claim may be un-runnable as a robot-free capture claim.
- **B1 (conditional factoring stratification):** threat, EBench already does five-dimensional mobile-manip diagnostic profiling, collapsing the surviving whitespace to a continuous-binning re-plot one benchmark-release from closure.

### Doc grade

**B+.** The most coherent and physically-serious doc of the axis, with two genuinely strong, fundable, still-whitespace anchors (C/C2, A/A1) and the highest-confidence single direction in the review (D1). It is held below A- by two systemic faults that recur in over half the units: a contact-and-actuation blind spot that means the "whole-body" coupling is repeatedly formalized as the wrong (rigid-body, kinematic, fixed-support-polygon) object, and an evidentiary-sourcing problem where the load-bearing number is in the card but not the vault. Several B-grade directions (A2, A4, B1, B3, D2) are diagnosis/stratification slices of settled main effects or rest on unverified out-of-card claims and need reshape before commit.

---

### A (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster A — "Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation" (A1–A4), whose shared bottleneck is stated as *"the arm–leg coupling is non-separable: an arm reach is a balance disturbance the legs must anticipate, and flat RL fails on the coupled high-DoF action space."* The cluster intro frames the four directions as attacking "the same non-separability from different layers": A1 models the coupling term itself, A2 composes feasible primitives so the action never leaves the feasible set, A3 closes the precision loop in the world frame with the base as an active transport DoF, A4 emits joint loco+manip commands through one latent interface. Does this grouping carve the problem at the right joint, and is the shared bet sound?

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys robot policies daily)**
- *Core position*: The cluster's non-separability bottleneck is real on hardware — anyone who has bolted an arm policy onto a leg policy has watched a fast reach tip the robot — but the four directions are bottlenecked by *different* practical realities, not one shared joint. A1's explicit-coupling bet lives or dies on the inertia model and the control-loop budget; A2/A3/A4 live or die on sim-to-real and data, and lumping them under one "coupling" headline hides that.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: A1's own anchor evidence is a *real-robot* signal — HEX 79.8% ID / 61.8% OOD vs GR00T N1.5 70.2/41.0 ([[2604.07993|HEX]]), and FALCON's 0.37-vs-0.60 upper-body tracking error under 100 N cart-pull + 1.2 kg/hand ([[2505.06776|FALCON-Loco-Manipulation]]). But SkillBlender ([[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]), the A2 anchor, is *state-based only, never deployed real* (its own named sim2real gap), and HiWET's 12.4 mm is sim with 12–15 mm real on *one* G1. The practitioner reads this as: A1 has a hardware story, A2 does not, A3 has one platform.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The arm→base inertial term A1 wants to predict ($\hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$) is exactly the quantity worst-corrupted by the URDF/mass-distribution error you have on a real robot — gripper payload, cable drag, battery shift. ADAPT ([[2606.16542|ADAPT]], found via alphaxiv) shows the *practitioner-grade* version: a momentum observer that needs FAST-LIO LiDAR odometry just to get root velocity clean enough to estimate the lumped disturbance. A1's predictor inherits that estimation burden, so the real first experiment is "how wrong is my analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ on hardware," not "does explicit beat implicit in sim."

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature)**
- *Core position*: The grouping is defensible as a taxonomy but the *shared* bottleneck claim is weaker than the card asserts: only A1 is literally about the coupling *term*; A2 (feasibility-by-composition), A3 (frame choice), and A4 (latent interface) are independently-motivated problems that touch coupling only glancingly. The literature already treats these as separate sub-fields with separate venues.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card itself concedes the erosion. A4's own anchor [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]] ranks the visual-dynamics split *third* ("beneficial but not the primary factor," behind +38.7% action-free pretraining and +24.0% LMO policy), so A4's live wedge is a narrow camera-motion-stratified ablation, not "non-separability." A2 admits "the downstream-RL-reference half is now near-consensus." A3's four world-frame precedents ([[2407.10353|UMI-on-Legs]], [[2409.16048|WB-EE-Pose-Tracking]], [[2412.03012|Omni-WBLM]], [[2507.08656|Multi-Critic-Twist-Tracking]]) are *quadrupeds* — a frame-choice lineage, not a coupling lineage. The common thread across A2/A3/A4 is closer to "factor the whole-body problem cleanly," not "the inertia matrix is non-block-diagonal."
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: SEEC ([[2509.21231|SEEC]]) is the linchpin the academic must scrutinize — the card builds A1's entire novelty on SEEC's stated assumption that arm→base back-coupling is "negligible because arms are dynamically light." But SEEC's KH note shows its *contribution* is robustness to unseen controllers (34.40% vs 57.45% degradation), and its premise holds for *light arms carrying light objects*. The card's own A1 table lists [[2506.14278|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] (40–60% limb mass) and [[2602.11758|HAIC]] (object-side dynamics prediction) — so the literature has *already* moved past "arms are light" for the heavy/loaded regime, which means A1's wedge is "explicit *learned* prediction of arm→base," not "anyone-noticing arm→base-matters."

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved)**
- *Core position*: A1's specific bet — that an *explicit predicted* arm→base term beats HEX's implicit MoE on the *same fixed data* by ≥+3 pp OOD — is the kind of within-architecture ablation that papers run internally and bury, and the surrounding mechanism space is saturated. The "non-separability" headline is true but trivially so; the field already restores the coupling a dozen ways, and the card lists 28 of them.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own A1 table is the indictment: [[2504.06662|RAMBO]] is "the exact analytic-term + learned-residual recipe A1 borrows," [[2510.05070|ResMimic]] is "the cleanest external instance of A1's exact framing (add the coupling as a residual on a fixed whole-body backbone)," [[2602.11758|HAIC]] is "the closest new paper in mechanism to A1's bet (predict-the-coupling-quantity-then-compensate)." The wedge that survives is purely *directional* (arm→base vs base→arm) and *representational* (predicted term vs reactive residual). ADAPT ([[2606.16542|ADAPT]]) now adds a momentum-observer that estimates the generalized disturbance $\tau_e$ from $M(q)\dot v + C v + g = \tau_m + \tau_e$ — the same $M(q)$ A1 invokes — and feeds it to the policy. The skeptic's charge: the delta between "estimate the lumped reaction and react" (ADAPT) and "predict the arm-induced reaction and feed-forward" (A1) may be a few percent that vanishes once HEX's MoE is at matched capacity (H1's own falsifier).
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The cluster is being held together by A1 as "the lead," but A1 is the *most* pre-empted of the four in mechanism and the *least* deployable. If A1's +3 pp doesn't materialize (the card's own H1 falsifier: "explicit ≤ 61.8% → the MoE already captures it"), the cluster's claimed synergy ("A1's predictor is the balance model A2/A3 both need") collapses to four loosely-related papers.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives)**
- *Core position*: Whole-body humanoid loco-manip is where the 2025–26 capital is — every anchor is an industry-or-industry-adjacent lab racing the same G1/H1 benchmarks — so the cluster is fishing in the most crowded, fastest-saturating pond in embodied AI. The incentive structure rewards a *new benchmark + a headline SR number on a real humanoid*, which favors A2/A4 (system papers with SR tables) over A1 (a mechanism ablation that needs HEX's exact backbone to be legible).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The anchor roster reads like a vendor map: HEX from Beijing Innovation Center of Humanoid Robotics, FALCON from CMU/Field AI/Nissan, SkillBlender from Berkeley/Stanford/USC/PKU, ADAPT from NTU MARS Lab — all on Unitree G1/H1. The benchmark-chasing is explicit: the cluster cross-references HumanoidBench, SkillBench, LHM-Humanoid's 350-scene suite, HumanoidArena, AGILE diagnostics. The economist notes that A1's whole bet is parasitic on one lab's artifact (HEX's 12M-frame backbone + UPP ablation); if that lab doesn't release weights, A1's "re-run HEX's UPP ablation with the explicit term swapped in" (H1's test) is unrunnable — a structural risk no other direction carries.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The cheapest, most fundable move is *not* A1 — it is A2's "pipeline-location head-to-head" (online-blend vs upstream-prior vs distilled-monolith on one shared 8-task/3-embodiment set), because it produces a clean comparison table across already-published systems with no dependence on a single proprietary backbone, and the reuse-vs-monolith debate ([[2506.12779|Experts-to-Generalist]], [[2606.06493|HANDOFF]]) is live and citation-hot.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves)**
- *Core position*: A1 is the latest turn of the oldest wheel in legged control: the model-based-vs-learned and feedforward-vs-feedback cycles. "Predict the reaction analytically and feed it forward" is centroidal-momentum / operational-space control reborn as a learned residual; the field has swung modular→end-to-end→hybrid before, and A1 sits squarely in the current "analytic prior + learned residual" reconciliation phase.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The A1 table *is* a history of the swing: pure-analytic ([[2506.14278|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] centroidal-momentum, [[2603.02443|Safe-WBLM]] reference-governor QP), analytic+residual ([[2504.06662|RAMBO]], [[2509.21231|SEEC]]), pure-learned ([[2603.10306|SteadyTray]] ReST-RL), implicit-learned ([[2604.07993|HEX]] MoE). Arm-swing-for-balance ([[2507.04140|Centroidal-Arm-Motion]], MIT) is literally the biomechanics insight that drove 2010s humanoid CAM controllers, now re-derived as a multi-agent RL reward. The historian recognizes ADAPT's momentum observer as De Luca's classic generalized-momentum residual (2000s) wired into PPO.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Every prior "make the coupling explicit" wave (operational-space inertia shaping, centroidal-momentum control) lost to "let a high-capacity learner absorb it" once compute and data arrived — which is precisely the bet HEX's implicit MoE represents and A1's H1 risks repeating on the losing side. The historically-informed hedge is to frame A1 not as "explicit beats implicit" (a bet history has lost before) but as "explicit *initializes/regularizes* implicit and wins in the data-poor, high-arm-acceleration tail" — the regime where analytic priors have *historically* held their edge.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Where 2+ perspectives directly clash.**
   - *A1's centrality*: SKEPTIC + ECONOMIST say A1 is the weakest commit (most pre-empted in mechanism, least deployable, parasitic on HEX's backbone) and A2 is the cheaper/cleaner bet; the card (and PRACTITIONER, partly) treats A1 as "the lead" whose predictor "A2/A3 both need." Specific clash: card claims removing HEX's coupling component (UPP) is "the single largest drop (Pouring 11/12→6/12)" so coupling is *the* lever; SKEPTIC counters that the *implicit* MoE already captured that drop, and the *explicit-over-implicit* delta (the only thing A1 owns) is the unproven +3 pp.
   - *Is the bet already solved?*: SKEPTIC says the mechanism space is saturated ([[2504.06662|RAMBO]]/[[2510.05070|ResMimic]]/[[2602.11758|HAIC]] = "exact framing"/"exact recipe"/"closest mechanism"); HISTORIAN + ACADEMIC say the *specific arm→base direction as a learned forward prediction* is genuinely unrun and the "arms-are-light" premise ([[2509.21231|SEEC]]) is a real, falsifiable opening in the loaded/fast regime.
   - *Explicit vs implicit*: HISTORIAN warns explicit-beats-implicit is a bet history has lost (learners absorb structure once data arrives, à la HEX's MoE); PRACTITIONER says on real hardware in the data-poor tail the analytic prior earns its keep. Direct contradiction on whether the +3 pp survives at scale.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the most evidence-bound and uses the card's *own* concessions (A4 split ranked third; A2 reference-half is consensus; A3 precedents are quadrupeds) to show the "single shared bottleneck" is partly rhetorical, while still locating the one real opening (SEEC's premise in the heavy/fast regime). It neither over- nor under-claims.
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — directionally right that the pond is crowded and A1 is backbone-dependent, but its "follow the money" frame adds little falsifiable content here; the incentive story doesn't change whether the coupling term is physically real, and its A2-is-more-fundable claim is a strategy preference, not evidence about the thesis.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   - *On HEX's exact backbone and fixed dataset, at matched parameter count and matched control-loop latency, does an explicit predicted arm→base reaction term ($\hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$, analytic + learned residual) raise OOD success above HEX's implicit-MoE 61.8% — and is that gain concentrated in the top arm-acceleration quartile where SEEC's "arms-are-light" premise is false?* A yes resolves SKEPTIC-vs-card and HISTORIAN-vs-PRACTITIONER in A1's favor; a no collapses A1 to the MoE and demotes the cluster's "A1 is the lead" claim.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   - The *physical* bottleneck is real and non-separable: the inertia matrix $M(q)$ is non-block-diagonal, an arm reach *is* a balance disturbance, and naive part-wise stacks visibly fail (uncoordinated/unstable). HEX's UPP-removal drop, FALCON's under-load gap, and ADAPT's emergent torso-tilt under asymmetric load all independently confirm coupling matters. The disagreement is only about *how much of A1's specific explicit-prediction wedge survives*, not about whether coupling is load-bearing.

5. **The blind spot (topic none addressed).**
   - *Contact and underactuation as the real coupling channel.* Every perspective argued about the *inertial* arm→base term ($M_{\text{base,arm}}$), but on a walking biped the dominant arm→base coupling at the moment that destabilizes is mediated through the **foot–ground contact / support-polygon and the underactuated floating base**, not just rigid-body inertia. A reach perturbs the *contact wrench* and can break contact; an inertial-only predictor that ignores contact-state transitions (single↔double support, impending slip) may predict the wrong reaction exactly when it matters. None of the five framed the coupling as a *contact-constrained, hybrid-dynamics* problem — yet that is what separates a humanoid from the floating-base manipulators the inertial story implicitly assumes.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary (nuanced).**
   Cluster A is built on a physically *correct* and well-evidenced bottleneck — whole-body loco-manipulation is non-separable because the inertia matrix is non-block-diagonal, and part-wise stacks demonstrably fail. But "non-separability" is doing double duty: it is a genuine shared *substrate* yet only A1 directly attacks the coupling *term*, while A2 (feasible composition), A3 (world-frame + active base), and A4 (unified latent) are independently-motivated factorings that the card itself admits are drifting toward consensus on their broadest claims. The cluster is therefore *coherent as a layered taxonomy* but *over-unified in its headline*: the synergy story ("A1's predictor is the balance model A2/A3/A4 all need") is real only if A1's narrow, still-unproven wedge — an *explicit predicted arm→base reaction* beating HEX's implicit MoE on fixed data — actually lands. That wedge survives a literature check (no one predicts arm→base as a learned forward term; the closest, ADAPT, *reacts* to a lumped disturbance rather than *predicting* the self-induced reaction), but it is the most pre-empted-in-mechanism and least-deployable of the four directions, and history warns that explicit-beats-implicit is a bet learners have won before. Commit to the cluster, but pivot the *framing* of its lead.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   1. *The coupling bottleneck is physically real and load-bearing* (HEX UPP-removal = largest drop; FALCON under-load; ADAPT torso-tilt). **Support**: all five perspectives. **Challenge**: none — only the *size of A1's wedge* is contested, not the bottleneck.
   2. *A1's surviving novelty is narrow and directional/representational*: explicit *learned forward prediction* of arm→base, vs the field's reactive residuals ([[2504.06662|RAMBO]], [[2510.05070|ResMimic]], [[2509.21231|SEEC]] base→arm) and lumped-disturbance observers ([[2606.16542|ADAPT]]). **Support**: ACADEMIC, SKEPTIC, HISTORIAN. **Challenge**: card's "A1 is the lead" framing treats it as broader.
   3. *The cluster is over-unified*: A4's split ranks third even in its own anchor; A2's reference-half is "near-consensus"; A3's frame precedents are quadrupeds. The shared "non-separability" headline partly papers over four distinct problems. **Support**: ACADEMIC, ECONOMIST. **Challenge**: PRACTITIONER (the bottleneck *is* felt across all four on hardware), card framing.
   4. *A1 is the least deployable / most backbone-dependent commit*: state-of-evidence is one humanoid, and H1's test needs HEX's exact (possibly closed) backbone; the inertia term is worst-corrupted by real payload/mass error. **Support**: PRACTITIONER, ECONOMIST. **Challenge**: HISTORIAN (analytic prior helps exactly in the hard tail).
   5. *Explicit-beats-implicit may not survive at scale* — the +3 pp OOD gain is the whole bet and its own falsifier admits the MoE may already capture it. **Support**: SKEPTIC, HISTORIAN. **Challenge**: PRACTITIONER (holds in data-poor/high-accel tail), card.

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.**
   PRACTITIONER's hardware worry (the analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ is exactly the quantity payload/mass error corrupts) and HISTORIAN's lesson (explicit priors win specifically in the data-poor, high-disturbance tail) point to the *same* reframing: A1 should not be sold as "explicit beats implicit globally" but as "an analytic-prior-regularized residual that wins in the **high-arm-acceleration, loaded** regime where both data is thin and SEEC's arms-are-light premise breaks." H2 (stratify the margin by arm-acceleration quartile) is therefore not a secondary test — it *is* the thesis. Both the practitioner and the historian independently converge on the tail as the only defensible battleground.

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK (do not drop, do not blind-commit).**
   - *Verdict*: COMMIT to Cluster A as a layered program, but **re-anchor A1's thesis to the stratified-tail claim** (H2), and **hedge the cluster's backbone dependency** by making A2's pipeline-location head-to-head (the ECONOMIST's cheap, backbone-free comparison) the parallel insurance bet.
   - *Concrete next experiment (the de-risking gate)*: Before touching HEX, run the cheapest falsifier — reproduce or obtain a coupling-aware baseline (FALCON's shared-observation or a HEX-class MoE you control), and run the **arm-acceleration-stratified ablation**: explicit predicted $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}=\hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ (analytic + learned residual) vs implicit, *measuring (a) OOD-success margin per arm-acceleration quartile and (b) the measured arm→base reaction magnitude vs SEEC's "negligible" threshold in the top quartile*. Decision rule: if the top-quartile margin is significant and the bottom-quartile margin is ~0 → A1 is alive and correctly scoped to the tail (proceed to HEX). If the margin is flat or the top-quartile reaction is genuinely small → the MoE/observer captures it; demote A1 and promote A2's pipeline-location study as the lead.
   - *Mandatory scope addition*: incorporate the blind spot — instrument **contact-state / support-polygon transitions** alongside the inertial term, so the predictor is contact-aware; an inertial-only term that ignores single↔double-support transitions will mispredict exactly at the destabilizing instant.

5. **Frontier question that would change everything.**
   On an underactuated walking biped, is the destabilizing arm→base coupling dominated by the **rigid-body inertial term** ($M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$, which A1 predicts) or by the **contact-wrench / support-polygon perturbation** the reach induces (which A1 ignores)? If the latter dominates, the entire cluster's "non-block-diagonal inertia matrix" framing is the wrong first principle, and the right object to predict is the *predicted contact-state transition under an intended reach* — reframing A1, A3 (base transport breaks/makes contact), and C2 (CBF feasibility under wrench) around a single hybrid-dynamics quantity instead of an inertial one.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Independent skeptical review of the Cluster A (Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation, A1–A4) strategy briefing. Grounded in `Capability/Whole-Body.md` lines 107–468 and cross-checked against the KnowledgeHub note for ADAPT ([[2606.16542|ADAPT-Locomotion]]). The briefing is a faithful compression of the upstream 5-perspective analyst part; this review tests whether its confidence is earned.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | Justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Coupling bottleneck is physically real and load-bearing | **9** | The strongest claim. The non-block-diagonal $M(q)$ is textbook physics, and three *independent* empirical legs back it (HEX UPP-removal = largest ablation drop 11/12→6/12; FALCON 0.37 vs 0.60 under load; ADAPT emergent torso-tilt under asymmetric load — all verified in card + KH). Docked one point only because "load-bearing" silently slides from "coupling matters" (proven) to "the *inertial* term is what matters" (the Frontier Question itself disputes). |
| 2 | A1's surviving novelty is narrow, directional/representational | **8** | Verified against the card's own 28-row table: RAMBO = "exact recipe," ResMimic = "exact framing," HAIC = "closest mechanism," SEEC = base→arm-only. ADAPT (2606.16542) confirmed in KH as a momentum-observer that estimates the *external lumped* wrench reactively — so "REACTS not PREDICTS the self-induced reaction" is an accurate, not strained, distinction. The wedge survives but is thin; the score reflects that the survival rests on a *direction + feed-forward-vs-react* distinction, which is exactly the kind of gap a competitor closes in one ablation. |
| 3 | The cluster is over-unified | **8** | Each sub-claim is quotable from the card: A4 split ranks third behind +38.7% / +24.0% (lines 395/399/408); A2 reference-half "near-consensus" (line 214); A3's four world-frame precedents are all quadrupeds (lines 326–329). This is the briefing using the card's *own concessions* against its headline — high-reliability because it is internally sourced, not externally asserted. Minus two because "over-unified" is a framing verdict, not a falsifiable fact; a sympathetic reader could call the layering a feature. |
| 4 | A1 is least deployable / most backbone-dependent | **7** | H1's test ("re-run HEX's UPP ablation with the explicit term swapped in") is genuinely unrunnable without HEX's 12M-frame backbone + dataset, and the briefing is right that the analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ is exactly what payload/mass error corrupts — ADAPT's KH note confirms the practitioner cost (it needs clean root-velocity estimation just to get the lumped observer working). Docked for two soft spots: "possibly closed backbone" is an *unverified assumption* (HEX's release status is not checked), and the Historian's rebuttal (analytic prior helps *most* in the hard tail) is a real, unresolved tension the finding flags but does not settle. |
| 5 | Explicit-beats-implicit may not survive at scale | **6** | The least-confident finding, correctly self-ranked last. The +3 pp OOD target is a *stated bet*, not a measured result; its falsifier ("the MoE may already capture it") is admitted in the card's own Risks (line 199). The historical prior ("learners have won explicit-beats-implicit before") is a heuristic, not evidence — and the Historian's *counter*-prior (analytic structure wins in the data-poor tail) cuts the other way. This is a genuine coin-flip dressed as a finding; 6 is generous. |

**Mean = 7.6, Min = 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 5 (explicit-beats-implicit at scale), and within it the +3 pp OOD claim.** The entire commit-vs-pivot recommendation hinges on whether an explicit predicted $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}=\hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ beats HEX's implicit MoE by ≥+3 pp *at matched parameter count and latency*. Nothing in the vault measures this — it is a prediction, and its own falsifier concedes the MoE may already capture the signal at scale.

**What would verify it:** the briefing's own proposed gate is the right one, but it must be run on a *controllable* baseline first (a FALCON-style shared-observation or HEX-class MoE you train yourself), because the headline experiment is blocked on a third-party artifact. Specifically: (a) confirm HEX's backbone/dataset release status before committing H1 — this single unverified fact ("possibly closed") gates the whole de-risking sequence; (b) run the arm-acceleration-*stratified* margin on the controllable baseline and check that the top-quartile measured arm→base reaction actually exceeds SEEC's "negligible" threshold. If the top-quartile reaction is genuinely small, Finding 5 collapses *and* takes Finding 2's "directional novelty" with it — the wedge would be real but worthless.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**The Academic perspective over-dominated** (with the Economist as a close second amplifier). The briefing's central move — "the cluster is over-unified, A1 is the lead only rhetorically" — is the Academic's thesis verbatim, and the upstream analyst part explicitly names Academic as "Strongest." Every CEO-summary sentence is evidence-bound and card-internal, which is the Academic's signature and a genuine strength.

But the dominance shows in two tells. First, the **Practitioner is consistently relegated to "Challenge," never "Support-lead"** — its hardware objection (the analytic term is corrupted by exactly the payload error you have on a real robot) is demoted into a *reframing* of A1 rather than a reason to question whether A1's sim-first experiment plan is even the right first step. The Practitioner's deepest point (the real first experiment is "how wrong is my $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ on hardware") is softened into "sell it as a tail claim." Second, the **Skeptic's strongest charge — that the surviving delta may vanish at matched MoE capacity — is acknowledged but immediately neutralized** by the "scope it to the tail" reframing, which is an Academic save, not a Skeptic concession. The synthesis is *evidentially* balanced but *rhetorically* tilted toward "commit-and-reframe" over "the bet may be empty." A truly balanced read would have given Finding 5 equal weight to Finding 1 in the headline, rather than burying the coin-flip at rank 5.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**A control-theorist / hybrid-dynamics perspective** — and to the briefing's credit, it surfaces this itself as the Frontier Question, then fails to let it touch the conclusions. The five perspectives all argued *inside* the rigid-body inertial framing ($M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$). None asked whether that is the *right object at all*.

On an underactuated walking biped, the moment a reach destabilizes the robot, the dominant arm→base coupling is mediated through the **foot–ground contact wrench and the support-polygon / ZMP margin**, not pure rigid-body inertia — the body is a *contact-constrained hybrid system* switching between single- and double-support, with impending slip and contact-break as the failure modes. An inertial-only predictor that ignores contact-state transitions predicts the wrong reaction *exactly when it matters most* (the high-acceleration loaded reach that breaks contact). This is not a footnote: if contact-wrench perturbation dominates, the cluster's stated **first principle ("the inertia matrix is non-block-diagonal") is the wrong first principle**, and the right quantity to predict is the contact-state transition under an intended reach — which would re-found A1, A3 (base transport makes/breaks contact), and C2 (CBF feasibility under wrench) on a *single hybrid-dynamics object* instead of an inertial one.

The conclusion this changes: the briefing recommends "commit to A1, reframe to the loaded tail." A control-theorist would say the *loaded tail is precisely where the inertial story breaks*, because that is where contact margins are tightest — so the very regime the briefing wants to scope A1 *to* may be the regime where A1's chosen predictor is *wrong*, not merely narrow. The de-risking gate's "mandatory scope addition" (instrument contact-state alongside the inertial term) is the right instinct but is treated as insurance; this perspective would make it the *primary* measurement and demote the inertial term to the hypothesis under test.

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: A−.**

This is an unusually disciplined briefing. It is faithful to its sources, every quantitative claim survives a card + KH cross-check (I verified ADAPT's identity, location in C1 not A1, A4's third-place split, A2's consensus admission, and A3's all-quadruped precedents), it correctly self-ranks its weakest finding last, and its central thesis (physically-real bottleneck, rhetorically-over-unified headline, narrow-but-surviving wedge) is the correct read. The Hidden Connection (Practitioner hardware-worry + Historian tail-lesson → "analytic-prior-regularized residual that wins in the loaded tail") is a genuinely sharp synthesis. It does not rubber-stamp — it tells the reader to *pivot the lead's framing*, which is the right verdict.

It falls short of A/A+ for three reasons, in priority order:

1. **(Top fix) Promote the Frontier Question from footnote to gate.** The briefing's own best insight — that the coupling may be contact-wrench-dominated, not inertial — is logically *upstream* of the entire A1 commit, yet it is parked as the last paragraph. If the inertial first principle is wrong, the de-risking gate measures the wrong quantity. The contact-state instrumentation must be the *primary* arm of the first experiment (measure inertial-term vs contact-wrench contribution to destabilization in the top arm-acceleration quartile), not a "mandatory scope addition." Decide which object to predict *before* committing to predicting the inertial one.

2. **Verify the load-bearing deployability assumption.** Finding 4 and the entire "run A2 as parallel insurance" hedge rest on HEX's backbone being "possibly closed." This is asserted, not checked. One lookup of HEX's (2604.07993) code/weights release status either removes the structural risk or hardens it into the decisive reason to lead with A2 — currently it floats as an unpriced contingency.

3. **Give Finding 5 its true weight.** The +3 pp explicit-over-implicit bet is the cluster's load-bearing empirical wager and an admitted coin-flip, yet it sits at rank 5 while the headline reads "commit." Either elevate it (the honest framing is "commit to the *cluster*, treat A1's specific wedge as a falsifiable side-bet whose null result is likely"), or state plainly that the recommendation is to commit to the *layered program* and that A1-as-lead is explicitly the part most likely to be demoted by the first experiment. The briefing nearly says this; it should say it without the rhetorical cushion.

*Net: a strong, evidence-bound, appropriately skeptical briefing whose only real failure is under-weighting its own sharpest doubt — that the inertial first principle may be the wrong one.*

---

### A1 (Direction review)

Topic: Build a whole-body humanoid policy that makes the **arm→base reaction** an *explicit* predicted quantity ($\hat\delta_{\text{base}}=\hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$, analytic term + learned residual, auxiliary-loss target) and beats [[2604.07993|HEX]]'s implicit-MoE coupling on the *same fixed data* — widening the 41.0→61.8% OOD margin by ≥ +3pp, concentrated in the top arm-acceleration quartile where [[2509.21231|SEEC]]'s "arms-are-light" premise is false.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet is mechanically sound but operationally fragile — the explicit term lives or dies on the accuracy of $M_{\text{base,arm}}$, which depends on a URDF/mass-distribution that is wrong on every real humanoid (battery, harness, payload shift). What actually ships is whatever survives sim-to-real, and a hand-derived coupling term is a new sim-to-real liability the implicit MoE doesn't carry.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2504.06662|RAMBO]] found "providing corrective feedback at the *joint position* level rather than solely at base-acceleration level proved more effective" — i.e., on real Go2 the base-acceleration-target channel (exactly A1's $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}$ surface) was the *weaker* correction site. [[2510.05070|ResMimic]] needed a virtual-object-force *curriculum* just to stabilize early training of a reactive residual; an *predictive* residual on a noisier target is harder, not easier.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The H6 latency framing is backwards — the risk is not whether the predictor fits a single-digit-ms budget (it will; it's a small head), it's that the analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ you feed it is computed from a wrong inertia model, so you spend your error budget on a *systematically biased* feedforward that the MoE never had to trust. Profile coupling-prediction sim-vs-real error *first*, before any success-rate claim.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is real but narrow — the literature has every neighbor (anticipatory, emergent, analytic+residual, base→arm-explicit) except the exact arm→base learned-prediction cell, so the contribution is a *representation-of-coupling* ablation, not a new capability. The card is honest that HEX's pretraining is second-order and the UPP (coupling) is the lever, which is the strongest single piece of evidence the field has that "coupling representation" is the right axis.
- *Strongest evidence*: HEX's own ablation (Pouring 11/12→6/12 on UPP removal vs second-order pretraining gain) is a clean within-paper control. [[2509.21231|SEEC]] *explicitly* states the recipe (analytic + learned residual under torque-matching loss) and *explicitly* asserts arm→base is negligible — so the field has already drawn the boundary A1 wants to cross, and named it.
- *Only-this-perspective*: SEEC's "negligible" claim is an *empirical* claim made for the *standing/walking-EE-stabilization* regime, not a theorem. The card's H2 is the right experiment, but the academic caution is: SEEC measured EE acceleration (2.26 vs 5.73 m/s²), *not* OOD task success — so even if A1 shows the reaction is non-negligible at high arm-accel, it must still show that translates to the success metric, not just a cleaner force/accel number.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or solved).**
- *Core position*: HEX's implicit MoE almost certainly already captures the arm→base reaction — it is a "Unified *Proprioceptive Predictor*" whose entire job is to forecast cross-body proprioceptive dependencies, which *is* the coupling. Making it "explicit" is re-labeling a quantity the MoE already learns end-to-end, and end-to-end almost always beats a hand-specified intermediate target on the final metric.
- *Strongest evidence*: This is the card's own H1 falsifier ("explicit ≤ 61.8% → the MoE already captures it") and Risk #2 stated verbatim. [[2502.03206|Unified-General-Humanoid-Whole-Body]] (HUGWBC) shows you can train the reaction *away as task-agnostic noise* and still get versatile real-H1 manipulation — evidence the reaction may not need a dedicated term at all. [[2508.00355|TOP]] beats Exbody (95.30 vs 92.46) by *capping arm speed*, suppressing the reaction at the source — if the reaction can be engineered away, predicting it buys little.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The +3pp target is suspiciously small and on *one* humanoid — a 41.0→mid-60s OOD swing where the baseline OOD numbers (HEX 61.8, GR00T 41.0) already span 20pp across *architectures*. A +3pp explicit-vs-implicit delta at matched everything is inside the noise band of a single-platform, single-seed humanoid eval. Demand seeds and CIs before believing the wedge exists.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
- *Core position*: The arm→base-coupling narrative is cheap to *publish* and expensive to *deploy*, which is exactly why it's whitespace — industry labs (Amazon FAR/ResMimic, NVIDIA, the BIGAI/Beijing-Humanoid HEX group) chase data-recipe scale ([[2603.12263|Psi0]]'s "critical data recipe" beating a 10×-data baseline) because compute+data is their moat, leaving the low-compute architectural-ablation lane open to academics. A1 is a *solo-buildable* bet precisely because it reuses HEX's fixed data and backbone.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2603.12263|Psi0]] and HEX both frame the win as a *data recipe* (12M frames), and HEX's card concedes pretraining is second-order — meaning the *marginal* return to more data is falling, which is when architecture-ablation papers get cited. The benchmark substrate (HumanoidBench [[2403.10506]], HEX's own task suite) is not yet saturated, so there's reward headroom.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The incentive trap is benchmark *non-comparability* — every paper here (HEX, FALCON, SEEC, HiWET, ResMimic) reports on a *different* robot and task set, so "beats HEX on HEX's data" is only citable if HEX's data+backbone are actually reproducible. If HEX's 12M-frame dataset and RL controller aren't released, the *entire* H1 experiment is non-runnable and the bet collapses to a re-implementation gamble. De-risk by confirming HEX reproducibility *before* committing.

**5. HISTORIAN (seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the analytic-prior-vs-end-to-end pendulum replayed at the limb level — the same swing as model-based-RL vs Dreamer, and DR-as-noise vs structured-dynamics. Explicit intermediate targets (analytic term + learned residual) historically win in *low-data, high-precision, well-modeled* regimes and lose once data scales enough for end-to-end to absorb the structure.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card itself lays the lineage out: pure-analytic ([[2506.14278|Heavy-Limbs-WBC]] centroidal-momentum, [[2603.02443|Safe-WBLM]] QP) → analytic+residual ([[2504.06662|RAMBO]], [[2509.21231|SEEC]]) → implicit-learned ([[2604.07993|HEX]] MoE). A1 proposes to push *back* one notch toward explicit structure. Historically (Dreamer beating model-based, DR beating system-ID for robustness) the field's long arc has been *away* from hand-specified terms.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The papers that *survived* the pendulum swings were the ones whose structure was *cheap to add and falsifiable* (residual corrections, auxiliary losses) rather than load-bearing — RAMBO and SEEC survive as residuals *on top of* a working controller. A1's safest historical analogue is "auxiliary-loss target," not "new control architecture": if the explicit term is an *aux loss* on HEX's existing backbone (as the card states), it inherits the residual-pattern's survival odds; if it becomes a structural rewrite, it inherits the model-based lane's decline.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic+Practitioner on "is the coupling already captured?"* Skeptic: HEX's UPP *is* a proprioceptive predictor, so the reaction is already learned — explicit is relabeling. Academic/Practitioner: the UPP predicts *generic* cross-body proprioception, not the *directed* arm→base reaction, and SEEC's explicit "arms-are-light" assumption proves the field treats the two directions as separable. The clash is whether "explicit arm→base" is a *distinct* quantity from "implicit unified proprioception."
   - *Historian vs Card on swing direction*. Card: pushing toward explicit structure is the opening. Historian: the field's arc runs *away* from hand-specified terms once data scales — and HEX already has 12M frames, i.e. it may be past the data threshold where explicit structure stops helping.
   - *Practitioner vs H6 framing on the real risk*. Card H6: the risk is latency. Practitioner: the risk is the *biased analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$*, not latency — a sim-to-real coupling-accuracy problem H6 never measures.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.** Strongest: **Practitioner** — its objection (the analytic coupling term is a *new* sim-to-real liability, and RAMBO already found the base-acceleration channel is the weaker correction site) is grounded in a real cited result that directly contradicts the bet's chosen prediction surface, and it reframes the falsifier toward something measurable before any success claim. Weakest: **Historian** — illuminating on lineage but its "pendulum swings away from structure" is a heuristic, not evidence about *this* metric; the card's auxiliary-loss framing (not a rewrite) already hedges it.

3. **Resolving question.** Run HEX's exact UPP-removal ablation with the explicit $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}$ aux-loss term swapped in at matched parameter count and latency, on HEX's released data+backbone, with seeds and CIs, **stratified by arm-acceleration quartile** — does explicit beat implicit (>61.8% OOD) *specifically in the top quartile*, and is the measured top-quartile reaction magnitude above SEEC's "negligible" threshold? This single experiment resolves the Skeptic-vs-Academic clash (already-captured?) *and* the H2 premise (is SEEC wrong?) at once.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective accepts that **the arm→base inertial coupling is physically real and that HEX's UPP-removal ablation (11/12→6/12) shows coupling-representation is the load-bearing axis** — the disagreement is only over whether *making it explicit* adds anything over the implicit MoE. The first-principles premise ($M(q)$ non-block-diagonal) is unchallenged.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** No perspective addressed **bimanual / dual-arm and object-mediated reaction**: $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}=\hat M_{\text{base,arm}}\ddot q_{\text{arm}}$ is written for a single arm's joint acceleration, but real whole-body manipulation is *two arms + a held object* whose inertia ([[2602.11758|HAIC]]'s object-dynamics term, [[2603.08961|FAME]]'s asymmetric/bimanual loads) dominates the reaction. The bet's coupling term may be specified for the wrong (arm-only, unimanual) quantity.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** A1 is a genuinely unoccupied but *narrow* and *high-execution-risk* bet: the literature has every neighbor of "explicit arm→base reaction prediction" (anticipatory, emergent, analytic+residual, and SEEC's base→arm-explicit) but not that exact cell, and HEX's own ablation is strong evidence that coupling-representation is the right axis. The danger is not novelty but *whether explicit beats implicit at all* — HEX's MoE may already capture the reaction, the +3pp target sits inside single-platform noise, and the chosen prediction surface (base-acceleration via a hand-derived $M_{\text{base,arm}}$) is exactly the channel RAMBO found weaker and the term most exposed to sim-to-real inertia error. It is a defensible *ablation paper* (auxiliary-loss target on HEX's backbone), not a new-capability paper, and it is only runnable if HEX's data+backbone are reproducible.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   1. *The arm→base inertial coupling is physically real and coupling-representation is the load-bearing axis* (HEX UPP-removal 11/12→6/12). Supported by all five; challenged by none. **Most reliable.**
   2. *The exact "explicit learned arm→base reaction" cell is unoccupied* — alphaxiv + WebSearch return only neighbors (FALCON emergent, SEEC base→arm, ResMimic reactive-residual, Mobile-TeleVision motion-prior, 2511.19709 pure-MPC). Supported by Academic, Economist; the whitespace is real but narrow. **High.**
   3. *HEX's implicit MoE may already capture the reaction* — the bet's own H1 falsifier. Raised by Skeptic; conceded by Academic/Historian; only Practitioner offers a path (measure coupling-prediction error directly). **Medium-high — this is the live falsifier.**
   4. *The chosen prediction surface (base-acceleration, analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$) is the weaker/riskier channel* — RAMBO found joint-position corrections beat base-acceleration corrections; sim-to-real inertia error biases the feedforward. Raised by Practitioner; consistent with RAMBO/ResMimic. **Medium.**
   5. *The +3pp explicit-vs-implicit delta is inside single-platform noise* — needs seeds/CIs and cross-speed stratification, not a single average. Raised by Skeptic, reinforced by Economist (benchmark non-comparability). **Medium — measurement-design risk, not a refutation.**

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "wrong prediction surface" and the Skeptic's "already captured" are the *same* underlying point seen from two sides: the implicit MoE wins *precisely because* it never commits to a biased analytic $M_{\text{base,arm}}$ — it learns whatever cross-body mapping the data supports, sidestepping the inertia-model error the explicit term inherits. So the explicit term's *only* edge is interpretability + an auxiliary gradient, and its cost is a systematic feedforward bias; the bet is really "does a structured-but-biased aux target beat an unstructured-but-unbiased learned one on fixed data" — which is the historian's pendulum stated quantitatively.

4. **Actionable insight: DE-RISK, do not yet COMMIT.** The novelty survives but two cheap pre-experiments gate commitment.
   - *Next experiment (gating, ~1 week)*: confirm HEX's data + RL whole-body controller are actually released/reproducible. If not, the H1 head-to-head is non-runnable → DROP or re-target to a reproducible backbone (ResMimic's GMT or a HumanoidBench policy).
   - *Then (decisive)*: run H1+H2 as **one** experiment — explicit $\hat\delta_{\text{base}}$ aux-loss vs implicit MoE at matched params/latency, ≥3 seeds with CIs, OOD success **stratified by arm-acceleration quartile**, and *separately report the measured coupling-prediction sim-vs-real error*. Commit only if the top-quartile margin clears CIs AND the measured top-quartile reaction exceeds SEEC's negligible threshold. Pre-register the bimanual+held-object reaction (blind spot) as the v2 axis.

5. **Frontier question.** Does an *explicit, structured* coupling target ever beat an *implicit, end-to-end* one on the final task metric once the dataset crosses HEX's ~12M-frame scale — or is structured coupling-prediction a strictly *low-data* win that the data-recipe labs ([[2603.12263|Psi0]], HEX) erase by scaling? Whichever way that resolves decides not just A1 but the whole analytic-prior-vs-end-to-end pendulum for humanoid whole-body control.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Grounding: read A1 card (Whole-Body.md L111–202) plus the KH notes for HEX (2604.07993), SEEC (2509.21231), RAMBO (2504.06662), FALCON (2505.06776), HiWET (2602.06341), BRS (2503.05652). Verifications noted inline.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (each 1–10, with justification)

| # | Finding | Score | Justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | Arm→base coupling is physically real; coupling-representation is the load-bearing axis (HEX UPP-removal "11/12→6/12") | **6** | The *physics* (non-block-diagonal M(q)) is a textbook certainty, deserving a 10. But the finding's empirical teeth — the "Pouring 11/12→6/12" / "−5/12 largest drop vs +1/12 for 12M-frame pretraining" ablation — **does not appear anywhere in the HEX KH note** (2604.07993.md). The note confirms UPP/MoE exist and that pretraining helps; it carries zero per-component ablation breakdown. The single most-cited number in the briefing is currently unverifiable from the vault. A finding labelled "MOST RELIABLE" cannot rest on an unsourced figure. Score reflects rock-solid physics dragged down by an unverified quantitative anchor. |
| **2** | The exact "explicit learned arm→base reaction" cell is unoccupied | **7** | Whitespace search (alphaxiv + WebSearch) returning only neighbors is consistent with the 28-row table, which is genuinely dense and well-differentiated (RoboDuet's arm→base guidance *channel*, SEEC's reverse-direction explicit term, ResMimic's frozen-backbone residual, HAIC's predict-then-compensate on the *object*). The neighbors are real and the gap is plausibly real. Knocked down because (a) the gap is narrow — RoboDuet already learns an arm-*drives*-base signal, so "nobody predicts it" is a fine distinction between a guidance command and a dynamics term, and (b) absence-of-evidence from two search tools is not proof of vacancy. |
| **3** | HEX's implicit MoE may already capture the reaction (the live H1 falsifier) | **8** | This is the briefing's most honest and most important claim, and it is internally well-argued. It is correctly flagged as the bet's own falsifier, conceded across perspectives, and has a concrete measurement path. The reason it is not a 9: it is a *risk*, not a *result* — its reliability is high precisely because it is a hypothesis the briefing refuses to pre-resolve. The hidden-connection passage (implicit MoE wins by never committing to a biased analytic term) is a genuinely sharp re-framing and raises confidence here. |
| **4** | The chosen prediction surface (base-acceleration, analytic M_base,arm) is the weaker channel — RAMBO found joint-position corrections beat base-acceleration corrections | **7** | **The strongest-grounded of the critical findings.** RAMBO's KH note (2504.06662.md) states verbatim: "Providing corrective feedback at the joint position level, rather than solely at the base acceleration level, proved more effective." So the Practitioner's critique is directly sourced, not inferred. Held to 7 (not higher) only because the transfer is non-trivial: RAMBO is loco-manip *quadrupeds/biped EE-tracking*, not the humanoid arm→base direction, so "weaker channel here too" is an extrapolation across embodiment and coupling-direction. The briefing's own MEDIUM rating *under*-credits the evidence and *over*-credits the transfer — the score should sit on the evidence, which is solid. |
| **5** | The +3pp explicit-vs-implicit delta is inside single-platform noise; needs seeds/CIs/stratification | **8** | Methodologically unimpeachable and correctly classified as a measurement-design risk, not a refutation. A +3pp target on one humanoid with no reported seeds or CIs is, by any reviewer's standard, inside noise. The Economist's non-comparability reinforcement is legitimate (HEX/GR00T/π0.5 are not a controlled comparison). This is the kind of finding that costs nothing to accept and would embarrass the project if ignored. Near-certain. |

**Mean = 7.2. Lowest = 6 (Finding 1).**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 1's empirical anchor — the "HEX Pouring 11/12 → 6/12, −5/12 largest drop, pretraining +1/12 second-order" ablation.** This single result is the keystone: it appears in the Cluster Overview, Why-it-matters, First-principles, the H1 *control*, and Risk #2, and it is the entire basis for calling coupling-representation "the load-bearing axis." **It is not present in the HEX KH note.** Everything downstream — "coupling, not data, carries whole-body performance" — inherits this gap.

*To verify:* pull the HEX paper's ablation table directly (alphaxiv `get_paper_content` on 2604.07993 or the arxiv PDF) and confirm (a) the per-component success counts are reported on a 12-trial Pouring task, (b) UPP-removal is in fact the *largest single-component* drop, and (c) the 12M-frame pretraining ablation is quantitatively second-order. If the "11/12→6/12" framing is the briefing's paraphrase of a percentage (e.g., 91.7%→50%) rather than HEX's reported counts, that should be stated. If the figure cannot be located in the paper, Finding 1 must be down-rated from "MOST RELIABLE" and the whole "coupling is the lever" thesis softened to a hypothesis.

*Secondary weak link:* the verbatim attribution to SEEC that arm→base is "negligible because arms are dynamically light." SEEC's KH note confirms its base→arm direction and its analytic+residual recipe, but does **not** contain this assumption-statement. The briefing presents it inside quotation marks as SEEC's premise; it reads more like the reviewer's reconstruction of SEEC's implicit scope than a SEEC claim. Either source the quote or demote it to "SEEC's framing implies."

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Skeptic over-dominated, and the synthesis is the better for it — but it tips into self-defeating territory.** Findings 3 and 5 (both Skeptic-led) carry the highest confidence; the hidden-connection and frontier-question both restate the Skeptic's "already captured / data-scaling erases the prior" worry. The net effect is a briefing that argues *against its own bet* more persuasively than for it: the CEO summary leads with "high-execution-risk," the actionable insight is DE-RISK-don't-COMMIT, and the frontier question openly entertains that the whole structured-prior premise is a low-data artifact. That is intellectually honest, but a reader could fairly ask why A1 is the *lead* direction of Cluster A (per the Cluster Overview) if the synthesis's center of gravity is "this probably loses at scale."

The **Practitioner is the load-bearing positive contributor** — it supplied the one finding (RAMBO surface) that is both critical and well-sourced, and the only constructive measurement path for the Skeptic's H1. The **Academic and Historian are thin**: they mostly *concede* (Finding 3) and supply framing (the pendulum) rather than independent evidence. The **Economist** appears only as reinforcement on non-comparability. So: Skeptic-dominant, Practitioner-anchored, Academic/Historian/Economist under-weighted.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The Reproducibility/Systems engineer — and specifically, "is the gating experiment itself even runnable, and at what cost?"** The briefing's entire de-risk plan hinges on one unexamined assumption: *that HEX's 12M-frame dataset and RL whole-body controller are released and reproducible.* It mentions the fallback (ResMimic GMT / HumanoidBench) in one clause but never checks. This matters because:

- If HEX is **not** released (foundation-scale humanoid datasets from industrial labs frequently are not), the entire H1 head-to-head — the decisive experiment — is non-runnable on HEX, and the project collapses to "re-implement coupling on a *different* backbone," at which point the carefully-constructed argument that *HEX's* MoE is the implicit comparator evaporates. The whole novelty framing is HEX-specific.
- A 12M-frame pretraining + matched-param MoE-vs-explicit-term ablation, with ≥3 seeds, stratified by arm-acceleration quartile, is **not a 1-week experiment** — it is multiple weeks of GPU-time per seed on a foundation-scale backbone. The "~1 week gating" estimate is optimistic by a wide margin, and the cost of the *decisive* experiment is never budgeted.

This 6th angle would change the actionable insight from "DE-RISK in ~1 week" to "the binary release-availability check must come *first*, in days, and if HEX is closed the project is a different paper entirely — re-scope before any modelling." It converts a soft risk into a hard go/no-go gate.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, unusually self-critical briefing: the whitespace is real if narrow, the falsifier is named and not papered over, the hidden-connection between the Practitioner and Skeptic is a genuine insight, and the RAMBO surface critique is properly sourced. It earns its grade by being honest about being an ablation paper rather than a capability paper. It falls short of A-range for three concrete reasons.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**

1. **Source or down-rate the HEX "11/12→6/12" ablation.** It is the keystone of Finding 1 and absent from the vault note. Pull it from the paper; if it is not there as stated, the "coupling is the load-bearing axis" claim must drop from MOST-RELIABLE to a hypothesis-to-be-confirmed. This is non-negotiable for an honest briefing.
2. **Fix the SEEC "arms-are-light" attribution.** Either quote the source line or demote the quotation marks to a paraphrase. The bet's *raison d'être* ("the premise A1 overturns") rests on a premise the cited paper may not actually state.
3. **Make HEX-release a hard pre-gate and re-cost the experiments.** The "~1 week" estimate is unrealistic for a 12M-frame, 3-seed, quartile-stratified ablation; and the entire HEX-specific novelty framing is contingent on a dataset whose availability is never checked. Run the release check in days, before any modelling, and budget the decisive experiment in weeks, not days.
4. **Reconcile "A1 is the lead" with a synthesis that leans bearish.** If the frontier question's honest answer is "structured coupling is plausibly a low-data win that scaling erases," either defend why it still leads Cluster A or re-rank it as a high-risk option rather than the cluster's anchor.
