### A2 (Direction review)

Topic: the "pipeline-location head-to-head" bet, online convex-blend of frozen feasible primitives ([[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]) vs an upstream feasible prior ([[2604.00202|DreamControl-v2]]) vs a distilled-experts monolith ([[2506.12779|Experts-to-Generalist]] / [[2606.06493|HANDOFF]]), on one shared 8-task / 3-embodiment skill set with zero per-task reward tuning, with the prediction that feasibility-by-construction matches the monolith's outperformance while the monolith reward-hacks.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: SkillBlender's own named limitation is that it is state-based and never deployed on real hardware, so the whole "feasibility by convex hull" argument is currently a sim claim, while every monolith comparator the card cites (DreamControl-v2 8 real G1 skills, HANDOFF real G1, Experts-to-Generalist real G1, X-Loco real G1) is already on hardware. The bet is asking me to bet *against* the methods that have crossed sim2real in favor of the one that has not.
Strongest supporting evidence: the card itself concedes the gap (row 2506.09366 "state-based only, not deployed real (its named sim2real gap)"); the rescue plan is to "borrow the diffusion-prior anchors' hardware recipe" (2606.03536 Bionic, 125 real trials) — i.e. the blend has no native sim2real story, it must import one from the prior camp it claims to beat.
The one thing only this perspective says: the convex-hull feasibility guarantee is a *kinematic/state-space* guarantee that says nothing about actuator limits, contact-force feasibility, or the real-robot dynamics gap, so "feasible by construction" can still be infeasible on the actual G1, and the blend's smoothness/jerk advantage (the AGILE 2603.20147 metrics the card promises) is exactly what evaporates under unmodeled latency and motor saturation.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
Core position: the card is honest that the "feasible reference → better RL" half is "now near-consensus," so the only live novelty is the head-to-head, and the head-to-head as framed is an evaluation/benchmark contribution, not a new mechanism. SkillBlender, HANDOFF, Experts-to-Generalist, X-Loco (2603.03733), MoE-Loco (2503.08564), and Ball-Composing (2509.22442) already collectively establish every cell of the design space.
Strongest supporting evidence: the Related table is 32 rows deep and the mechanism is already triangulated, soft-routing vs hard-switching is settled by Ball-Composing's 98.3% vs 0.7%; jointly-trained-MoE-online-recombine is settled by MoE-Loco's 0.879; distill-into-one is settled by HOVER (11/12 real metrics) and X-Loco (94.8% of specialist SR). The convex-hull-preserves-feasibility claim is the contraction-theory/movement-primitive result (2501.09198) re-stated for humanoids.
The one thing only this perspective says: the bet's falsifier ("if a reward-tuned monolith matches the blend without hacking, composition buys nothing") is *already partially triggered* in the literature, X-Loco shows a distilled monolith reaching ~95% of specialist performance "with minimal performance loss," and HOVER beats specialists on 11/12 real metrics, so the premise that monoliths must reward-hack or destabilize is empirically shaky before the experiment is even run.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or solved).**
Core position: the whitespace is a *comparison no one bothered to run*, not a phenomenon no one understands, and there is a reason, the three arms optimize different objectives (blend = instantaneous feasibility, prior = reference quality for RL, distill = single-policy deployment), so a "fair" head-to-head requires arbitrary normalization choices that will determine the winner. The bet smuggles its conclusion into the setup.
Strongest supporting evidence: the card admits "no one has run online-blend against upstream-prior against the distilled monolith on the same skills" — that is an absence of a paper, not an absence of knowledge; and the H4 prediction quotes DreamControl-v2's own 68%/0.925 numbers as the target the blend should be lifted *toward*, conceding the prior already wins on the RL-reference axis.
The one thing only this perspective says: the "zero per-task reward tuning" constraint is the load-bearing trick — it is engineered to hand the win to the blend (which needs no per-task reward by construction) and penalize the monolith (which the card assumes needs tuning). But X-Loco and Experts-to-Generalist distill *without* per-task reward tuning too (they distill from experts under a single distillation loss), so the constraint does not actually separate the arms — the skeptic's charge is that the headline asymmetry is illusory.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
Core position: the entire whole-body field is benchmark-chasing on HumanoidBench / SkillBench / the new SIMPLE (2606.08278) and HumanoidArena (2606.17833) suites, and a "head-to-head benchmark paper" is the cheapest publishable unit in a saturating area, no new hardware, no new mechanism, just a controlled comparison table. That is why it looks like whitespace, it is low-status work the mechanism-paper labs skip.
Strongest supporting evidence: the proliferation of comparator papers in a single 4-month window (HANDOFF 2606, X-Loco 2603, ULTRA 2603, Embodiment-Aware GSD 2602, SIMPLE 2606) shows the money is in *generalist monoliths* (the deployable product), not in frozen libraries — vendor incentives (Unitree G1 everywhere) favor one-policy-ships-on-the-robot over a primitive library the customer must curate.
The one thing only this perspective says: whoever owns the *benchmark* the head-to-head runs on captures the citation flywheel, so the real strategic move is not to win the comparison but to define SkillBench-as-the-arena, and SIMPLE/HumanoidArena are already racing to be that arena — if the bet ships as "a comparison on SkillBlender's own SkillBench" it inherits SkillBlender's framing and cedes the benchmark-ownership upside.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: this is the modular-vs-end-to-end pendulum, options-and-skill-chaining (2111.07999 Adversarial Skill Chaining, 2009-era options framework, DeepMind's MOO/locomotion-primitives) swung to monolithic end-to-end RL, and is now swinging back to composition because monoliths got expensive — the same cycle pixel-vs-latent world models and DR-vs-system-ID went through.
Strongest supporting evidence: movement-primitives/DMP literature (2310.18771, 2505.10694) and Free-Gate free-energy policy composition (2412.06636) show "compose frozen primitives" is a 15-year recurring idea; each wave the composition camp wins on data-efficiency and feasibility, then the monolith camp wins back on asymptotic performance once compute scales (exactly the X-Loco/HOVER result).
The one thing only this perspective says: every prior composition wave lost not to a better monolith but to *scale* — when demos/compute became abundant the feasibility-by-construction advantage stopped mattering because the monolith could just learn feasibility. So the bet's durability is a bet that whole-body demo data stays scarce (Cluster D's "data wall"); if D2/D4 (EgoHumanoid, HumanoidMimicGen, GRAIL) break the data wall, the historical pattern says the monolith wins and composition becomes a footnote.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

(1) Direct clashes.
- PRACTITIONER vs the card's H4: practitioner says the blend has no real-robot evidence and must borrow Bionic's recipe; H4 still predicts the *upstream prior* (already real) beats the blend as an RL reference — so the card's own H4 sides with the practitioner against H1, the blend may lose the part that matters for deployment.
- ACADEMIC/SKEPTIC vs the bet's falsifier: the card says "if a reward-tuned monolith matches the blend without hacking, composition buys nothing"; academic supplies X-Loco (~95% of specialist, minimal loss) and HOVER (11/12 real metrics) as monoliths that already do roughly this. The bet treats the falsifier as open; the skeptic treats it as half-closed.
- ECONOMIST vs HISTORIAN on durability: economist says ship the benchmark now while the area is hot; historian says the result expires the moment the data wall falls, so a benchmark built on today's scarcity is a depreciating asset.

(2) Strongest vs weakest. Strongest: ACADEMIC — it is the only perspective that lands specific counter-evidence (X-Loco 94.8%, HOVER 11/12) directly on the bet's own falsifier, grounded in papers already in the card's table. Weakest: ECONOMIST — its benchmark-ownership point is strategically real but does not bear on whether the *scientific* claim is true or novel; it critiques the packaging, not the bet.

(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction: *When you hold the reward fixed and the skill set fixed, does the distilled monolith (X-Loco/HANDOFF-style) actually reward-hack or destabilize over the 8 s / no-reset long horizon, or does it match the blend?* This is the H1 falsifier and it splits academic (says monolith already matches) from the card (says it hacks). Everything downstream hinges on it.

(4) Universal agreement (likely true): all five accept that *confining the action to a feasible set helps* and that feasibility-preserving composition reduces per-task reward engineering, the disagreement is only about *whether composition beats a feasibility-respecting monolith* and *whether the win survives sim2real and data-scaling*. The mechanism (feasible substrate good) is consensus; the comparative ranking is not.

(5) The blind spot none addressed: the *cost and compositionality of building the frozen primitive library itself across embodiments* — the card's own Risk #2 ("primitive library design is itself engineering") is exactly the thing H5 defers and no perspective resolves, and critically, whether primitives trained on H1 even *transfer* as feasible primitives to G1/H1-2 (HumanoidArena 2606.17833 shows cross-GMT transfer drops 39.9% with 0.0% retention on leg-critical tasks). If primitives are per-embodiment, the convex-hull guarantee is per-embodiment too, and the "3-embodiment shared skill set" premise collapses.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

(1) CEO summary. A2 bets on running a controlled, single-reward head-to-head that no one has published, online convex-blend of frozen primitives vs an upstream feasible prior vs a distilled-experts monolith, predicting feasibility-by-construction matches the monolith without reward-hacking. The mechanism it rests on (feasible substrate reduces reward engineering) is genuinely near-consensus and well-evidenced, and the specific three-way comparison genuinely has not been run, so the whitespace is real but narrow, it is an evaluation/benchmark contribution, not a new mechanism. The central danger is that the bet's own falsifier is already half-triggered in the literature (X-Loco's distilled monolith reaches ~95% of specialist performance, HOVER beats specialists on 11/12 real metrics), the headline "zero per-task reward tuning" asymmetry does not cleanly separate the arms (distillation also avoids per-task reward tuning), and the blend is the one arm with no real-robot evidence. This is a credible, low-cost, defensible paper if framed as "where in the pipeline should feasibility live, and does it survive sim2real and the long horizon," and a weak one if framed as "composition beats the monolith."

(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.
- F1 (most reliable). A feasible substrate reduces per-task reward engineering and reward-hacking. Supported by SkillBlender's softmax-removal ablation (feasibility collapses), DreamControl-v2 (68% vs 8% valid trajectories), Ball-Composing (98.3% soft-route vs 0.7% switch). Supported by all five; challenged by none. This is the safe core.
- F2. The three-way head-to-head with a shared reward has genuinely not been published. Supported by the card and confirmed by alphaxiv (closest papers X-Loco, HANDOFF, ULTRA, SIMPLE all pick *one* mechanism and ship it; none run the controlled comparison). Challenged by SKEPTIC (absence of paper != absence of knowledge) and ECONOMIST (it is low-status missing-table work).
- F3. The monolith does NOT obviously reward-hack or destabilize, so the bet's headline prediction is at risk. Supported by ACADEMIC via X-Loco (94.8% of specialist, "minimal performance loss") and HOVER (11/12 real metrics). Challenged by the card's H1 and by Adaptive-Humanoid-Control (2511.06371), which shows a distilled monolith *needs* PCGrad + per-behavior critics to avoid collapse (evidence the cost is real). Genuinely contested, hence the resolving question.
- F4. The blend has no native sim2real story and must import one. Supported by PRACTITIONER and the card's own Risk #3 (SkillBlender state-based, borrows Bionic's recipe). Challenged by no one, it is conceded.
- F5 (least settled). Durability depends on whole-body demo data staying scarce. Supported by HISTORIAN (every prior composition wave lost to scale) and by HumanoidArena's cross-embodiment transfer collapse (primitives may not be shared). Challenged implicitly by Cluster D's data-synthesis push (HumanoidMimicGen, GRAIL) which is *designed* to break that scarcity.

(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection. The PRACTITIONER's "no real-robot evidence" and the HISTORIAN's "composition loses to scale" are the same fact from two angles, both say the blend's advantage is *substrate-relative*, it only matters while real data/compute are scarce. And the ACADEMIC's X-Loco counter-evidence is the *mechanism* by which that scarcity gets defeated, distillation lets a monolith absorb the specialists' feasibility once you can afford to train the specialists. So three perspectives independently converge on one threat, the bet wins only in the regime where you can build feasible primitives but cannot scale a monolith, and that regime is exactly what Cluster D is trying to close.

(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to a reframed version. Do not commit to "composition beats the monolith" (F3 says you will likely lose). Commit to "where in the pipeline should feasibility live, and what survives sim2real + the long horizon" — an evaluation contribution where every outcome is publishable. Concrete next experiment: run the minimal H1 falsifier first and cheaply — on SkillBlender's own SkillBench, with one shared reward, pit the frozen-blend against a *distilled* monolith built X-Loco/Experts-to-Generalist style (not a from-scratch RL monolith, which is a strawman), and measure two things the field has not isolated: (a) feasibility/jerk-limit (AGILE 2603.20147 diagnostics) over the *8 s and no-reset long horizon* (LHM-Humanoid 2508.16943's 350-scene continuous suite), where the card predicts the monolith destabilizes, and (b) cross-embodiment primitive transfer (H1-trained primitives → G1, against HumanoidArena's 39.9%-drop baseline). If the distilled monolith stays stable and feasible (the X-Loco-shaped outcome), pivot the headline to "the upstream prior is the right place for feasibility" (H4), which the literature already favors and which is the half with a real-robot story. Kill criterion: if a distilled monolith under one reward matches the blend on feasibility over the long horizon *and* transfers across embodiments, drop the composition framing entirely.

(5) Frontier question that would change everything: *Is feasibility a transferable, composable property across embodiments, or is it irreducibly per-body?* If a primitive library trained on one humanoid yields feasible blends on another (defeating HumanoidArena's transfer collapse), composition becomes a genuine cross-embodiment asset and the bet is strong; if feasibility is per-body, the convex-hull guarantee is per-body, the "3-embodiment shared skill set" premise dissolves, and the distilled monolith (which re-learns feasibility per body anyway) wins by default.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. I read the A2 card (Whole-Body.md L203-298) and spot-checked the load-bearing numbers against the KnowledgeHub. The briefing is unusually honest about its own narrowness, which I credit. But the honesty is doing work that the evidence does not fully back, and one of the five findings rests on a number that is not in this vault at all.

---

##### 1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability, with justification)

- **F1 — feasible substrate reduces per-task reward engineering / reward-hacking: 9/10.**
  Triple-verified in KH. SkillBlender's KH note states outright "applying softmax to skill weights is crucial for preventing reward hacking" and the ablation "confirmed the critical role of the softmax layer"; DreamControl-v2 KH confirms 68% vs 8% valid-trajectory and 0.925 vs 0.101 RL; Ball-Composing KH confirms 98.3% catch. Supported by all five perspectives, challenged by none. Docked one point only because the three exhibits prove three *different* things (during-control blend, upstream prior, soft-routing) and the briefing fuses them into one near-consensus claim — the consensus is real but is an OR over mechanisms, not a single replicated result.

- **F2 — the three-way shared-reward head-to-head has not been published: 7/10.**
  Plausible and probably true, but the verification is the weak kind: "I searched and found papers that each ship one mechanism." That is evidence of a missing *table*, not proof the comparison is unrun or unknown — the SKEPTIC's "absence of paper != absence of knowledge" caveat is correctly flagged and correctly not resolved. Also note the search anchors (X-Loco, ULTRA, SIMPLE) are not all in the card: ULTRA 2603.03279 is an A1 paper, X-Loco and SIMPLE 2606.08278 are in neither KH nor the A2 table. The novelty claim is "no one drew this table," which is a true-but-low-ceiling kind of true.

- **F3 — the monolith does NOT obviously reward-hack/destabilize, so the headline prediction is at risk: 5/10.**
  This is the most consequential finding and the least verifiable as stated. The HOVER "11/12 real metrics" is confirmed in KH — but the same KH note says HOVER wins only "at least 7 of 12" in *simulation*, which the briefing omits; that omission cuts the wrong way for F3 (it makes the monolith look more dominant than the sim evidence supports, and the whole experiment is sim-first). More seriously, the X-Loco "94.8% / ~95% of specialist" number — the single most load-bearing falsifier evidence — is **not in the KnowledgeHub and not in the A2 card**. It exists only in the sibling analyst file. I cannot verify 94.8% against any primary source in this vault. The *direction* of F3 (the falsifier is at least half-triggered) is defensible from HOVER alone and from Adaptive-Humanoid-Control's counter-evidence; the *specific number* anchoring it is unsourced here.

- **F4 — the blend has no native sim2real story and must import one: 8/10.**
  Well-grounded. SkillBlender's KH note and the card's own Risk #3 both name "state-based observation, no real deployment" as the limitation; the card concedes it borrows Bionic 2606.03536's 125-trial recipe. Conceded by all perspectives, which is exactly why it is reliable — nobody is incentivized to overstate it. The only reason it is not 9 is that "import a recipe" understates the risk: a state-based blend that never saw a real observation may not survive the import, and the briefing treats borrowing the recipe as nearly free.

- **F5 — durability depends on whole-body demo data staying scarce: 6/10.**
  This is a forecast, not a finding, and the briefing labels it least-settled honestly. The HISTORIAN's "every composition wave lost to scale" is a pattern-match (DMP/movement-primitive lineage cited in the analyst file), not a measurement on whole-body humanoids specifically, and it is explicitly contradicted by Cluster D's own data-synthesis push (HumanoidMimicGen, GRAIL). HumanoidArena's 39.9% transfer drop is real and in the card, but it measures *cross-tracker* transfer collapse, not the data-scarcity timeline. The claim is reasonable but inherently low-confidence because it is a bet about the future regime, not the present literature.

Mean = (9 + 7 + 5 + 8 + 6) / 5 = **7.0**. Lowest = **5** (F3).

---

##### 2. WEAKEST LINK

**F3, specifically the X-Loco "distilled monolith reaches ~94.8% / ~95% of specialist performance" claim.** It is the keystone that flips the whole recommendation from "commit to composition" to "de-risk and reframe," yet it is the one number with no primary source in this vault: X-Loco (2603.03733) is absent from the KnowledgeHub and absent from the A2 Related-table. The briefing inherited 94.8% from the analyst pass and never re-grounded it.

What would verify it: ingest X-Loco 2603.03733 into the KnowledgeHub (or pull the alphaxiv paper content) and confirm three things the briefing asserts — (a) the 94.8%/~95% figure is specialist-relative SR, not a cherry-picked subset; (b) X-Loco's monolith is distilled under a *single* loss with *zero per-task reward tuning* (the briefing's claim that distillation "also avoids per-task reward tuning," which is what makes the headline asymmetry illusory); and (c) it was evaluated over a long / no-reset horizon comparable to the card's 8 s and LHM-Humanoid criteria, since the card's prediction is specifically about long-horizon destabilization, not single-episode SR. Until (b) and (c) are checked, F3 could be either a fatal falsifier or a non-comparable point estimate, and the entire "reframe away from composition" recommendation hinges on which.

---

##### 3. BIAS CHECK — dominant perspective

**The ACADEMIC over-dominated.** By the synthesis's own accounting (analyst file: "Strongest: ACADEMIC — the only perspective that lands specific counter-evidence directly on the bet's own falsifier"), the ACADEMIC supplies the two exhibits (X-Loco, HOVER) that drive F3, the hidden cross-perspective connection (#3, "distillation is the mechanism by which scarcity gets defeated"), and the kill criterion (#4). The actionable insight is essentially "the ACADEMIC is right, so reframe." The PRACTITIONER and HISTORIAN are then folded *into* the ACADEMIC's thesis ("the same fact from two angles"), which reads less like independent convergence and more like the ACADEMIC's frame absorbing the other two. The SKEPTIC and ECONOMIST are cited mainly to be set aside (F2's caveats are noted but not allowed to move the conclusion; the ECONOMIST is explicitly graded "weakest, critiques the packaging not the bet"). Net: a five-perspective process produced a one-perspective conclusion. That is fine *if* the ACADEMIC's evidence is solid — but per the Weakest Link, its keystone number is unverified, so the dominance is risky rather than earned.

---

##### 4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE (6th angle that would change conclusions)

**A control-theorist / dynamicist.** Every perspective here argues about *empirics* (who ships, who scales, who has real-robot data) and takes the card's first-principle — "a softmax-weighted blend of feasible primitives stays in the feasible convex hull, so feasibility is free" — at face value. A dynamicist would attack the premise directly: feasibility under contact and underactuation is **not** a convex property. A convex combination of two individually-balance-feasible joint-target trajectories is not guaranteed to be balance-feasible, because the map from joint targets to the constraint that matters (ZMP/centroidal-momentum/contact-force feasibility) is nonlinear, and the blend can land outside both primitives' regions of attraction at exactly the instants where arm and leg "disagree" (the very reach-while-stepping case H2 leans on). SkillBlender's softmax ablation shows the blend *empirically* preserves a feasibility *metric* — it does not prove a hull *guarantee*, and the card repeatedly upgrades the former into the latter ("for free," "convex-hull guarantee"). If the convexity argument is only empirically true and only in SkillBlender's state-based sim, then the whole "feasibility by construction" thesis is on shakier ground than any of the five perspectives admit, and the right experiment is not the three-way SR bake-off but a measurement of *how far* the blend departs from each primitive's certified feasible set as a function of weight disagreement. This angle would push the contribution from "evaluation benchmark" toward "characterize when convex blending actually preserves dynamic feasibility," which is a stronger and more honest paper.

---

##### 5. OVERALL GRADE + TOP FIXES

**Grade: B / B-minus.**

This is a competent, self-aware strategy memo. Its great virtue is calibration: it does not oversell, it pre-registers its own falsifier, it names the reframe that survives a loss, and four of its five findings are grounded in numbers I could verify in the vault. That is well above the median direction memo. It does not reach the A band for three reasons: (1) the keystone counter-evidence is unsourced in this vault; (2) the headline novelty is an evaluation/missing-table contribution dressed in mechanism language, and the memo knows this but still leads with the mechanism framing in the thesis; (3) it accepts the card's convexity-implies-feasibility premise without a dynamicist's scrutiny, which is the one assumption that, if wrong, voids the entire bet.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Ground or retract the 94.8% X-Loco number.** Ingest 2603.03733, confirm it is specialist-relative SR under a single distillation loss over a long horizon, and add it to the A2 table. If it cannot be confirmed long-horizon, F3 reverts to "open," and the recommendation should soften from "you will likely lose" to "untested."
2. **Resolve the headline asymmetry honestly.** The briefing already spots that distillation also avoids per-task reward tuning, which means "zero per-task reward tuning" does not separate blend from distilled monolith. The fix is to redefine the discriminating axis: it is *online recombination without retraining* and *per-instant feasibility*, not reward tuning. Rewrite H1 around that, or the experiment measures the wrong thing.
3. **Add the dynamicist falsifier as the first experiment.** Before the three-way SR bake-off, measure whether the convex blend actually stays inside each primitive's certified feasible set under weight disagreement (ZMP/contact-force margin vs blend weights). If it does not, "feasibility by construction" is false and every downstream claim collapses — cheaper to learn that first.
4. **Drop the sim-only HOVER asymmetry.** Report HOVER's "7/12 in sim" alongside "11/12 real," since the proposed experiment is sim-first; otherwise F3 quietly inflates the monolith's apparent dominance in exactly the setting the study will run in.

---

### A3 (Direction review)

Topic: A3 bets that on a *bipedal humanoid with a walking gait*, a world-frame Commander/Tracker hierarchy with a *learned continuous α-schedule* holds HiWET's 12.4 mm sim / 12-15 mm real error over long horizons, where (H1) a matched body-centric controller's error grows monotonically with locomotion distance while the world-frame one stays flat, and (H2) the learned α reaches more out-of-static-range targets than HiWET's tuned-constant α or a discrete go/no-go trigger. The whitespace is narrowly the *frame-as-lever isolation on a bipedal gait* plus the *learned continuous schedule*, not world-frame closure itself (which HiWET, HERO, CLOT already do on humanoids).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet's load-bearing falsifier (H1, error-vs-base-travel-distance) is a measurement artifact of base-state estimation, not of control frame. On real hardware, "world-frame" is only as good as your odometry; HiWET's own ablation already says removing the state estimator costs ~10 mm and A3's own H5/Risk concede drift just moves into the estimator. You will spend the project fighting VIO/leg-odometry drift, not proving a frame principle.
- *Strongest evidence*: HERO (2602.16705) had to *learn residual neural FK + residual odometry* to drop translation error 4.67 cm → 2.56 cm on a real humanoid; that is the practitioner tax the frame story hides. CLOT (2602.15060) only stays "drift-free for 30 min" by fusing OptiTrack (external mocap), i.e. it imports ground-truth global pose the field cannot deploy outside a lab. HiWET's 12-15 mm real is on a G1 in a controlled setup.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Pre-register the estimator. If your H1 "body-centric grows with distance" curve is run with a clean simulator base pose, it proves nothing about the field's real problem; if run with onboard estimation, the world-frame branch will *also* drift and your headline flattens. Budget the project around base-state estimation as the actual deliverable.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: World-frame EE tracking on a walking *bipedal humanoid* is no longer whitespace; it is 2026 consensus, and the card's own table admits it. The genuinely novel residue is two narrow deltas on the anchor: a *learned* α (vs HiWET's tuned constant) and the error-vs-distance *plot* nobody bothered to run. That is a solid workshop/ablation contribution, not a thesis-level frame discovery.
- *Strongest evidence*: HiWET (2602.06341) already is world-frame + base-as-scheduled-transport-via-tunable-α + KMP on a bipedal G1. HERO (2602.16705) closes the world-frame loop on a bipedal humanoid (2.44 cm, 5.5× prior). CLOT (2602.15060) attacks accumulated *global* drift directly on a full-size biped. CEER (2605.19981, Duke, surfaced via alphaxiv, NOT in card) controls *root + EE jointly* in task space (3.3 cm EE RMSE) with the root commanded as a transport DoF. The "frame distinction proven only on quadrupeds" framing is already false at card-write time.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Reframe the contribution honestly as "is HiWET's α better learned than tuned, and does the frame advantage survive a controlled error-vs-distance test" — and make H2 (learned-α workspace gain) the headline, because H1 risks being a tautology (world-frame closure is *defined* to be distance-invariant given perfect base pose).

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or solved).**
- *Core position*: H1 is close to unfalsifiable-in-your-favor and therefore worthless as a bet: a world-frame controller is *by construction* parameterized in world coordinates, so of course its error doesn't grow with distance given the base pose it consumes; the "discovery" is a definitional restatement. Meanwhile the actually-interesting claim (learned α beats tuned α) may already be a tie.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own falsifier for H2 — "scheduled α ties fixed α → the schedule adds nothing" — is the likely outcome: HiWET reports strong results with a *constant* α, and HANDOFF (2606.06493) expands robust workspace to 0.31 m³ at 97.7% feasibility *without any α at all*, just a 10-D command interface, beating FALCON (0.06) and SONIC (0.26). If a body-centric pelvis-frame interface already extends workspace this much, the "frame is the lever" premise is undercut. Hold My Beer/SoFTA (2505.24198) gets 50-80% EE-acceleration reduction during fast walking *in a robot-centric frame* — the foil the card itself flags for H1/H6.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Run H2 (learned vs tuned-constant vs HANDOFF-style interface) FIRST as a kill-switch. If learned α ties tuned α on workspace coverage, the whole direction collapses to a re-plot of HiWET, and you should drop it before investing in hardware.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction is cheap to *publish* and expensive to *win*. The incentive gradient pushes toward the safe, citation-friendly version (re-run HiWET with a learned α, add a clean plot, cite 21 systems) — exactly the kind of incremental "isolate-the-frame" paper that accrues citations off a hot anchor (HiWET, HERO, CEER all 2026) without de-risking the hard part (real-robot odometry).
- *Strongest evidence*: The cluster is anchored on platform-locked G1 results (HiWET, HERO, CEER, HANDOFF, SEEC all Unitree G1 / Booster T1), which is the vendor-subsidized benchmark substrate that makes "another G1 loco-manip controller" trivially fundable but crowded. 21 cited systems in <18 months signals a saturating sub-field where marginal novelty per paper is shrinking.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The defensible economic moat is NOT the controller — it is the *protocol* (a standardized error-vs-locomotion-distance benchmark + a cross-embodiment KMP-transfer test, H3). Own the measurement, not the method; AGILE (2603.20147) shows the field rewards deployment/diagnostic workflows, and a benchmark outlives a controller.

**5. HISTORIAN (seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the world-frame-vs-body-frame swing recapitulating the mobile-manipulation "decouple-then-recouple" cycle and the SLAM-era "the map is only as good as the loop closure" lesson. Every generation rediscovers that moving the base to extend reach is free in simulation and brutal on a real estimator; the pendulum (modular task-space interface ↔ end-to-end joint policy) has swung many times.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own lineage shows the swing: BRS (2503.05652) mitigates drift by *factoring* (autoregressive base→torso→arm) without closing the world loop; HiWET/HERO/CLOT close it; CEER/HANDOFF abstract back up to a task-space interface. This is the classic modular↔monolithic oscillation (cf. end-to-end vs modular nav). UMI-on-Legs (2407.10353) making the *task frame* the interface is the quadruped precedent of the same idea that the card credits.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The papers that survived prior waves were the ones that nailed the *estimator/loop-closure* substrate (ORB-SLAM survived because of loop closure, not the front-end). Bet on the H5 base-state-estimation result as the durable contribution; the α-schedule is this cycle's fashion and will be subsumed by the next unified-latent interface (CEER, WholeBodyVLA 2512.11047).

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Card (on H1)*: Card treats "body-centric error grows with distance, world-frame stays flat" as a discovery; Skeptic calls it a definitional tautology (world-frame controllers consume world pose by construction). Specific claim in tension: whether a *matched-capacity* body-centric baseline can be made distance-flat (card's own falsifier admits if it stays flat, "closure buys nothing").
   - *Practitioner vs Academic (on what's hard)*: Practitioner says the deliverable is base-state estimation (H5); Academic says the deliverable is the learned-α delta (H2). They disagree on which sub-hypothesis is load-bearing vs incidental.
   - *Skeptic vs Card (on H2 novelty)*: Skeptic cites HANDOFF's 0.31 m³ pelvis-frame (body-centric) workspace expansion as evidence the frame is *not* the lever for reachability; the card asserts the learned continuous α is what extends reach. Direct metric clash: HANDOFF extends workspace with no α and no world-frame.
   - *Economist vs Academic (on contribution type)*: Economist says publish the protocol/benchmark; Academic says the controller delta is the contribution. Clash on what the unit of novelty should be.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: SKEPTIC. It is the only one that names a concrete kill-experiment (H2 first, learned-vs-constant-vs-HANDOFF) and grounds the "already solved / tautology" charge in real competing metrics (HANDOFF 0.31 m³, HiWET constant-α success, SoFTA 50-80%). It directly threatens the bet rather than the periphery.
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN. Insightful on the modular↔monolithic pendulum but its prescription (bet on the estimator) converges with Practitioner without adding an independent test; its analogies (SLAM loop closure) are evocative but don't pin a falsifiable prediction the card doesn't already have via H5.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   - *"On a single bipedal humanoid with onboard (not simulator-ground-truth) base-state estimation, does a learned continuous α-schedule produce a statistically separable reduction in EE error-vs-locomotion-distance slope AND in out-of-static-reach failure rate, versus (a) HiWET's tuned-constant α and (b) HANDOFF's α-free pelvis-frame interface — when all three share policy capacity and estimator?"* This collapses the H1-tautology dispute (forces onboard estimation), the H2-tie dispute (head-to-head with the constant-α and the α-free competitor), and the Practitioner/Academic split (estimator and α tested in the same run).

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   - World-frame closure on a *real* humanoid is bottlenecked by base-state estimation, and that bottleneck is first-class, not a footnote. Practitioner (odometry tax), Academic (HERO needed residual odometry), Skeptic (H1 is about base pose), Economist (the protocol must control for it), and Historian (loop-closure lesson) all land here. The card's own H5/Risk already says it. This is the most reliable claim in the whole review.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by no perspective, including the card).**
   - *Dynamic/contact loads and timing*, not just kinematic precision: every metric in play (12.4 mm, 2.44 cm, 0.31 m³) is free-space EE pose tracking. None of the perspectives or hypotheses addresses what happens to the world-frame closure when the EE is *under external load / in contact* (the wrench propagates to the support polygon and corrupts both the estimator and the α trade-off), nor *control-rate/latency* of the learned α-scheduler on real hardware. CEER's whole point was compliance under contact; A3 isolates the frame in free space and never tests it under the loads that define manipulation. That is the unexamined axis.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary (nuanced).** A3 is a *sound but narrow incremental bet sitting on top of a single hot anchor (HiWET)*. World-frame end-effector tracking on a walking bipedal humanoid is no longer whitespace — HiWET, HERO, and CLOT all do it on real bipeds in 2026, and CEER/HANDOFF already abstract the base into a commanded task-space DoF. The residual novelty is genuinely two narrow deltas: replacing HiWET's *tuned-constant* α with a *learned continuous* schedule (H2), and running the *error-vs-locomotion-distance* curve nobody plotted (H1). H1 risks being a near-definitional restatement of "world-frame controllers consume world pose," and H2 risks tying a tuned constant. The durable, defensible value is not the controller but the *measurement substrate* — a controlled error-vs-distance + cross-embodiment-KMP-transfer protocol run with onboard (not mocap/simulator-ground-truth) base-state estimation. Commit only if you reframe around that protocol and the learned-α kill-switch passes; otherwise this is a re-plot of HiWET.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   1. *World-frame closure on real bipedal humanoids is already done; A3's whitespace is the learned-α + error-vs-distance delta only.* — Supported by Academic, Skeptic, Historian (HiWET 2602.06341, HERO 2602.16705, CLOT 2602.15060, CEER 2605.19981). Challenged by: the card's framing ("proven mostly on quadrupeds"). Highest reliability — multiple real-biped anchors.
   2. *On real hardware the binding constraint is base-state estimation, not control frame.* — Supported by ALL five perspectives + the card's own H5/Risk (HERO residual odometry 4.67→2.56 cm; CLOT needs OptiTrack). Challenged by: none. Very high reliability.
   3. *H2 (learned α beats tuned-constant α on reachable workspace) may already be a tie or pre-empted.* — Supported by Skeptic, Economist (HANDOFF 0.31 m³ α-free; HiWET strong with constant α). Challenged by: card's H2 prediction. Medium-high reliability; this is the load-bearing empirical risk.
   4. *H1's "error grows with distance for body-centric / flat for world-frame" is partly tautological and depends entirely on whether the estimator is onboard or ground-truth.* — Supported by Skeptic, Practitioner. Challenged by: card (treats it as the headline falsifier). Medium reliability — depends on experimental setup the card hasn't pinned.
   5. *The contribution is more defensible as a benchmark/protocol than as a controller, given a saturating G1-locked sub-field.* — Supported by Economist, Historian. Challenged by: Academic (controller delta is real). Medium reliability — a strategy claim, not an empirical one.

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner (odometry tax), Skeptic (H1 is about base pose), and Historian (SLAM loop-closure survived, not front-ends) are independently saying the *same* thing: A3's three differentiators (world-frame, learned α, error-vs-distance) all secretly reduce to one quantity — *base-state-estimation quality*. The frame only matters because it relocates error into the estimator; the α only helps if the estimator survives the extra base motion it commands; the distance-curve only separates the frames if the estimator is real. The bet is, underneath, an estimation bet wearing a control-frame costume — which is exactly H5, currently ranked 5th of 6.

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not yet COMMIT.** The bet is alive but thin and mis-ordered. Concretely:
   - *Promote H5 and H2 to the front; demote H1 from headline to control.* Run the kill-switch experiment FIRST (the Prompt-2 Q3 design): one G1, onboard base-state estimation, three matched-capacity controllers — learned-continuous-α world-frame, HiWET tuned-constant-α world-frame, and a HANDOFF-style α-free pelvis-frame interface — compared on (i) EE-error-vs-base-travel-distance *slope* and (ii) out-of-static-reach failure rate.
   - *Gate*: if learned α does not separate from tuned-constant α on reachable workspace, DROP (it's a HiWET re-plot). If it separates only with simulator-ground-truth base pose but ties under onboard estimation, the real contribution is the estimator (pivot to H5 as the paper).
   - *Reframe the deliverable* as a *standardized error-vs-locomotion-distance + cross-embodiment-KMP-transfer benchmark* (Economist/Historian moat), so the paper survives even if the α delta is marginal.
   - *Add the missing axis*: include at least one *under-load / in-contact* condition so the frame claim isn't only a free-space result (closes the Prompt-2 blind spot and differentiates from CEER on its own turf).

5. **Frontier question that would change everything.** *Is "world-frame vs body-centric" even the right axis once a learned unified-latent interface (CEER 2605.19981, WholeBodyVLA 2512.11047) lets the policy choose its own internal reference frame end-to-end — i.e., does the optimal frame for long-horizon precision emerge from data rather than being imposed, making the entire frame-as-lever framing a transitional artifact?* If yes, A3's hand-designed Commander/Tracker world-frame hierarchy is this cycle's soon-subsumed fashion, and the lasting contribution can only be the measurement protocol, not the architecture.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Independent reviewer: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. The card under review is A3 (Whole-Body.md L299-382). The briefing is a five-perspective synthesis (Academic, Skeptic, Historian, Economist, Practitioner). My job is to find the soft claims and over-reach, not rubber-stamp.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (each 1-10 for reliability)

**Finding 1 — "World-frame closure on real bipedal humanoids is already done; A3's whitespace is the learned-α + error-vs-distance delta only." Score: 8/10.**
Strongly grounded in the card's own table: HiWET (2602.06341), HERO (2602.16705, 2.44 cm real humanoid), CLOT (2602.15060, drift-free 30 min on a full-size biped), and HALOMI (2606.18772, humanoid world-frame from-demo) are all bipedal-humanoid world-frame closures. The card's repeated "mostly proven on quadrupeds" framing (L310, L320, L348) is genuinely *stale relative to its own anchor list* — it foregrounds UMI-on-Legs / WB-EE / Omni WBLM / Multi-Critic (all quadruped) while three to four bipedal humanoid world-frame papers already sit in the table. The finding is right that the whitespace has shrunk to the two deltas. I dock 2 points only because "done" is overstated: HERO's 2.44 cm and CLOT's reliance on OptiTrack are not the same regime as HiWET's 12 mm onboard, so "world-frame closure on bipeds" is demonstrated but not *solved* at the precision A3 targets.

**Finding 2 — "On real hardware the binding constraint is base-state estimation, not control frame." Score: 8/10.**
This is the most defensible technical claim in the briefing and it is corroborated by the card itself, not just the reviewers. HiWET's own ablation (removing the state estimator costs ~10 mm, L368/L379), HERO's residual-odometry numbers, and CLOT's OptiTrack dependence all converge. The first-principles argument is sound: in world-frame closure EE error is the composition of tracker error and base-pose error, and the latter is unbounded over distance without good odometry. I dock 2 points because "binding" is asserted, not measured at A3's target precision — it is plausible the tracker, contact, or actuation bandwidth co-binds, and no one has decomposed the error budget at 12 mm. H5 is exactly the experiment that would confirm it, which is the point of the briefing.

**Finding 3 — "H2 (learned α beats tuned-constant α) may already tie or be pre-empted." Score: 7/10.**
This is the load-bearing empirical risk and the briefing is right to flag it. HANDOFF (2606.06493) reaches a robust 0.31 m³ workspace at 97.7% feasibility *with no α at all* — a pelvis-frame command interface — beating FALCON and SONIC. QuadWBG's GORM (+54% volume) and the differentiable reachability map (2508.11275) both extend reachable workspace without a *learned continuous schedule*. So the burden on H2 is to show the *schedule* (continuity + task-conditioning), not merely base-as-reach-extender, is the lever. I hold this at 7 rather than higher because the briefing slightly conflates two things HANDOFF does NOT settle: HANDOFF expands a *static-stance* reachable workspace via a command interface, whereas H2's claim is about reach-extension *during locomotion with a balance trade*. They may not be the same axis — so "pre-empted" is the right risk to log but is not yet a clean tie. The verification is one experiment away.

**Finding 4 — "H1's grows-with-distance claim is partly tautological and depends entirely on onboard-vs-ground-truth estimation." Score: 7/10.**
Correct and well-argued. "A world-frame controller consumes world pose; a robot-centric one integrates base motion into the EE estimate and therefore accumulates" is close to definitional *if* the body-centric baseline is given perfect proprioception and no global correction. The empirical content of H1 lives entirely in *how the base pose is obtained*: under simulator ground-truth base pose the curve is near-trivial; under onboard estimation it is a real and interesting measurement. The card half-sees this (H5 is listed) but ranks H1 as the headline falsifier (L311, L316, L347) while burying the estimation dependency in H5/Risk. I dock to 7 because "partly tautological" is itself hedged — H1 retains non-trivial content (the *slope* and the failure-onset distance are empirical even with a good estimator), so the briefing's own "partly" is doing honest work and I won't over-credit a claim that the briefing itself softens.

**Finding 5 — "The contribution is more defensible as a benchmark/protocol than a controller in a saturating G1-locked field." Score: 6/10.**
Directionally persuasive and I agree with the strategic instinct: a standardized error-vs-locomotion-distance + cross-embodiment-KMP-transfer protocol with *onboard* estimation is a more durable moat than another G1 tracker. But this is the softest finding because (a) it is a taste/strategy judgment, not a verifiable claim; (b) the Academic perspective rightly pushes back that protocols without a method rarely clear a top venue's novelty bar on their own; and (c) the field-saturation premise ("G1-locked") is asserted from the anchor list, not measured — much of the table *is* G1, but AGILE, BRS, ALMA, Booster T1, and PNDbotics Adam Pro also appear, so "G1-locked" is somewhat overstated. The protocol reframing is a good bet, not an established fact. I score 6.

**Mean of the five: 7.2. Lowest: 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 5 (protocol-over-controller, 6/10) is the least-confident, and Finding 3 (H2 may already tie, 7/10) is the most load-bearing-if-wrong.** I name Finding 5 as the weakest *link* because it is the one the actionable insight hangs the whole pivot on ("reframe the deliverable as a benchmark"), yet it is a strategy claim with no falsifier.

What would verify it: (a) a concrete venue-acceptance precedent — find 2-3 recent embodied-AI papers whose *primary* contribution was a measurement protocol / benchmark for a control property (not a new controller) and that cleared a top venue, to establish that the protocol-as-contribution path is real in this subfield; (b) a citation-velocity or adoption check on whether any existing whole-body benchmark (HumanoidBench, AGILE, BRS, HumanoidArena) actually gets *used* as a shared error-vs-distance protocol, which is the closest analogue to A3's proposed moat; (c) a direct count of distinct *embodiments* (not just G1) across the 21-row table to test the "G1-locked saturation" premise quantitatively rather than by impression.

For the most consequential risk (Finding 3): the single missing fact is a head-to-head, on *one* robot under *onboard* estimation, of learned-continuous-α vs HiWET tuned-constant-α vs HANDOFF α-free pelvis-frame, on reachable-workspace coverage *during locomotion*. That experiment (the briefing's own kill-switch) is the entire bet. Until it is run, H2 is unverified.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Skeptic and the Practitioner over-dominate; the Academic is under-weighted.** Three of the five findings (1, 3, 4) and the entire "hidden connection" reduce to the same Skeptic/Practitioner move: *every differentiator secretly collapses into base-state estimation*. That collapse is insightful and probably correct, but it is run three times under different labels (odometry tax / H1-is-about-base-pose / SLAM-front-ends-died), which inflates apparent corroboration — three voices saying the same thing is one finding, not three. The Historian is used mainly as a garnish on the Skeptic's estimation thesis (loop-closure-survived analogy). The Economist supplies the protocol-moat angle but only co-signs Finding 5. The Academic — whose job is to defend that a *learned continuous schedule* and a *new measurement* can be a real methodological contribution — is repeatedly cast as the lone dissenter ("challenged by the card," "challenged by Academic") and never gets to make its strongest case: that H3 (KMP-as-mechanism + cross-embodiment transfer), which is the card's *most novel and least-tautological* hypothesis, is barely mentioned in the briefing at all. The synthesis is a strong demolition but a one-sided one. Verdict: **not balanced — Skeptic-dominant, with Academic suppressed.**

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The Controls/Estimation Theorist (an observability-and-error-budget specialist).** The whole briefing treats "base-state estimation" as a monolithic scalar knob ("degrade the estimator, EE error degrades roughly linearly," H5). A controls theorist would reframe the entire bet: the real question is not *whether* estimation binds but *what is the closed-loop observability of base pose under active, scheduled base transport* — and here the conclusion could flip. Scheduling α to extend reach (H2) deliberately moves the base in ways that may *improve or destroy* the observability of the base-state estimator (proprioceptive odometry on a walking biped degrades precisely during the aggressive transport phases H2 commands). That means H2 (the schedule) and H5 (the estimator) are not independent — the schedule *changes the noise model of the thing it depends on*. This is a coupling the briefing never names, and it changes two conclusions: (1) the "estimation bet wearing a control-frame costume" framing is incomplete — it is a *joint estimation-and-excitation* bet, and the right kill-switch must vary α and estimator *together*, not hold one fixed; (2) it suggests a genuinely novel contribution the briefing dismisses — an α-schedule co-designed for base-pose observability (active-perception-style), which is neither HiWET's tuned constant nor a re-plot. A second under-weighted sub-angle: the Safety/Certification view (CMP 2604.07457 sits in the table as a feasibility filter) — under-load, in-contact world-frame error is the regime that actually matters for deployment, and the briefing only adds it as an afterthought ("add one under-load condition").

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a genuinely good adversarial brief: it correctly identifies that A3 has narrowed to two thin deltas, it locates the real load-bearing risk (H2 ties), it proposes a concrete, falsifiable kill-switch, and its central insight (everything reduces to base-state estimation) is sharp and probably right. That is well above average for a research-strategy memo. It does not reach A range for four reasons: (1) it triple-counts one insight as three corroborated findings, inflating confidence; (2) it under-engages H3 (KMP-as-mechanism + cross-embodiment transfer), which is the card's strongest non-tautological hypothesis and the one most likely to survive — the briefing's own framing would be *less* bearish if it took H3 seriously; (3) it misses the estimation-excitation coupling, which is the one technical point that could turn the bet from "incremental" into "novel"; (4) its headline strategic recommendation (protocol-over-controller) is its least-supported claim yet carries the most weight.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Run the kill-switch as specified, but vary α *and* the estimator jointly** (per the missing 6th perspective) — the current three-controller comparison holds estimation fixed, which cannot detect the schedule-degrades-observability failure mode that would actually decide the bet.
2. **De-duplicate the "it's all base-state estimation" insight** — state it once as Finding 2/the hidden connection, and free up a finding slot to *seriously evaluate H3* (KMP mechanism + cross-embodiment transfer), the card's most defensible and least-tautological lever, which the briefing currently ignores.
3. **Substantiate or soften the protocol-moat claim (Finding 5)** with a concrete venue/adoption precedent and an actual embodiment count across the 21-row table, instead of asserting "G1-locked saturation" from impression.
4. **Add the under-load / in-contact condition as a first-class arm of H1, not an afterthought** — free-space world-frame closure is the easy regime; the deployment-relevant and CEER-differentiating claim is precision under contact, and that is where the frame-vs-estimation question is actually hard.

*Net: DE-RISK before COMMIT is the right call. The briefing's own kill-switch is sound; my one substantive amendment is that the kill-switch must perturb the estimator and the α-schedule together, because they are coupled, and that coupling is also where A3's only path to real novelty lives.*

---

### A4 (Direction review)

Topic: A4 — "Unified-Latent Policy for Joint Loco-Manipulation Commands." The bet (verbatim from First-principles): the load-bearing test is the *camera-motion-stratified* two-LAM-vs-one-LAM comparison that [[2512.11047|WholeBodyVLA]]'s internal ablation leaves open — its two visual-dynamics-split VQ-VAE latents beat a single shared latent *with the margin stratified by camera-motion magnitude*, using [[2606.09215|MotionWAM]]'s *external unified* whole-body motion latent as the negative control, at matched capacity, with the separate-over-shared margin growing on loco-heavy splits and near-zero on manip-heavy. Falsifiable: if an external shared latent matches the split, or the margin is flat across camera-motion bins, the visual-dynamics-split is not the lever.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys humanoid policies daily).**
Core position: The papers the card leans on run at humbling real-robot frequencies and tiny task sets — MotionWAM is 4.9 Hz on an *A100*, WholeBodyVLA reports 78.0% on exactly three tasks (Bag/Box/Cart), HALOMI 90% Bag-Transfer on a single G1. The "camera-motion-stratified margin" is a clean lab variable that evaporates the moment you have one ego-camera physically bolted to a moving head, where IMU jitter and motion blur dominate the "static-vs-moving" distinction you want to bin on.
Strongest supporting evidence: HALOMI's own ablation shows the *active neck / ego-view alignment* is the lever (Bag Transfer 10%→90% with ego-alignment; 90%→30% without active perception) — i.e. on a real humanoid the gain came from gaze control, not from splitting the latent. WholeBodyVLA itself ranks the split *third* (+12.0 pp) behind action-free pretraining (+38.7%) and the LMO RL policy (+24.0%).
The one thing only this perspective says: Your two-LAM architecture doubles the data/training pipeline you must maintain (two VQ-VAE codebooks, two video corpora) for the *third*-ranked driver; on a real robot the +24.0% LMO-RL controller (precise squat/turn/advance commands) and active perception buy more success per engineering-hour than the split you're betting the paper on. Build the controller and the gaze policy first.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: The card's mechanism — "manipulation video (static cam) and locomotion video (moving cam) need separate latent models" — is a *specific instance* of the now-well-characterized LAM camera-motion confound, and the literature is converging on *disentanglement within one latent* rather than *hard-splitting into two*. That directly contests "the split is the lever."
Strongest supporting evidence: DiLA (2605.15725) decouples dynamics-relevant "structure" from appearance "content" inside a *single* continuous latent and shows the predictive bottleneck itself drives disentanglement; "What Do Latent Action Models Actually Learn?" (2506.15691) and "Why Latent Actions Fail" (2605.20223) formalize that frame-to-frame change conflates agent action with camera/ego motion; VLA-JEPA (2602.10098) argues latent-action objectives wrongly anchor to pixel variation. None split manip-vs-loco; all attack the same confound A4 names.
The one thing only this perspective says: Your falsifier may already be half-triggered in spirit — if a *disentangled* single latent (DiLA-style) absorbs the camera-motion confound, then MotionWAM-style "naive shared" is the wrong negative control; the real comparison is split-by-dynamics vs *disentangled-unified*, and the card never names that arm.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already answered).**
Core position: WholeBodyVLA already ran two-LAM-vs-shared-LAM (78.0% vs 66.0%) and *its own authors* labeled decoupling "beneficial but not the primary factor." The card concedes this in writing. So the headline ("unified beats decoupled," "split beats shared") is settled; what's left is a stratification sub-study that the original authors implicitly judged not worth elevating.
Strongest supporting evidence: The card's own text: WholeBodyVLA ranks the split third and provides "no camera-motion-stratified margin"; MotionWAM (one shared latent) and Fast-Slow WB VLA (one unified token, 90% scoop, tokenizer-ablation 83.3%→0%) both *win* with a single latent on their own tasks — direct existence proofs that a unified latent is sufficient for SOTA whole-body results. HALOMI (one manifold latent) hits 90% Bag-Transfer matching WholeBodyVLA's 78.0% Bag/Box/Cart.
The one thing only this perspective says: A result of the form "the +12.0 pp split→shared gap is concentrated on loco-heavy bins" is a *negative/nuance* paper, not a method paper — reviewers will ask "so should I split or not?" and your honest answer (only on loco-heavy tasks, and it's third behind pretraining) is a weak headline. The whitespace is real but *small*.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: "Unified whole-body VLA" is the 2025-26 industry land-grab — Astribot (Fast-Slow/DuoCore-FS), AgiBot/OpenDriveLab (WholeBodyVLA), the GR00T ecosystem — so the narrative is propelled by humanoid-startup fundraising and the GR00T-baseline-beating treadmill, not by whether the *split* is the lever. Every anchor paper benchmarks against GR00T (N1.6/N1.7) because that's the citation-magnet comparator.
Strongest supporting evidence: The card's targets are saturated with "+X pp over GR00T-N1.x" (MotionWAM +32 pp over N1.7; Psi0 >40% over N1.6). Real-robot task counts are tiny (3–9 tasks) because real humanoid data is the binding cost — exactly the regime where an academic lab cannot out-spend the labs that own the robots and the 800h human / 30h robot data (Psi0).
The one thing only this perspective says: A pure-architecture stratification study is *cheap and defensible* for an academic — it needs no new fleet, just a re-analysis harness (HumanoidArena 2606.17833 gives the latent↔tracker isolation; LHM-Humanoid 2508.16943 gives the 350-scene suite). That's the correct economic wedge: don't try to beat WholeBodyVLA's SR, *explain* its ablation. But the same cheapness means a lab with the robot can scoop you with one appendix.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen the prior waves).**
Core position: This is the pixel-vs-latent and modular-vs-end-to-end pendulum replaying on humanoids. The "two specialized models because the statistics differ" instinct echoes the early Dreamer-era split between reward/dynamics heads and the later collapse into single shared world models; the field repeatedly starts with hand-split modules and ends with one bigger model + better disentanglement once data scales.
Strongest supporting evidence: VENOM (2606.16696) is the historian's exhibit — a *single shared central transformer*, explicitly *not* decoupling body parts, matching RL experts (RMSE ~0.42–0.58 rad) across 5 humanoids and *beating* MLP baselines that suffer negative transfer. Historically the shared model wins once it has enough data; the split wins only in the low-data regime. MotionWAM/HALOMI/Fast-Slow continue that arc.
The one thing only this perspective says: Bets premised on "the data statistics *force* an architectural split" have a poor track record (cf. separate optical-flow streams in video nets, later absorbed into unified transformers). Frame A4 as "*when* does the split help (low-data, loco-heavy)?" — a scaling-regime claim that survives the pendulum — not "the split is the lever," which the next data-scale model will erase.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Academic vs the card's premise*: The card says different visual dynamics *force* two latent models; the LAM-confound literature (DiLA 2605.15725, 2506.15691, 2605.20223) says the camera-motion confound is better dissolved by *disentangling* one latent. Split-as-architecture vs disentangle-within-one.
   - *Skeptic vs Practitioner-as-stated*: Skeptic says the split question is settled (78.0 vs 66.0, authors call it "not primary"); the card stakes the paper on stratifying exactly that margin. Settled-headline vs unrun-substudy.
   - *Historian vs card*: VENOM/MotionWAM (single shared latent, SOTA) vs WholeBodyVLA's split — the shared latent is an *existence proof* the historian reads as "the split is a low-data artifact," directly opposing "the split is the lever."
2. **Strongest vs weakest.** Strongest: ACADEMIC — it is the only perspective that engages the *mechanism* (the camera-motion confound) with papers that target it head-on, and it reframes the negative control (disentangled-unified, not naive-shared). Weakest: ECONOMIST — correct about incentives and the cheap-wedge, but it adjudicates *fundability*, not *truth* of the split claim.
3. **Resolving question.** Does a *disentangled* single latent (DiLA-style content/structure split inside one codebook) match the two-LAM split on loco-heavy, camera-moving tasks at matched capacity? If yes, the architecture-split is not the lever — disentanglement is — and the bet collapses to a known result. If no, A4 has a real, defensible mechanism.
4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** A *unified/joint* loco+manip latent interface beats a naive decoupled "manipulation-policy + separate velocity controller" stack on the same tasks (WholeBodyVLA 78.0 vs ≤56.7 same-LMO; LeVERB 58.5% vs 7.5% naive hierarchical; HALOMI 90%; MotionWAM +32 pp). Every perspective grants the *joint-interface* claim; they only contest whether the *split-by-visual-dynamics* is what carries it.
5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** Safety/stability of the *joint* prediction under real perturbation and the *failure mode of the split*: when a single physical ego-camera produces both static and moving regimes within one episode (mid-task gaze + step), which latent owns the boundary frames, and does mis-routing cause a balance-relevant action error? No perspective, and no card hypothesis (H6 tests entanglement, not boundary mis-routing), addresses the real-time routing/fallback that decides whether the split is *deployable*, not just *accurate*.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** A4 is a *real but narrow* whitespace built on a *settled* foundation. The field-level claim ("a unified joint loco+manip latent beats a naively decoupled stack") is consensus and well-evidenced (WholeBodyVLA 78.0% vs ≤56.7% same-LMO; LeVERB 7.8× over naive hierarchical; MotionWAM +32 pp over GR00T-N1.7). The card honestly narrows its bet to the *unrun* sub-claim — that the visual-dynamics *split* is the lever, measured by a *camera-motion-stratified* two-LAM-vs-one-LAM ablation against an external unified latent. That specific stratification is genuinely unrun. But its central mechanism — that camera/ego-motion contaminates latent actions — is the exact problem a fast-moving LAM-disentanglement literature (DiLA, "What Do LAMs Actually Learn?", "Why Latent Actions Fail", VLA-JEPA) is already attacking, and that literature's answer (disentangle one latent) competes with A4's answer (split into two). The honest framing is a *scaling-regime / nuance* result ("the split helps on loco-heavy, low-data tasks, and ranks third behind pretraining"), not a SOTA method paper.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *A unified joint loco+manip latent beats a naive decoupled stack on the same tasks.* Supported by Practitioner, Academic, Skeptic, Historian (all grant it); challenged by none. Most reliable — but it is the *consensus*, not A4's contribution. (WholeBodyVLA 78.0 vs 56.7; LeVERB 58.5 vs 7.5; HALOMI 90%.)
2. *The visual-dynamics split is NOT the primary driver — pretraining and the LMO controller are.* Supported by Skeptic, Practitioner, and WholeBodyVLA's own authors ("not the primary factor"; +38.7% pretraining, +24.0% LMO, +12.0 pp split). Challenged by the card's optimism that stratification will elevate it. Highly reliable from the source paper itself.
3. *The camera-motion-stratified margin study is genuinely unrun.* Supported by Academic, Economist (the cheap wedge), card. Challenged by Skeptic (it's a substudy the authors deprioritized). Reliable as a *novelty* fact; contested as a *significance* fact.
4. *A disentangled single latent may absorb the confound, dissolving the split.* Supported by Academic, Historian; challenged by the card (which only pits split against *naive* shared, not disentangled-shared). Reliable that the competing approach exists (DiLA 2605.15725); unresolved whether it wins.
5. *On real robots the lever is the controller (LMO-RL, +24.0%) and active perception (HALOMI 90%→30% ablation), not the latent split.* Supported by Practitioner, Economist; challenged by none on evidence. Reliable, and the most actionable de-risking signal.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Academic's "disentangle one latent" and the Historian's "shared model wins at scale" are the same claim viewed from two angles: both predict the split is a *low-data artifact* that a better-regularized single latent erases. If true, A4's correct falsifier is not "MotionWAM's naive shared matches the split" but "a *disentangled* shared (DiLA-style) matches the split" — a stronger negative control the card has not specified. That is the single edit that would make or break the bet.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not COMMIT as framed.** The "split is the lever" headline is too narrow and too exposed (the authors deprioritized it; a robot-owning lab can scoop it in an appendix). Re-aim at the *scaling-regime* question and harden the negative control. Concrete next experiment: on LHM-Humanoid's 350-scene seen/unseen suite (2508.16943), run a 3-way matched-capacity ablation — (a) two-LAM visual-dynamics split, (b) MotionWAM-style naive unified latent, (c) a *disentangled* unified latent (DiLA-style content/structure within one codebook) — with tasks stratified by measured camera-motion magnitude, using HumanoidArena (2606.17833) for the latent↔tracker isolation. Headline target: characterize *the data/camera-motion regime where the split beats both unified arms*. If (c) matches (a), report it honestly as "disentanglement substitutes for the split" (still publishable, different paper). This costs no new fleet — the Economist's cheap-wedge — and pre-empts the obvious reviewer scoop.

**Frontier question.** Is the manip-vs-loco visual-dynamics split a *fundamental* structure of embodied latent actions, or a *transient* low-data symptom that one disentangled, sufficiently-pretrained latent erases? Whoever answers *when* the split is necessary (as a function of data scale, camera-motion entropy, and pretraining budget) — rather than *whether* it helps once — owns the durable result.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Grounding: A4 card lines 383-468 of `Capability/Whole-Body.md`; spot-checked against KH notes for the 14 load-bearing IDs.*

**Verification summary (what I actually checked).**
- The four "LAM-disentanglement literature" papers the CEO summary leans on do NOT appear in the A4 card body (zero grep hits, lines 383-468). Two of the four exist in the vault — DiLA `2605.15725` and VLA-JEPA `2602.10098` — and two do NOT exist in KH at all: `2506.15691` ("What Do LAMs Actually Learn?") and `2605.20223` ("Why Latent Actions Fail"). So the "fast-moving literature" is, in this vault, a 1-to-2-paper claim wearing a field's clothing.
- DiLA `2605.15725` is real and on-point: it "separates video into dynamics-relevant 'structure' and dynamics-irrelevant 'content' pathways" via an information bottleneck, within ONE framework. This is exactly a disentangled-single-latent competitor to A4's two-LAM split. The briefing's strongest move — promote a DiLA-style arm to the negative control — is technically sound.
- WholeBodyVLA `2512.11047` KH (65 lines) confirms +38.7% pretraining, +24.0% LMO, and the qualitative "separate LAMs outperform a single shared model." It does NOT contain the `66.0%` shared-LAM number, the `12.0 pp` gap, the "not the primary factor" quote, or the "ranks it third" framing. Those four specifics — the spine of finding #2 — are not vault-traceable.

**1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability).**

1. **Unified joint latent beats a naive decoupled stack — 9/10.** Multiply-evidenced across WholeBodyVLA / LeVERB / MotionWAM / HALOMI / LHM-Humanoid, and the briefing correctly notes this is consensus, not A4's contribution. Docked one point only because "naive/same-LMO" comparators are weak baselines, so the margin overstates the structural win.

2. **The split is NOT the primary driver; pretraining + LMO are — 5/10.** The DIRECTION is almost certainly right (the +38.7% / +24.0% deltas dwarf any split effect, and both are confirmed in KH). But the specific evidentiary props the briefing cites — `66.0%`, `12.0 pp`, "not the primary factor," "ranks it third" — are NOT in the WholeBodyVLA KH note. The briefing presents an authors'-own-words quote that the vault cannot source. Right conclusion, unverified quotation.

3. **The camera-motion-stratified margin study is genuinely unrun — 7/10.** Plausible and consistent with the card; no paper in the pool stratifies the split→shared margin by camera-motion magnitude. Capped at 7 because "unrun" is an absence claim, and absence over a non-exhaustive vault is inherently soft — and the Skeptic's framing (it is a substudy the authors deliberately deprioritized, not whitespace nobody saw) is the more likely reading.

4. **A disentangled single latent may dissolve the split — 7/10.** DiLA verified as a genuine content/structure disentanglement competitor, so the threat is real (the "competing approach exists" half is 9/10). The "may win" half is unresolved by anyone, which is honestly what the briefing says, so 7 overall. This is the briefing's best insight.

5. **On real robots the lever is the controller + active perception, not the split — 6/10.** HALOMI's 90%→30% active-perception ablation and the +24.0% LMO gain are real and directionally support this. But it conflates three different systems' levers (HALOMI's perception, WholeBodyVLA's LMO) into one "the lever is not the split" headline; that is a synthesis convenience, not a controlled finding. Actionable, slightly over-generalized.

Mean ≈ 6.8; min = 5.

**2. Weakest link.** Finding #2's evidentiary props — `66.0%` shared-LAM, `12.0 pp` split→shared gap, and the "beneficial but not the primary factor" / "ranks it third" quotations. These are the load-bearing facts behind the entire "DE-RISK, do not COMMIT" thesis (if the split really is the authors' deprioritized third-place lever, the scoop risk is real; if not, the whole re-aim is unmotivated). They are asserted in the card (lines 395/399/400/408) and inherited wholesale by the briefing, but the WholeBodyVLA KH note does not contain them. **To verify:** pull the WholeBodyVLA PDF/appendix ablation table and confirm (a) the shared-LAM SR is 66.0%, (b) the paper text literally calls decoupling "not the primary factor," and (c) the ranking against +38.7%/+24.0% is the authors' framing and not the card's interpolation. Until then, finding #2 is a strong claim on an unsourced quote.

**3. Bias check.** The **Skeptic** over-dominated, with the **Economist** as co-pilot. The entire CEO summary, the "actionable insight," and the frontier question all converge on one move — shrink the claim, harden the negative control, do the cheap ablation — which is the Skeptic-Economist axis. The Academic supplies exactly one idea (disentangled-single-latent), the Historian one (scale erases the split), and these are explicitly collapsed into "the same claim from two angles," which conveniently removes the one voice that might have argued the split is fundamental. The **Practitioner** is cited but only in service of de-risking (controller > split), never to defend that a manip/loco split is a real deployment win because the two genuinely have different failure modes. Net: a coherent but mono-thesis review. A balanced synthesis would have let the Academic argue the split is a true inductive bias, not just a low-data artifact.

**4. Missing perspective.** A **Hardware/Control-Theorist (the Roboticist)**. Every perspective here treats the manip-vs-loco distinction as a *visual-statistics* property (camera motion entropy). But the more fundamental reason to split may be *control-theoretic*: locomotion is a underactuated, contact-switching, balance-constrained problem at 50 Hz; manipulation is a quasi-static, force-modulated problem in a stable base frame. If the split is justified by the **plant dynamics and timescale separation** rather than the **camera**, then the briefing's whole falsifier is mis-aimed — a disentangled single visual latent (DiLA) could absorb the *camera* confound and STILL fail to capture the *dynamics/timescale* split, meaning A4's bet survives even if H1's camera-motion stratification comes back flat. This 6th angle inverts the conclusion: it makes the camera-motion framing (the card's H1, lines 431-435) the weak formalization and the dynamics-split the durable one. The briefing should re-state the bet in terms of plant/timescale, not pixels.

**5. Overall grade: B.** This is an unusually honest, well-structured review that correctly identifies A4 as a narrow whitespace on a settled foundation, and its single best contribution — promoting a DiLA-style disentangled latent from afterthought to the actual negative control — is verified-correct and would genuinely make-or-break the bet. It loses the A range on three things, in priority order.

**Top fixes.**
1. **Source or retract the finding-#2 quotes.** Either cite the WholeBodyVLA ablation table for the `66.0%` / `12.0 pp` / "not the primary factor" claims, or downgrade them to "the card asserts" hedged language. The de-risk thesis cannot rest on a quote the vault cannot produce.
2. **Stop calling it a literature.** Two of four "disentanglement literature" papers do not exist in the vault and were not in the card; name DiLA (and at most VLA-JEPA) as the one-to-two specific competitors they are, and verify the two missing IDs before citing them as the field's "answer."
3. **Re-aim the bet from pixels to plant.** Add the control-theorist falsifier: test whether the split is justified by dynamics/timescale separation, not just camera-motion magnitude — otherwise H1 can come back flat for the wrong reason and the team kills a live bet on a mis-specified test.
