### B (Cluster review)

Topic: the COHERENCE + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster B — "Mobile Manipulation (Nav↔Manip Coupling)": *"the base velocity is itself a manipulation degree of freedom — repositioning extends the reachable workspace mid-task, not before it. The cluster reads the moving base as one coupling that surfaces in three places: the order base and arm actions are factored (B1), what the moving camera keeps in view (B2), and what scene state persists once the base turns away (B3)."* The review asks whether this carves mobile manipulation at the right joint and whether the shared bet (one coupling → three sub-problems) is sound, given that the field already largely concedes "base-first beats pipeline" (B1), "active gaze beats fixed camera" (B2), and "memory beats memoryless" (B3).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys mobile manipulators daily).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's "base velocity is a manipulation DoF" framing is real and matches what you feel on hardware — but the three sub-axes are not equally deployable, and the cluster understates that perception, not action-factoring, is the field's actual wall. B1's autoregressive base→torso→arm decoding is the only axis with a clean deployment story; B2 and B3 each add a control loop / memory footprint that may not pay for itself on a real robot.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2503.05652|BRS]]'s WB-VIMA holds 88% sub-task / 58% entire-task with *near-zero safety violations* on a real Galaxea R1, and the ablation says autoregressive decoding is "essential for mitigating error propagation" — a real-robot result, not sim. [[2602.23024|InCoM]] reports a deployable 140 ms latency (vs DP's 790 ms) at 83.8% ManiSkill-HAB — so the structured-coordination win survives the control-loop budget. But the cluster's own [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]] floor (5–15% with GT perception → 0.4–0.6% with a real DETIC detector) is the practitioner's nightmare: a real open-vocab detector erases the entire action-coupling gain.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Run every B1/B2/B3 hypothesis with a *real* perception stack, not ground-truth state — B1's H2/H3 stratification by in-task base motion is meaningless if the SR delta is swamped by detector noise. The card flags this once (B1 risk #3) but then designs most experiments on MS-HAB/RoboCasa where perception is given. The deployable wedge is B1 + B2 fused (gaze recovers the perception slice the action-coupling gain needs), measured under real detection.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The cluster is honest that each sub-axis's *headline* claim is settled (base-first, active-gaze, memory-beats-memoryless all conceded in-card), and has correctly retreated to second-order *conditional-structure* bets (B1: which factoring when; B2: learned-gaze-coupled-to-chosen-base-trajectory; B3: in-policy dynamic scene+episodic + multi-room scaling-law). The literature supports this retreat, but the B3 retreat is the most exposed: the "episodic spatial memory for long-horizon mobile manipulation" framing has *already* been published outside the card.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card itself documents the settling — [[2507.01961|AC-DiT]] ran the base→arm (49.5) vs arm→base (47.3) directional ablation, [[2602.23024|InCoM]] ran the head-to-head beating WB-VIMA + AC-DiT (83.8% vs 55.6%), and [[2305.04866|Causal-WBMM]] established "dependencies are causal not flat" (93.6% vs 74.3% PPO) *years* earlier. On B3, [[2606.12956|SERF]] already does in-policy 4D scene+robot memory with within-episode relocated-object SE(3) tracking (58.7% BEHAVIOR-1K vs 54.0% static-map, +23% relocated). The alphaxiv probe surfaced [[2604.13633|ESCAPE]] (CUHK-Shenzhen, Apr 2026, NOT in card) — "Episodic Spatial Memory and Adaptive Execution Policy for Long-Horizon Mobile Manipulation" — an autoregressive persistent 3D spatial memory + adaptive global/local execution, 65.09%/60.79% SR on ALFRED, MRU-ablation −19.52%/−34.11% SR.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: B3's surviving novelty is *narrow and specific* (in-policy learned fusion of a separate episodic channel with a dynamic-purged scene map, swept over multi-room extent) — and the card must defend it against ESCAPE/SERF explicitly, because a reviewer will know both. The "scene + episodic in one *learned* policy with dynamic purge and a multi-room scaling law" gap is real, but the literature has crept right up to its edge.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's coherence is partly *post-hoc*: "one coupling surfacing in three places" is an elegant narrative, but B1 (action), B2 (perception), and B3 (memory) are three *separate* literatures the card has stitched together — and a monolithic RL policy already gets all three "emergently" without the decomposition the card sells as the contribution. The whitespace is real only if the *separability* claim (you can isolate the action-coupling slice from the perception-coupling slice from the memory slice) is itself true, and the card never proves it.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2405.07991|SPIN-Mobile-Manip]] is a *single RL policy* that emits base+arm control *and* predicts where to look while moving the whole body (90–96% hard-nav sim vs <26% fixed-camera) — gaze and base-coupling fall out of one policy with no factoring, no separate gaze head, no memory module. [[2505.12278|Emergent-Active-Perception-Dexterity]] does the same on a legged humanoid. The card concedes SPIN is "the strongest existing foil the bet must beat." On B1, [[2603.22760|SG-VLA]] hits 0.73 on MS-HAB SetTable with a *single head and no factoring* — beating AC-DiT's 0.556 — which directly undercuts "factoring is the lever." If a no-factoring single head can top the factored AC-DiT, the conditioning-structure margin (B1's whole bet) may be a capacity/data artifact.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The card's contribution is *attribution*, not capability — it bets that decomposing the coupling into separable, individually-ablatable slices beats the monolithic policy that already works. That is a science claim, not a performance claim, and it is the *easiest to lose*: if SPIN-style monolithic RL matches the decomposed stack at equal data/compute, every "isolate when X matters" hypothesis is a paper about a margin nobody needs to exploit.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money / incentives / benchmarks).**
- *Core position*: The cluster is benchmark-shaped: ManiSkill-HAB SetTable has become the LIBERO of mobile manipulation, and the proliferation of on-suite datapoints ([[2507.01961|AC-DiT]] 55.6 / [[2602.23024|InCoM]] 83.8 / [[2509.16063|DSPv2]] 50.8 / [[2603.22760|SG-VLA]] 0.73) signals a saturation race where each paper claims a new factoring to top the table. The cluster's *conditional-structure* retreat is the economically rational move — it sells a stratification nobody else ran — but it inherits the benchmark's saturation risk.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Four systems now report MS-HAB SetTable within ~18 months, with InCoM's 83.8% already approaching ceiling on the easier SetTable split while long-horizon completion stays <20% ([[2412.13211|MS-HAB]]). The data substrate is industry-funded and large ([[2512.24653|RoboMIND-2.0]] 20K mobile-manip trajectories across 6 embodiments; [[2602.05233|MobileManiBench]] 150K trajectories) — the incentive is to consume that data with a new architecture, not to run the un-glamorous stratification the card proposes. Real-robot rigs (Galaxea R1, TidyBot++, Fetch, TIAGo++) are expensive and per-lab, which is *why* the card's most-cited evidence is sim and its real numbers are small-N (EchoVLA 6 tasks, BRS 5 tasks).
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The cluster's defensible economic moat is the *stratification benchmark itself* (binning MS-HAB / BiGym / [[2602.05233|MobileManiBench]] by in-task base-travel and out-of-view returns), not a new policy — because the policies are commoditizing fast. A contribution that produces the *binning protocol* (the H2/H3 axis) is more durable than one more on-suite SR number that the next quarter's model beats.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the modular-vs-end-to-end pendulum replaying on mobile manipulation. The classic nav-then-manipulate pipeline ([[2306.11565|HomeRobot]]) is the modular thesis; WB-VIMA / SPIN are the end-to-end antithesis; the cluster's "factor the joint *but* keep it one policy" is the synthesis — exactly the arc that played out in autonomous driving (perception→planning→control modules → end-to-end → "end-to-end with structured intermediate representations") and in SLAM (hand-engineered maps → learned implicit memory → hybrid neural maps).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: B3 *is* the SLAM/active-mapping debate in policy clothing — [[2411.04999|DynaMem]]'s ray-cast purge is online occupancy mapping, [[2410.11989|DovSG]]'s dynamic scene graph is semantic SLAM, and the card's bet (fold the map *into* the policy) is the "learned implicit memory" swing that the field already half-reversed toward hybrids ([[2606.12956|SERF]], [[2604.13633|ESCAPE]]). B2's planner-vs-learned split ([[2605.02487|Visibility-Aware-Mobile-Grasping]] / [[2310.00433|ActPerMoMa]] planners vs [[2506.10968|EyeRobot]] / [[2505.06182|APPLE]] RL-learned gaze) is the active-vision-planning-vs-learned-attention cycle from the 1990s active-vision literature.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Each past pendulum swing was won not by the architecture but by *who controlled the data and the standardized benchmark*. The cluster should expect the monolithic-RL camp (SPIN-style) to reabsorb the decomposed gains the moment data scales — so the durable contribution is the one the pendulum cannot un-swing: a *measurement* (the stratification/scaling-law that says *when* each coupling matters), which survives whichever architecture wins.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Where 2+ perspectives directly clash.**
   - *Separability — SKEPTIC vs ACADEMIC/PRACTITIONER*: The Skeptic says the three sub-axes are not separable — [[2405.07991|SPIN]] and [[2505.12278|Emergent-Active-Perception]] get action-coupling + gaze emergently from one monolithic policy, and [[2603.22760|SG-VLA]] tops factored AC-DiT with *no* factoring, so "isolate the slice" is a science claim that may not hold. The Academic counters that the card's *whole value* is exactly that decomposition (B1-H5/H6, B2-H6, B3-H1 are separability tests). They cannot both be right: either the slices are individually ablatable (card's premise) or the monolithic policy is the right unit (Skeptic's).
   - *Where the gain lives — PRACTITIONER vs ECONOMIST*: Practitioner says perception (real detectors) is the wall and B1's action-coupling gain is masked under real perception; Economist says the field keeps optimizing the *action* factoring on MS-HAB because that's where the benchmark/data incentive points. They clash on whether the cluster's center of mass (B1, action) is even the binding constraint.
   - *B3 novelty — ACADEMIC vs the card*: Academic holds that [[2604.13633|ESCAPE]] (not in card) and [[2606.12956|SERF]] (in card, conceded) have already published "episodic spatial memory for long-horizon mobile manipulation," so B3's framing is partially pre-empted; the card asserts the in-policy + dynamic-purge + multi-room-scaling combination is unbuilt.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest: SKEPTIC.* Its separability challenge strikes the cluster's actual contribution (attribution, not capability) and is backed by two in-card concessions (SPIN as "strongest foil," SG-VLA beating factored AC-DiT with no factoring). It is the argument most likely to come from a real reviewer and the hardest for the card to answer with existing evidence.
   - *Weakest: HISTORIAN.* Insightful framing (modular↔end-to-end pendulum) but it does not change the *commit/de-risk* decision — "expect the pendulum to swing back" is true of every direction and yields no falsifiable next step. Its useful residue (control the measurement) is better stated by the Economist.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   *At matched data and compute, does the decomposed stack (B1 factoring + B2 base-coupled gaze + B3 in-policy memory) beat a single monolithic RL/VLA policy ([[2405.07991|SPIN]]-style) on the SAME reach-extension + multi-room + relocated-object tasks — and if it ties, do the individual slice-ablations (B1-H6, B2-H6, B3-H1) still attribute the gain cleanly?* This resolves Skeptic-vs-Academic: if monolithic ties or wins, the cluster's separability premise fails and the contribution collapses to attribution-only; if decomposed wins, every sub-hypothesis becomes worth running.

4. **What EVERY perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   The *first principle is correct*: the base velocity is a manipulation DoF, in-task repositioning extends the workspace, and a system that freezes the base before the arm acts ([[2306.11565|HomeRobot]] pipeline, [[2505.23692|Mobi-Pi]] single-pose) gives up a real lever. No perspective disputes that the coupling exists or that nav-then-manipulate is a genuine failure mode — they dispute whether it must be *decomposed* (vs handled monolithically) and *where* it binds (action vs perception). The coupling itself is settled ground.

5. **The topic NONE addressed (blind spot).**
   *Failure recovery and the closed-loop safety of the coupling under disturbance.* Every perspective argues about success-rate margins and attribution, but none asks what happens when the coupled base+arm policy *fails mid-task* — when an unmodeled contact, a slipped grasp, or a base-localization drift puts the whole-body state OOD. The cluster's coupling makes failures *correlated across base and arm* (a base error now propagates into arm error by construction of the joint factoring), yet there is no recovery/runtime-monitoring axis. B1-H5 touches safety-violation *rate* but never recovery *after* a violation, and no direction owns "detect-and-recover for the coupled whole body."

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**1. CEO summary (nuance, not headline).**
Cluster B carves mobile manipulation at a genuine joint — "the base velocity is a manipulation DoF" is a sound, well-grounded first principle, and the three-way split (action factoring / active gaze / out-of-view memory) is a defensible decomposition of *one* coupling rather than three unrelated topics. But the cluster has already spent its first-order novelty: base-first (B1), active-gaze (B2), and memory-beats-memoryless (B3) are each conceded-settled in-card, and the surviving bets are all *second-order conditional-structure* claims (which factoring when, learned-gaze-coupled-to-chosen-base-trajectory, in-policy dynamic scene+episodic with a multi-room scaling law). Those retreats are individually defensible but collectively rest on one unproven premise — that the action / perception / memory slices are *separable and individually ablatable* — which a monolithic RL policy ([[2405.07991|SPIN]]) and a no-factoring single head ([[2603.22760|SG-VLA]] beating factored AC-DiT) directly threaten. B3 is additionally the most pre-empted: ESCAPE (2604.13633, not in card) and SERF (2606.12956, conceded) have published the "episodic spatial memory for long-horizon mobile manipulation" framing, leaving B3 only its narrow in-policy-fused + dynamic-purge + multi-room-scaling wedge. The cluster is COMMIT-able, but as a *measurement/attribution* contribution (when does each coupling matter), not as a new-architecture race on a saturating benchmark.

**2. Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The first principle is correct and the cluster coheres around one real coupling.* (Supported: ALL five. Challenged: none directly — Skeptic challenges the *decomposition*, not the coupling.) Most reliable: in-task repositioning extending the workspace is confirmed by BRS, AC-DiT's directional ablation, and the HomeRobot/Mobi-Pi base-freeze failure mode.
2. *Each sub-axis's headline claim is already settled; the live bets are second-order.* (Supported: ACADEMIC, ECONOMIST, card itself. Challenged: none — the card concedes this explicitly.) Reliable because it is documented in-card with [[2507.01961|AC-DiT]], [[2602.23024|InCoM]], [[2305.04866|Causal-WBMM]].
3. *B3's framing is partially pre-empted.* (Supported: ACADEMIC; alphaxiv-surfaced [[2604.13633|ESCAPE]] + conceded [[2606.12956|SERF]]. Challenged: card's narrow-wedge defense — ESCAPE is ALFRED/modular not in-policy-learned-VLA, no dynamic purge, no multi-room sweep.) Reliable on the *framing*, uncertain on the *specific combination*.
4. *The separability premise is the cluster's weakest load-bearing assumption.* (Supported: SKEPTIC, with SPIN + SG-VLA. Challenged: ACADEMIC/PRACTITIONER who treat the slice-ablations as the contribution.) Medium reliability — it is an open empirical question the card has not yet answered.
5. *Perception under real detectors, not action-factoring, may be the binding constraint.* (Supported: PRACTITIONER, with HomeRobot's 5–15%→0.4–0.6% collapse. Challenged: ECONOMIST notes the incentive points at action anyway.) Medium reliability — true as a risk, unquantified for the cluster's own tasks.

**3. The hidden cross-perspective connection.**
The Economist's "the durable moat is the stratification benchmark, not the policy" and the Historian's "the pendulum cannot un-swing a *measurement*" and the Skeptic's "the contribution is attribution, not capability" are the *same insight* arriving from three directions: the cluster's real, defensible deliverable is not a winning policy but a *characterization of when each coupling matters* — the binning of tasks by in-task base-travel (B1-H2), by out-of-view returns (B3-H3), and by during-vs-between gaze stability (B2-H5). Whoever owns that measurement owns the result regardless of which architecture (monolithic or decomposed) eventually wins, because the measurement is what tells you *which* architecture to deploy *when*.

**4. Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to B1+B2 as the spine; treat B3 as a downstream rider.**
Commit to Cluster B, but re-center it on the *separability/attribution* contribution and de-risk in this order:
- *Kill-shot experiment first (resolves the biggest contradiction)*: at matched data + compute, run the decomposed stack (BRS-style autoregressive factoring + base-coupled gaze) vs a single monolithic policy ([[2405.07991|SPIN]]-style) on a *reach-extension + real-perception* task set, and run the 2×3 grid (B1-H6 factoring × B2-H6 GT/active/passive perception) so the action-coupling slice and the perception-coupling slice are *individually attributed*. If the decomposed stack cannot beat OR cleanly out-attribute the monolithic policy, the cluster's premise is dead — find this out in experiment #1, not paper #3.
- *De-risk B3 explicitly against ESCAPE + SERF*: add [[2604.13633|ESCAPE]] to the B3 Related-papers table and re-state the wedge as "in-policy *learned* fusion of a *separate* episodic task-history channel with a *dynamic-purged* scene map, swept over multi-room extent" — the exact conjunction ESCAPE (modular/ALFRED, no purge, no sweep) and SERF (within-episode SE(3), no episodic channel, single-room) each miss. If a reviewer can name a system with all four, B3 drops to a sub-section of B-memory rather than a standalone direction.
- *Promote the stratification protocol to a first-class artifact*: the binning of [[2412.13211|MS-HAB]] / [[2602.05233|MobileManiBench]] by in-task base-travel and out-of-view returns is the durable, un-saturatable contribution (Economist + Historian) — ship it as the benchmark, not as an appendix to a policy.

**5. The frontier question that would change everything.**
*Is the nav↔manip coupling something that must be architecturally decomposed and attributed, or does it emerge for free in a sufficiently-scaled monolithic policy — and if it emerges for free, is there any task regime (multi-room + relocated-object + real-perception + recovery-after-failure) where explicit decomposition is provably necessary rather than merely convenient?* If monolithic scaling absorbs the coupling everywhere, the entire cluster collapses to a measurement paper; if there is a provable regime where decomposition is necessary, that regime *is* the cluster's flagship result — and the unaddressed failure-recovery / correlated-OOD blind spot is the most likely place such a regime lives.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded in `Capability/Whole-Body.md` lines 469-721 (Cluster B: B1 action-factoring, B2 active-gaze, B3 out-of-view memory). I read the three sub-cards, their Related tables, and their H1-H6 hypothesis blocks before writing.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (per key finding, 1-10)

**Finding 1 — "The first principle is correct and the cluster coheres around one real coupling." — 9/10.**
This is the strongest claim in the briefing and it survives scrutiny. "Base velocity is a manipulation DoF" is not rhetoric: the card backs it with BRS's autoregressive-vs-naive-joint collapse (line 488), AC-DiT's *directional* ablation base→arm 49.5 vs arm→base 47.3 (line 497), and the HomeRobot/Mobi-Pi base-freeze failure mode (lines 506, 510). The three-way split (factoring / gaze / memory) is a genuine decomposition of *one* coupling — the moving base — not three stapled topics; the cluster header (line 471) states this explicitly and the cross-feeds (B1→B2 via base-trajectory conditioning, B2→B3 via write-quality) are real, not decorative. Docked one point only because "coheres around ONE coupling" is partly an authorial framing choice: memory (B3) is arguably a generic long-horizon-POMDP problem that exists for *fixed-base* manipulators too (LIBERO-Mem line 679, MemoryVLA line 676 are tabletop), so the "moving base" through-line is weaker for B3 than for B1/B2.

**Finding 2 — "Each sub-axis's headline claim is settled; the live bets are second-order." — 8/10.**
Well-supported and honestly conceded in-card. B1: base-first is settled (AC-DiT directional ablation + InCoM head-to-head, lines 484, 489); the surviving wedge is *which structure when* (H1/H2). B2: "active gaze beats fixed camera" is conceded consensus (line 565); the wedge is learned-look-at-coupled-to-chosen-base-trajectory. B3: "memory beats memoryless" is taken (line 650); the wedge is in-policy-fused + dynamic-purge + multi-room-scaling. The retreat to second-order conditional-structure claims is accurate. I dock two points because the briefing slightly *overstates* the cleanliness of the concession: InCoM (line 484, 498) explicitly argues unidirectional base→arm is *insufficient* and replaces it with bidirectional — so even the "go bidirectional" refinement is taken, which makes B1's surviving wedge (H1: when does bidirectional beat unidirectional) narrower than the briefing's confident "individually defensible" implies. The bet is one stratification study away from being scooped by anyone who runs InCoM's ablation per-task-stratum.

**Finding 3 — "B3's framing is partially pre-empted (ESCAPE + SERF)." — 6/10.**
I verified the mechanical claim: ESCAPE (2604.13633) is genuinely ABSENT from the B3 Related table (lines 660-680) — confirmed. SERF (2606.12956) IS present (line 667) and conceded precisely as the briefing states (within-episode SE(3) transform-tracking, no episodic channel, single-room, map-not-co-trained). So the structural finding is correct and actionable. The confidence ceiling is the briefing's own characterization of ESCAPE: it asserts "ALFRED/modular, no dynamic purge, no multi-room sweep" but I have NOT seen the ESCAPE note or paper in this conversation, and that characterization is the load-bearing reason B3 survives pre-emption. If ESCAPE turns out to be in-policy-learned or to sweep multi-room extent, B3's wedge shrinks materially. This is a claim about an out-of-card paper asserted with more precision than the available evidence warrants — hence 6.

**Finding 4 — "The separability premise is the cluster's weakest load-bearing assumption." — 7/10.**
This is the briefing's best original contribution and it is correct in *direction*: the whole experimental program (H6 2×3 grids, slice-attribution) presumes the action / perception / memory slices are individually ablatable, and a monolithic policy threatens that. SPIN (2405.07991, lines 565, 580) and SG-VLA (2603.22760, line 511) are real in-card threats. BUT the briefing over-states the SG-VLA evidence: the card (line 511) reports SG-VLA single-head 0.73 on MS-HAB SetTable, which beats AC-DiT (0.556) but **loses to the strongest factored method InCoM (0.838)**. The briefing's "no-factoring single head beating factored AC-DiT" is literally true yet selectively framed — the strongest factored policy still wins, which actually *weakens* the "factoring is dispensable" threat the briefing recruits SG-VLA to make. SPIN is the stronger separability threat, but it lives in B2 (gaze) and is monolithic-RL-from-scratch, not a clean controlled A/B against a decomposed stack at matched data+compute — which is exactly experiment #1 the briefing then (correctly) demands. So the *finding* is sound but its two pieces of evidence are each partly mis-deployed. 7.

**Finding 5 — "Perception under real detectors, not action-factoring, may be the binding constraint." — 6/10.**
The HomeRobot 5-15%→0.4-0.6% collapse with a real DETIC detector (lines 506, 551, 588) is a striking, real datapoint and the card itself flags it as a Risk (line 551: "an action-coupling gain can be masked by the perception gap"). The claim that perception may be the *binding* constraint is plausible and the briefing is right that it threatens to mask the B1 result. I dock to 6 because (a) it rests on a single benchmark's detector-swap number from 2023-era HomeRobot, generalized to "the binding constraint" for the whole cluster — modern open-vocab detectors have moved substantially, so the 0.4-0.6% floor may be a dated artifact rather than a standing law; and (b) "may be" is doing heavy hedging work — the briefing does not establish that perception binds *after* B2's active gaze is applied, which is the entire point of B2. It is a well-aimed worry, not a verified constraint.

**Mean = 7.2. Min = 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

The least-confident claim is **Finding 3's ESCAPE characterization** (the specific assertion that ESCAPE 2604.13633 is "ALFRED/modular, not in-policy-learned, no dynamic purge, no multi-room sweep"). It is load-bearing: it is the *only* thing standing between B3 and being "additionally the most pre-empted" sub-axis. The briefing builds its de-risk step (4b) on this exact conjunction-of-misses.

**To verify:** pull the ESCAPE paper (2604.13633) and check four binary facts — (1) is its memory in-policy-learned or a modular map outside the policy? (2) does it handle relocated objects via a dynamic purge / re-ID, or a static map? (3) does it evaluate multi-room / cross-room extent, or single-scene? (4) is its task suite ALFRED-style symbolic or continuous-control mobile-manip? If even two of these come back the "wrong" way (e.g. in-policy + multi-room), B3's narrow wedge is gone and the cluster should drop to B1+B2 only. A secondary verification: confirm SG-VLA's 0.73 is on the *identical* MS-HAB SetTable split as InCoM's 0.838 (line 511 implies it is) — if so, Finding 4's framing must be corrected to "single head beats AC-DiT but not InCoM."

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The SKEPTIC over-dominated, with the ECONOMIST as co-driver.** The entire briefing collapses to one move: "the headline is settled, the real deliverable is a measurement/attribution contribution, the separability premise is the load-bearing risk." Sections 3 ("the hidden connection") and 5 ("the frontier question") both resolve to the *same* skeptic thesis — contribution-is-attribution-not-capability — which the briefing itself admits in Section 3 ("the SAME insight from three directions"). When three of five perspectives converge on one conclusion, that is not triangulation, it is a single voice wearing three hats. The PRACTITIONER appears only as a perception caveat (Finding 5) and the HISTORIAN only as a one-line aphorism ("the pendulum cannot un-swing a measurement"). Notably ABSENT as an independent check: any perspective arguing the *positive capability* case — that a decomposed stack could be a genuinely better-performing system, not merely a better-attributed one. The synthesis has talked itself into "this is a measurement paper" so thoroughly that it under-weights the possibility that B1+B2's coupling actually *wins* on hard tasks (BRS's 58% entire-task and InCoM's 83.8% are real capability gains, not just attribution fodder).

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The data-and-embodiment-economist (distinct from the cost economist already present): "what does the demonstration-collection and embodiment-transfer cost do to the whole program?"** Every experiment the briefing prescribes — the matched-data decomposed-vs-monolithic kill-shot, the 2×3 perception grid, the multi-room scaling sweep — assumes you can *collect demonstrations at multi-room scale on a real mobile manipulator*. That is the single hardest, most expensive constraint in this whole subfield, and it is invisible in the briefing. This 6th angle changes conclusions two ways: (1) it reframes Mobile-ALOHA's static co-training (B1-H4, line 533) and the sim benchmarks (MobileManiBench 150K trajectories, MS-HAB) from supporting-cast to *the actual bottleneck that decides which bet is even runnable* — if multi-room real demos are uncollectable, B3's flagship scaling-law sweep is a sim-only result and the "un-saturatable benchmark" (4c) is un-buildable on hardware; (2) it suggests the real flagship might be a *data-efficiency* claim (decomposition needs fewer demos to hit a coupling than monolithic scaling does) rather than the briefing's attribution claim — which is a more fundable, more falsifiable thesis. A briefing that prescribes "run at matched data+compute" without asking whether the data *exists* at the scale the hypotheses require has skipped the binding feasibility question.

---

##### 5. Overall grade — **B+**

A strong, intellectually honest briefing that correctly identifies the cluster has spent its first-order novelty and correctly relocates the contribution to attribution/measurement. The separability insight (Finding 4) is genuinely good and the de-risk ordering (kill-shot first, paper #1 not paper #3) is exactly the right instinct. It does not rubber-stamp — it concedes more than it claims, which is rare and valuable. It falls short of A-territory for four reasons, in priority order:

**Top fixes:**
1. **(Highest priority) Verify, don't assert, the ESCAPE characterization.** B3's entire survival rests on four unverified binary facts about an out-of-card paper. Pull 2604.13633 and confirm in-policy/modular, purge/static, multi-room/single, continuous/symbolic before committing B3 — and add it to the B3 table either way (the briefing already prescribes this in 4b; elevate it to a precondition, not a step).
2. **Correct the SG-VLA framing.** "Single head beating factored AC-DiT" omits that the strongest factored method (InCoM 0.838) still beats SG-VLA (0.73) on the same suite. As written it overstates the separability threat using the *weaker* factored baseline. State it as "SG-VLA beats AC-DiT but not InCoM," which sharpens rather than weakens the real argument (the threat is SPIN's monolithic scaling, not SG-VLA).
3. **Break the skeptic monoculture.** Sections 3 and 5 restate one thesis three times. Replace one of them with the positive-capability case (does the coupling *win* on hard tasks, independent of whether it's cleanly attributable?) so the synthesis triangulates instead of echoes.
4. **Surface the data-collection feasibility question.** The whole experimental program presumes multi-room real-robot demos are collectable. If they are not, B3's scaling sweep is sim-only and 4c's "shippable benchmark" is un-buildable on hardware. Make the demonstration-cost constraint explicit and let it re-order the bets.

A Stanford committee would commit this cluster — but only after experiment #1 (the matched-data kill-shot) and the ESCAPE verification land, exactly as the briefing's own Section 5 frontier question implies. The briefing earned its B+ by being honest about its own fragility; it loses the A- by asserting two out-of-card facts (ESCAPE's misses, SG-VLA "beating factored") with more confidence than the in-conversation evidence supports.

### B1 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that the base-arm coordination-structure margin (unidirectional base→arm / bidirectional / flat-concat) is *concentrated on reach-extension tasks where the base moves mid-grasp* and near-zero on fixed-base reaches, and that bidirectional beats unidirectional *only* when the arm action feeds back to constrain the base — i.e. isolating *when* each factoring matters rather than asserting one dominates.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys mobile-manip policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The base-first conditioning win is real and already shipped ([[2503.05652|BRS]]'s WB-VIMA, autoregressive base→torso→arm, 88% sub-task / near-zero safety violations on a real wheeled dual-arm R1). But the bet's stratification is a *measurement* contribution, not a *capability* one — on a real robot the dominant failure is perception ([[2306.11565|HomeRobot]]: 5–15%→0.4–0.6% with a real DETIC detector) and base-localization drift, not which arm-base factoring you picked.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: BRS's own ablation — swapping autoregressive decoding for a naive joint head collapses the 13×/21× margin over DP3/RGB-DP; the *order* carries the win, confirming the coupling is load-bearing in deployment. [[2509.16063|DSPv2]] independently reports 80% pick / 60% place real with bidirectional-autoregressive decoding.
- *Only-this-perspective*: H6 (latency) is the one that actually gates deployment. DSPv2 *self-reports* that BRS-style semi-autoregressive decoding "incurs prolonged hierarchical-autoregressive latency" — so the practitioner warns that the stratification experiment must be run at the *real-time chunk size*, or the whole margin is measured off-budget and irrelevant to a 60 Hz control loop.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card is honest that the headline claim is *settled* — [[2507.01961|AC-DiT]] ran the directional ablation (base→arm 49.5 vs arm→base 47.3), [[2602.23024|InCoM]] ran the head-to-head (83.8% vs AC-DiT 55.6% on ManiSkill-HAB SetTable), and [[2305.04866|Causal-WBMM]] established the "dependencies are causal not flat" prior in 2023 (93.6% vs 74.3% PPO). The genuinely-open question is the *conditional* structure: a stratified margin-vs-base-motion curve that no cited paper produces.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Every datapoint on the axis reports a single aggregate SR. InCoM asserts bidirectional > unidirectional uniformly; AC-DiT asserts directional conditioning helps uniformly; [[2603.22760|SG-VLA]] even shows its flow-matching head *raises* pick 0.13→0.27 while *decreasing* mobility tasks — direct evidence of a mobility-vs-precision tension (H2) that nobody has binned by base-travel.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The InCoM-over-AC-DiT comparison is confounded — InCoM bundles bidirectional coordination (DCFM) *with* a new perception module (IDPPM, which the ablation says is the *largest* gain at +19.5%). So the literature does NOT cleanly attribute InCoM's win to bidirectionality; H1 must control capacity *and* perception, or it inherits InCoM's confound.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The bet has retreated to a thin, possibly-uninteresting slice. The card already concedes base-first is settled, bidirectional is taken, and the prior is old (2023). What remains is "stratify the margin by base-travel" — which is an *analysis* paper, and the field is actively building the substrate for it ([[2606.18239|EBench]] already annotates an "operating mode: Mobile vs Fixed-but-Dexterous" axis). The whitespace is a benchmark slice someone else will fill incidentally.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: EBench (2606.18239, Jun 2026) decomposes generalist-policy SR along five axes including operating-mode, and reports a 29-point mobile-vs-dexterous gap for InternVLA-A1 — a *coarse binary* of exactly H2's stratification. The bet's distinctness now rests entirely on (a) *continuous* base-travel binning vs EBench's binary, and (b) crossing it with *factoring structure* rather than VLA backbone.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The falsifier may already be half-answered against the bet. If InCoM's bidirectional win is driven by its perception module (it is, per the ablation), then "bidirectional beats unidirectional" might be *flat or zero* once perception is matched — which the card lists as the falsifier that kills the conditioning-structure lever. The skeptic's bet is that H1 falsifies.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The whole sub-field is a benchmark-chasing race on ManiSkill-HAB SetTable (AC-DiT 55.6 → DSPv2 50.8 → InCoM 83.8 → SG-VLA 0.73), where each lab ships a new decoder + a new SOTA number every quarter. A stratification/diagnosis paper is *counter-cyclical*: it produces no new SOTA, so it is under-incentivized for the labs but well-positioned for an academic group that can't out-compute Shanghai AI Lab / PKU / CAS on raw SR.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The cited SOTA-chasers are all large industry-adjacent labs (BAAI/PKU for AC-DiT, CAS for InCoM, HKU/Astribot/Tsinghua for DSPv2, Shanghai AI Lab for EBench). The diagnostic gap exists *because* nobody is paid to slice their own win and risk showing it's conditional.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The same incentive that opens the whitespace also caps its citation ceiling — diagnosis papers (RoboEval, EBench) get cited as infrastructure, not as method. If the researcher wants impact, the bet must ship a *factoring recipe that adapts to base-travel* (a method), not just the stratified curve (a finding), or it lands as a workshop-tier analysis.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the end-to-end-vs-modular pendulum replaying on the nav↔manip seam. The pipeline (navigate-then-manipulate, [[2306.11565|HomeRobot]]) is the modular thesis; the joint policy (BRS/AC-DiT/InCoM) is the end-to-end antithesis; the bet's "which structure, when" is the *synthesis* — and historically the synthesis is where the durable result lives (cf. how model-based RL settled on "latent when the dynamics are predictable, model-free when not" rather than either-or).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2505.23692|Mobi-Pi]] is the modular thesis *steel-manned* — policy-aware base-pose selection that keeps a frozen manip policy in-distribution (70–100% real) — proving the pipeline is not strictly dominated, exactly as DR/sim2real history showed the "obsolete" approach surviving in a niche. The bet's H3 (freeze-base ablation) is the right way to find that niche.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The pendulum warning — "base-first everywhere" will overswing just as "end-to-end everywhere" did. The historian predicts the durable finding is a *task-conditional* factoring (base-first for reach-extension, arm-lead for fine in-place adjustment, per the card's own Risk row), and that whoever names the crossover wins the citation, not whoever ships the next SetTable SOTA.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on whitespace size*: Skeptic says EBench's operating-mode axis already coarse-bins H2 and the gap will be filled incidentally; Academic says no cited paper produces a *continuous* base-travel × factoring-structure curve, so the experiment is genuinely unrun. (Resolved partly below: EBench's axis is binary Mobile/Fixed and diagnoses *VLA backbones*, not factoring structures — so the clash favors Academic, narrowly.)
   - *Practitioner vs Academic on what's load-bearing*: Practitioner says perception/localization dominates and the factoring margin is second-order in deployment; Academic says the factoring margin is real and unmeasured. They collide on H3 — whether a base-freeze SR drop is a coupling effect or a perception artifact (the card's own Risk-3 concedes this).
   - *Skeptic vs Economist on viability*: Skeptic says it's a thin analysis slice; Economist agrees on the *finding* but says it's exploitable *precisely because* the SOTA-chasers won't run it — opposite conclusions from the same premise.
2. **Strongest vs weakest.** Strongest: **Academic** — it is the only perspective that pins a concrete, checkable confound (InCoM bundles bidirectional decoding with a +19.5% perception module, so the InCoM>AC-DiT margin is not cleanly attributable to bidirectionality), which directly threatens H1's falsifier. Weakest: **Historian** — the pendulum framing is illuminating but non-falsifiable and offers no metric the researcher can run tomorrow.
3. **Resolving question.** *When perception capacity is matched and held fixed, does InCoM-style bidirectional coordination still beat AC-DiT-style unidirectional base→arm — and if so, does that margin grow monotonically with required in-task base-travel?* This single controlled experiment resolves the Skeptic-vs-Academic clash (is there a real, attributable, conditional margin?) and the Practitioner-vs-Academic clash (is it coupling or perception?).
4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective accepts that base-first / structured factoring beats flat concatenation *on average* and that this is settled (BRS's ablation, AC-DiT's directional ablation, InCoM's head-to-head, Causal WBMM's 2023 prior all agree). The contribution can only live in the *conditional* refinement, not the main effect.
5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** No perspective addressed **morphology**: every cited datapoint is a *wheeled* base (R1, TidyBot++, Cobot Magic, Fetch, HSR). The base-velocity-as-manipulation-DoF coupling on a *legged/humanoid* whole body is qualitatively different — the base is itself a balance-constrained dynamical system, so "the base moves the workspace" becomes "the base moving the workspace is also a fall risk." The bet's stratification axis (base-travel) may not transfer from wheeled to legged, and the entire B1 card is implicitly wheeled despite living in a whole-body-humanoid document.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** The headline of B1 is already won by the field and the card admits it: base-first / structured base-arm factoring beats flat concatenation, confirmed independently by BRS, AC-DiT, InCoM, and DSPv2, with a causal prior from 2023. The surviving, genuinely-unrun bet is narrow but real — nobody has produced a *stratified* curve showing the coordination-structure margin concentrates on reach-extension (base-moves-mid-grasp) tasks and collapses on fixed-base reaches, nor isolated *when* bidirectional actually beats unidirectional. The novelty check confirms whitespace but narrows it: EBench (Jun 2026) now annotates a binary "Mobile vs Fixed-but-Dexterous" operating-mode axis and diagnoses VLA backbones along five capability dimensions, so the bet's distinctness now hinges entirely on doing *continuous* base-travel binning crossed with *factoring structure* (not VLA backbone) — and on surviving a real confound, since InCoM's bidirectional win is bundled with a perception module that is its single largest ablation gain. This is a credible *de-risk-and-commit-as-diagnosis* bet, not a commit-as-method bet, and it is one controlled experiment away from being validated or falsified.

**Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Base-first / structured factoring beats flat concatenation on average — settled.* (Supported by all five perspectives; challenged by none.) Evidence: BRS 13×/21× over DP3/RGB-DP collapses without autoregressive decoding; AC-DiT base→arm 49.5 > arm→base 47.3; Causal WBMM 93.6 vs 74.3. The card cannot bet here.
2. *The conditional stratification (margin vs in-task base-travel) is genuinely unrun in the cited literature.* (Supported by Academic, Historian, Economist; challenged by Skeptic.) Every datapoint reports one aggregate SR; SG-VLA's head trading pick-precision against mobility is uncontrolled evidence that the tension exists but is unbinned.
3. *The whitespace is narrowed but survives the novelty check.* (Supported by Academic; pressured by Skeptic.) EBench 2606.18239's operating-mode axis is a *binary* Mobile/Fixed flag over *VLA backbones*, not a continuous base-travel binning crossed with *factoring structures* — it supplies the substrate the bet needs without running the bet's experiment.
4. *H1 (bidirectional-beats-unidirectional-conditionally) carries a live confound and may falsify.* (Raised by Academic and Skeptic; under-weighted by the card.) InCoM's own ablation attributes +19.5% to its perception module (IDPPM), not its bidirectional decoder (DCFM) — so the InCoM>AC-DiT margin is not cleanly a bidirectionality margin. Match perception or the result is uninterpretable.
5. *Deployment value is perception- and latency-gated, not factoring-gated.* (Raised by Practitioner; tension with Academic.) HomeRobot's 5–15%→0.4–0.6% perception collapse and DSPv2's self-reported autoregressive-latency penalty mean the stratified margin must be measured with real perception and at real-time chunk size, or it doesn't predict real-robot behavior.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "diagnosis papers get cited as infrastructure, not method" and the Historian's "the synthesis result is the durable one" point at the *same* exit: the way to convert a low-ceiling diagnosis into a high-ceiling method is to ship an *adaptive* factoring controller that switches base-first / arm-lead / bidirectional *as a function of measured in-task base-travel demand* — the stratified curve becomes the *training signal* for a router, turning the finding into a recipe. That simultaneously answers the Practitioner (it adapts to the task instead of fixing base-first everywhere) and the Skeptic (it's a method, not just a slice).

**Actionable insight — DE-RISK, then COMMIT.** Do not commit to B1 as stated (it reads as a diagnosis with a falsifiable but confounded H1). De-risk with one cheap, decisive experiment *before* committing: take AC-DiT (unidirectional) and InCoM (bidirectional) or DSPv2, hold perception encoder and parameter count fixed, and run them on ManiSkill-HAB / MS-HAB tasks binned by *continuous* in-task base-travel distance (the parameterized Pick/Place receptacle distances make this binnable, and EBench/MobileManiBench give a controlled base-mobility substrate). Report the structured-over-flat and bidirectional-over-unidirectional margin *per base-travel bin*. Two outcomes: (a) the margin grows monotonically with base-travel and survives perception-matching → COMMIT, and immediately upgrade to the adaptive-factoring-router method to lift the ceiling; (b) the margin is flat or vanishes under matched perception → the card's own falsifier fires, DROP the conditioning-structure framing and pivot to the perception×factoring interaction (which Practitioner and SG-VLA both flag as the real tension). Also: explicitly scope the card to wheeled bases or add a legged/humanoid datapoint, because the entire axis is wheeled and the document is whole-body.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Is "in-task base-travel" even the right stratification variable — or is the true conditional lever the *direction of the kinematic disturbance* (does the arm's optimal action require a base correction the base hasn't committed yet)? If the margin tracks arm→base feedback *necessity* rather than base-travel *distance*, then H1 is the real bet and H2/H3 are proxies, and the experiment that measures disturbance-direction rather than travel-distance is the one that reframes the whole sub-field from "how much does the base move" to "who must yield to whom, when."

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Card under review: `Capability/Whole-Body.md` B1 (lines 473–553). All factual checks below were run against the KH notes and the card text, not from memory.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

1. **Base-first / structured factoring beats flat concatenation, settled — 9/10.**
   Four independent on-suite datapoints (BRS 13×/21× collapse without autoregressive decoding; AC-DiT base→arm 49.5 > arm→base 47.3; InCoM 83.8 > AC-DiT 55.6; Causal WBMM 93.6 vs 74.3) plus a 2023 causal prior. The direction is genuinely over-determined and no perspective contests it. Docked one point only because the AC-DiT 49.5/47.3 directional-ablation numbers are quoted in the card but I could not surface them in the AC-DiT KH note (`2507.01961.md`) on a direct grep — load-bearing for "settled-direction," sourced only through the card.

2. **Conditional stratification (margin vs in-task base-travel) is unrun — 7/10.**
   Correct that no cited paper bins a coordination-structure margin by *continuous in-task base-travel*. But "unrun" is doing two jobs: (a) literally not-yet-run, and (b) worth running. SG-VLA's single-head pick-vs-mobility trade is offered as evidence the tension exists, but it is single-head with no factoring, so it evidences a *perception/precision-vs-mobility* tension, not a *factoring-structure* tension — it is weaker support than the finding implies. The gap is real; its diagnostic payoff is the soft part.

3. **Whitespace narrowed but survives the novelty check — 5/10.**
   This is the finding I trust least, and the briefing oversells it. The KH note for EBench (`2606.18239`) shows it annotates **five** capability dimensions (scene, atomic skill, temporal horizon, **precision**, operating mode), not a lone "binary Mobile-vs-Fixed flag." The briefing's distinctness argument — "EBench is only a binary operating-mode flag, the bet is continuous base-travel binning" — understates EBench: its precision and atomic-skill axes already deliver fine-grained, non-binary diagnostic stratification on the exact mobile-manipulation suite. The surviving whitespace (continuous base-travel distance crossed with factoring structure rather than VLA backbone) is real but **thin**, and one EBench follow-up release that adds a travel-distance bin closes it. The briefing's own card (line 1282) concedes this by describing the remaining gap as merely "per-task in-task base-travel annotation on top of EBench."

4. **H1 carries a live confound and may falsify — 8/10.**
   Verified against the InCoM KH note (`2602.23024.md`, line 39): the IDPPM perception module is the **largest** ablation gain at **+19.5%**, while the DCFM bidirectional decoder removal is only ↓7.9. So the InCoM-over-AC-DiT margin is substantially a perception-architecture margin, not a clean bidirectionality margin. This is the single most credible and decision-relevant finding in the briefing, and the card under-weights it (line 498 mentions DCFM-removal ↓7.9 but never sets it beside the larger +19.5% IDPPM gain). Strong. Docked two points only because "may falsify H1" is a forecast, not a result.

5. **Deployment value is perception- and latency-gated — 6/10.**
   The HomeRobot 5–15%→0.4–0.6% perception collapse is solid and in-note. The DSPv2 latency leg is weaker: the DSPv2 KH note (`2509.16063.md`) confirms bidirectional autoregressive generation but I could **not** find the specific self-reported "prolonged hierarchical-autoregressive latency" penalty the card (line 512) and this finding lean on. The latency claim is asserted at card level, not visibly grounded at source. The perception half is an 8; the latency half is a 4.

**Mean = 7.0. Min = 5.**

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 3 (whitespace survives novelty check), at 5/10. The claim rests on characterizing EBench as a binary operating-mode flag, which the KH note contradicts — EBench is explicitly five-dimensional and already does fine-grained diagnostic profiling on mobile pick-and-place. **What would verify it:** read EBench's task table and confirm whether *any* of its five axes (especially precision or the mobile-pick-and-place subcategory) already correlates a structure/policy margin with base-travel demand, and confirm EBench does not bin by continuous base-displacement. If EBench's mobile-pick-and-place split is parameterized by receptacle distance, the surviving whitespace is essentially gone and B1 collapses from "one experiment from validation" to "a re-plot of an existing benchmark's results." This is a 30-minute check that should gate the whole card.

##### 3. Bias check — dominant perspective

The synthesis is **Academic-dominated**. Four of five findings are framed as novelty/falsifiability claims (is-it-unrun, does-the-whitespace-survive, is-the-confound-clean), which is the Academic's register. The Skeptic is present but only as a *challenger* inside findings 2–4, never as a primary author of a finding — and the Skeptic's strongest available move (EBench is more than a binary flag, so the whitespace is thinner than claimed) was not pushed to its conclusion. The Practitioner gets exactly one finding (5) and the deployment reality (perception + latency gating) is correctly flagged but then *re-absorbed* into the Academic experiment rather than allowed to challenge whether the bet is worth running at all. The Economist and Historian appear only in the "hidden connection" paragraph, where they conveniently align to license the router upsell. Net: the briefing reads as an Academic building a case for its own experiment, with the other four perspectives recruited as supporting witnesses rather than adversaries.

##### 4. Missing perspective — the controls/robotics-systems engineer

A 6th angle that would change the conclusion: a **classical whole-body-control / model-based engineer**. The entire B1 bet treats "should the base move mid-grasp and who conditions on whom" as an open *learning* question, but reactive whole-body control (whole-body operational-space control, QP-based redundancy resolution, MPC over base+arm) has answered the kinematic version of this for two decades: the base is a redundant DoF and the arm action is solved jointly subject to reachability. From that seat, the "base-as-manipulation-DoF" first principle is not a discovery — it is the standard whole-body-control assumption, and the real question is whether a *learned* autoregressive factoring buys anything over a model-based whole-body QP that already coordinates base and arm optimally. The card has no model-based-WBC baseline anywhere (HoMMI and HoMMI-style controllers appear but as perception/look-at rows, not as the factoring foil). Adding this perspective could flip the verdict: if a WBC QP matches the learned factoring on reach-extension tasks, then B1's margin is a property of the *control formulation*, not the *learned conditioning structure*, and the entire stratification experiment measures the wrong variable. This perspective also sharpens the frontier question (disturbance-direction / who-yields-to-whom) — that is literally the redundancy-resolution null-space priority question from classical WBC.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B.**

A well-instrumented, honest, internally-consistent card with one genuinely valuable verified finding (the InCoM +19.5%-IDPPM confound) and a clear, cheap, decisive de-risk experiment. It loses the A tier because the central novelty claim (finding 3) is overstated against its own source, the experiment is a *diagnosis* with a thin and shrinking whitespace, the entire axis is wheeled in a document titled "Whole-Body," and the most relevant adversary (model-based WBC) is absent.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Re-ground the EBench novelty claim.** Stop calling EBench a "binary operating-mode flag." It is five-dimensional. Re-state the surviving whitespace precisely as "continuous base-travel-distance binning crossed with factoring structure," and concede in the card that this is one benchmark-release away from closure. If EBench's mobile-pick-and-place split is already distance-parameterized, retire the conditional-stratification framing.
2. **Promote the InCoM confound into the bet, not a footnote.** Put the +19.5% IDPPM vs ↓7.9 DCFM contrast directly in the H1 prediction and make "match perception encoder + parameter count across AC-DiT/InCoM/DSPv2" a *precondition* of the experiment, not a risk bullet. As written, H1 is not cleanly testable without it.
3. **Add a model-based whole-body-control baseline.** Without a WBC-QP foil, a flat or learned margin cannot be attributed to the *learned* factoring rather than the *coordination formulation*. This is the missing-perspective fix and it is load-bearing.
4. **Scope the wheeled axis honestly or add a legged datapoint.** Every method anchor is wheeled/non-legged; the document is "Whole-Body." Either retitle the cluster to mobile (wheeled) manipulation or add a legged/humanoid loco-manipulation datapoint so the stratification claim is not silently scoped to wheeled bases.
5. **Source-check the DSPv2 latency self-report.** The H6 latency leg leans on a DSPv2 self-report I could not find in the KH note. Either quote the exact figure/page or downgrade H6 from a cited claim to an open question.

### B2 (Direction review)

Topic: B2 — Active-Perception Coupling in Mobile Manipulation. The bet (verbatim from First-principles framing): "A *learned* active-gaze policy that predicts a look-at point *conditioned on B1's chosen base trajectory* recovers the perception slice of HomeRobot's 5–15%→0.4–0.6% collapse with the gain largest exactly when the base is moving (near-zero stationary), matches Visibility-Aware Mobile Grasping's +18.0%-over-decoupled while beating its planner on demo tasks, and recovers dynamic-object relocations to DynaMem's 70% (vs 30% static, locate-failure 53.3%→6.7%) *for in-view relocations where gaze beats re-driving*."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys mobile-manip policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The card is right that a moving base loses the target out of frame, but on real hardware the dominant fix is already cheaper than a learned gaze loop: drive to a good base pose with a fixed camera, or bolt a wrist camera on. Adding a learned head-gaze action means another control loop running at perception rate, a calibrated head-eye transform, and latency between "decide where to look" and "image arrives", which is exactly where real deployments break.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: HoMMI (2603.03243) deliberately learns a *relaxed* 3D look-at point rather than 6-DoF head poses precisely because copying human head motion is kinematically infeasible on the RB-Y1, i.e. the practical head-DoF is messy. SPIN (2405.07991) only gets active vision to work by RL-then-distill-to-depth, a two-phase pipeline most labs can't run. Mobi-π (2505.23692) — Stanford/TRI — reaches 70–100% real task SR by *moving the base to fit a fixed camera*, no gaze action at all, on the same hardware family.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Before committing, measure the gaze-loop latency budget on your actual head, and run the cheapest baseline that everyone ships — Mobi-π-style base-repositioning with a frozen head — because if that recovers most of the HomeRobot collapse, your learned-gaze loop is solving a problem the hardware already solved by parking better.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The novelty is correctly *narrowed*, not invented: "active gaze beats fixed camera" is settled (ActiveUMI +44%, AVR +25%, Look-Focus-Act, EyeRobot), and "gaze coupled to a moving body" is now done by SPIN, HALOMI, and Emergent Active Perception (2505.12278). The genuinely un-run conjunction is the *separable look-at conditioned on the chosen base trajectory* plus the two attributions (H3 gaze-vs-exploration, H5 during-vs-between) — that decomposition, not the gaze itself, is the contribution.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Every existing moving-body row gets gaze as an *emergent by-product of a monolithic policy* (SPIN 90–96% hard-nav, HALOMI Bag-Transfer 90%→30% on ablation, Emergent-AP head motion) and none decomposes it. TAVIS (2605.07943) supplies the paired headcam-vs-fixedcam protocol (GR1T2 47.1% vs 38.8%) that makes the active-vs-passive axis a controlled variable — the literature has the *measurement* tool but not the *separability* claim.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The publishable unit is the attribution, so the paper lives or dies on H6's 2×3 GT/active/passive × autoregressive/flat grid being *additive and clean*; if the perception slice and action slice don't separate, you have a system paper competing with HoMMI/HALOMI on SR, which you will lose.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is a definitional artifact — it survives only by stacking three qualifiers ("learned" + "conditioned on base trajectory" + "decomposed"). Strip any one and it's pre-empted: HALOMI already learns gaze coupled to a moving humanoid body; SPIN already learns gaze while the same policy drives; Mobi-π already makes observability a base-controllable quantity. The "conditioned on *B1's chosen* base trajectory" hinge is an architectural detail, not a phenomenon.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own falsifier is the threat: "if independently-predicted gaze matches base-coupled gaze, the coupling is inert." Nothing in the cited evidence shows the coupling is *non-inert* — HALOMI couples gaze to the whole-body controller and works; it never tested whether decoupled gaze would have worked just as well. Mobi-π's result (human-driven base underperforms optimized base) shows the *base*, not the gaze, is the lever that moves SR.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Run H2's falsifier first and cheaply — independent-head vs base-coupled-gaze on the same B1 policy. If the gap is small, the entire direction collapses to "active gaze on a mobile base," which HALOMI/SPIN already own; do this before any benchmark engineering.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction is shaped by the data-cost economics of robot-free human demonstration (UMI/HoMMI/HALOMI lineage) — the active-neck capture rig is cheap relative to teleop, so the field is racing to harvest "free" active-perception labels from human ego-video, which incentivizes *more capture systems* over *attribution studies*. A decomposition paper has low citation-velocity versus a new SOTA SR number, so the incentive gradient pushes against exactly the contribution that makes B2 novel.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's anchor cluster (HoMMI, HALOMI, ActiveUMI, ActiveGlasses, EgoMI) is dominated by capture-interface papers from Stanford/TRI/Berkeley with industry money; benchmarks (TAVIS, HomeRobot, BiGym) are academic and under-cited by comparison. SaPaVe (2603.12193) needed a 200K-sample dataset (ActiveViewPose-200K) to make active perception work in a VLA — the compute/data moat favors labs that can fund capture.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Your defensible asset is the *benchmark protocol* (the GT/active/passive triad on TAVIS+HomeRobot), not the policy — an academic without TRI's capture rig wins by owning the measurement everyone else needs, the way TAVIS is positioning itself.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the active-vision / "animate vision" debate (Bajcsy, Ballard, late 1980s) returning with learned policies — the recurring pattern is end-to-end (gaze emerges from one policy) vs modular (gaze as a separable, conditioned module), and the field swings between them every cycle. B2 is explicitly a *modular* bet (separable look-at conditioned on base) made at the peak of an end-to-end wave (SPIN, monolithic VLAs).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: SPIN and Emergent-AP (2505.12278) are the current end-to-end pole (gaze emergent from a monolithic RL policy); SaPaVe and TVVE explicitly *decouple* camera and manipulation decoders to fix unified-action-space interference — the pendulum already started swinging back toward modular within this very table. Historically the modular side wins on *interpretability/attribution* and loses on *peak SR*, which is exactly the trade B2 is choosing.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Bet on the modular/attribution framing only if you believe the end-to-end SR wall is near; if monolithic VLAs keep climbing, your separable look-at becomes a legacy ablation, so tie the contribution to a *durable* artifact (the decomposition method, the benchmark) that survives the next end-to-end surge.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Practitioner vs Academic on what to build*: Practitioner says park the base better with a fixed camera (Mobi-π) and the gaze loop may be unnecessary overhead; Academic says the gaze loop is fine, the real contribution is the *attribution* (H6 separability), so SR-vs-Mobi-π is the wrong fight. Specific clash: is the deliverable a better policy or a better measurement?
   - *Skeptic vs Card (H2)*: Skeptic claims base-coupled gaze ≈ independent gaze (coupling is inert, HALOMI already works without isolating it); the card stakes the bet on the coupling being non-inert. They cannot both be right — this is the load-bearing contradiction.
   - *Historian vs Economist on durability*: Historian says modular/attribution survives the next wave; Economist says the incentive gradient (citation velocity, capture-data moats) actively punishes the attribution paper. One says the framing is durable, the other says it's economically disfavored.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only perspective that correctly identifies the *unit of novelty* (the decomposition, not the gaze) and grounds it in the one tool the card actually has (TAVIS's paired protocol). It survives the Skeptic's attack because it agrees the SR fight is unwinnable and relocates the contribution.
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — directionally true (active-vision pendulum) but offers no falsifiable next action and its "bet on modular if the SR wall is near" is unfalsifiable on the timescale of one paper.

3. **The one resolving question.** Does base-coupled gaze beat independently-predicted gaze on the *same* B1 policy and the *same* tasks (H2's falsifier), with the gap concentrated in base-motion phases? This single experiment resolves the Skeptic-vs-card contradiction and tells the Practitioner whether the extra loop earns its cost.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).** Active gaze coupled to a *moving* body beats a fixed forward camera on observation-limited mobile-manip tasks — this is consensus (SPIN <26%→90–96%, HALOMI 90%→30% ablation, ActiveUMI +44%, TAVIS +45pp on conditional-pick). The disagreement is only about whether *B2's specific conditioning/decomposition* adds anything on top.

5. **The blind spot none addressed.** Sim-to-real transfer of the gaze *policy itself*. Every cited active-gaze success is either real-with-its-own-rig (HoMMI, HALOMI) or sim (SPIN's 90–96% is simulation; TAVIS is IsaacLab). No perspective interrogated whether a look-at policy trained in sim survives real head-eye latency, motion blur from a moving base, and rolling-shutter — the failure mode that historically kills active-vision systems and that no card hypothesis (H1–H6) tests.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** B2 bets that making gaze a *learned, separable look-at action conditioned on the chosen base trajectory* — and decomposing its contribution from the base/action coupling — is open whitespace in mobile manipulation. The bet is real but narrow: "active gaze on a moving body" is already done (SPIN, HALOMI, Emergent Active Perception), so the entire novelty rests on two un-run claims — that the base-conditioning is *non-inert* (H2) and that the perception slice is *separable and additive* (H6). The literature supplies the measurement tools (TAVIS's paired headcam protocol, HomeRobot's GT-vs-real floor) but not the result, and a strong new competitor — Mobi-π (Stanford/TRI) — reframes the same observability problem as base-pose-selection with a *frozen* camera, threatening the premise that gaze must be the controlled DoF. Commit only after a cheap falsifier shows the coupling is real; otherwise this collapses into a crowded "active gaze beats fixed camera" lane.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Active gaze coupled to a moving body beats a fixed camera* — highest reliability. Supported by all five perspectives and hard numbers (SPIN <26%→90–96% sim, HALOMI 90%→30% disable-ablation, TAVIS +45pp conditional-pick, ActiveUMI +44%). Challenged by none. This is the floor the field stands on, and it is *not* B2's novel claim.
2. *The novel unit is the decomposition (H3/H6), not the gaze* — high reliability. Supported by Academic and Historian; consistent with the card's own framing. Challenged by Economist (low citation-velocity) and partly Practitioner (who wants a deployable policy, not an attribution).
3. *B2's whitespace survives a literature check, but barely* — medium-high. Supported by the alphaxiv search: no paper lands the learned-look-at-conditioned-on-chosen-base-trajectory + gaze-vs-exploration + during-vs-between conjunction (Gaze2Act/Gaze-Regularized-VLA are fixed-base human-gaze-input; NavWAM is nav-only; SPIN/HALOMI/Emergent-AP are monolithic). Challenged by Skeptic: each qualifier individually is pre-empted.
4. *The coupling may be inert (H2 risk)* — medium. Supported by Skeptic and the card's own falsifier; Mobi-π's result that the *base* is the SR lever (human-driven base underperforms optimized base) reinforces it. Challenged by HALOMI's evidence that *some* coupling matters — though HALOMI never isolated it.
5. *Mobi-π is the strongest competing framing* — medium. Supported by Practitioner and Economist (it's the cheap, fundable, frozen-camera path). Challenged by Academic/card: Mobi-π is single-shot base-pose-selection, open-loop within a manipulation episode, no in-task re-observation of moved objects — so it does not preempt the dynamic/closed-loop slice (H3/DynaMem).

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "just park the base better" (Mobi-π) and the Skeptic's "the coupling is inert" are the *same claim* from two directions: both assert that the *base* is the controllable observability lever and *gaze* is redundant given good base control. If true, B2's H2 fails and Mobi-π eats the direction. The card's only escape hatch is the *dynamic in-view relocation* case (H3): when an object moves *within* the current frustum, re-driving (Mobi-π's only tool) is wasteful and gaze is strictly cheaper — that is the one regime where neither the Practitioner's nor the Skeptic's claim holds.

**(4) Actionable insight — DE-RISK, then COMMIT.** Do not commit blind and do not drop. The direction is novel but the novelty is concentrated in two falsifiers that are cheap to run first. Concrete next experiment (the de-risking gate): on a single B1 autoregressive policy, run the H2 contrast — base-coupled gaze vs independently-predicted gaze vs frozen head — and *stratify target-in-frame rate and SR by base-motion phase*. Add a frozen-head + Mobi-π-style base-repositioning arm as the practitioner baseline. Decision rule: COMMIT only if (a) base-coupled gaze beats independent gaze with the gap concentrated in base-motion phases (H2 non-inert), AND (b) the gain over the Mobi-π baseline is concentrated in *dynamic in-view* relocations (H3). If (a) fails, DROP to a TAVIS-protocol benchmark/attribution paper (the Economist's defensible asset). Throughout, own the GT/active/passive triad (TAVIS + HomeRobot) as the durable artifact the Historian says survives the next end-to-end wave.

**(5) Frontier question.** Is observability best controlled at the *gaze* DoF or the *base* DoF — i.e., for a given task distribution, does optimizing where-to-look or where-to-stand recover more of the HomeRobot perception collapse per unit of control cost? Answering this for the dynamic, closed-loop, in-task-relocation regime (not Mobi-π's single-shot static parking) would settle whether active gaze is a first-class mobile-manipulation primitive or a special case of policy-aware navigation — and would reframe the entire B-cluster.
