### C1 (Direction review)

Topic: a humanoid whole-body policy that *estimates an unknown external hand wrench* then *learns to forward-model* its whole-body reaction (a learned residual through $J_{\text{ext}}^{\top}$ feeding pre-emptive leg compensation), so it holds balance under a step-applied load with a measurable advantage in the first ~100 ms after onset where reactive compensation lags, extending ALMA's anticipatory mechanism from self-induced-known to unknown-external on a tight support polygon.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys humanoid policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The 100 ms anticipatory window is real physics but operationally fragile: a humanoid running a policy at 50–100 Hz has 1–2 control ticks inside that window, and the forward model's value is gated by how fast and how accurately you can *estimate the unknown wrench in the first place*. If the estimator (proprioceptive momentum observer) already lags 30–70 ms, the "anticipation" budget is mostly consumed before the predictor fires.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: WT-UMI (2606.13232) measures force directly and *still* reports a **68 ms** lag at 1.05 N RMSE; ADAPT (2606.16542) only "captures onset and relative magnitude" of injected disturbances on real hardware, not a calibrated wrench. So the upstream estimate the whole bet rests on is itself a 30–70 ms-lagging, noisy signal on real G1-class hardware.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Build the H1 ablation around a *latency budget table* (estimator lag + inference lag + actuation lag vs the 100 ms window), not just a CoM-excursion number. If the summed pipeline latency eats the window, the headline metric is unwinnable on hardware regardless of how good the forward model is. Also: step-applied unknown loads need a repeatable real apparatus (drop-rig / quick-release cart) or the "step" is a sim artifact.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's framing of the gap is precise and largely correct: ALMA (2201.03871) does prove anticipatory-feedforward beats reactive (208%), but only for the *self-induced known* arm wrench on a *quadruped* with a *model-based* predictor. The novel delta (unknown-external + learned-forward-model + humanoid support polygon) is genuinely unoccupied by the cited set.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Every humanoid force paper in the table is reactive: FALCON (2505.06776) implicit-curriculum (0.37 vs 0.60), SplitAdapter (2606.03297) history-factorized (26/27), ADAPT (2606.16542) estimate-as-observation, Thor (2510.26280) reward-shaped 167.7 N, WT-UMI (2606.13232) sensed-then-admittance, Unified Force-Position (2505.20829) estimate-then-impedance. None forward-models the reaction the *next* action induces. RAMBO (2504.06662, alphaxiv) is model-WBC + *reactive* RL correction on Go2, virtual forces in training but no wrench estimation/anticipation.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The contribution must be framed as a *mechanism transplant* (ALMA's feedforward, generalized) not a new capability, and the falsifier the card already wrote (H1: anticipation == reactive at matched step load → redundant) is the exact ablation reviewers will demand. Pre-register it. Also flag: ALMA's 208% is on a quadruped's wide support polygon; the humanoid's narrow polygon may *increase* the anticipatory payoff (the bet's upside) or break it (estimator noise dominates), and the literature cannot tell you which.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already absorbed).**
- *Core position*: "Reactive may already suffice" is not a footnote, it is the likely outcome. FALCON's curriculum and SplitAdapter's context already win under load *without* any explicit force term, and a fast-enough reactive estimate-then-compensate loop (ADAPT, Unified Force-Position) may close the 100 ms gap once tuned, making the forward model a wash.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card itself concedes (Risks, H4) that FALCON reaches its gains with *no sensing* and Sim-to-Real Door (2512.01061) hits 83% on rigid doors *RGB-only, no force channel*. If randomization breadth and implicit curricula already cover the deployable load envelope, the anticipatory term is solving a problem the field has been routing around. ALMA's 208% is also against "reactive *or naive*" baselines: a *well-tuned* reactive humanoid controller is the honest comparator, not a naive one.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The whitespace is real but *thin*: it is a single mechanism (feedforward) crossed with two scope-extensions (unknown-external, humanoid). The risk is a "negative-or-marginal result" paper, valuable scientifically but weak as a publication bet. Demand a power analysis: how large must the first-100 ms CoM-excursion gap be to clear measurement noise on real hardware, and is that effect size physically plausible given a 30–70 ms estimator lag?

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The incentive gradient pushes toward *high-load demos* (113 N carts, 70 kg pulls, fire doors) because they make spectacular videos and map to logistics/industrial buyers, not toward a subtle 100 ms transient-balance metric. The bet's headline metric is *unsexy by design*, which is good science but bad for citation/funding velocity.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The cluster's loudest numbers are load magnitudes (Thor 167.7 N / 70 kg cart, UAN 113 N / 8 kg, FALCON 100 N) and full-task SR (SplitAdapter 26/27). Vendors (Unitree G1, Booster T1) and labs are racing the load-ceiling and benchmark-SR axes (HumanoidBench 2403.10506). A transient-balance-advantage metric has no existing leaderboard, so it competes for attention against saturating SR benchmarks.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Co-report a *load-ceiling* number (UAN-class 113 N) alongside the 100 ms metric, so the paper sits on the axis funders/vendors already reward *and* makes the novel scientific point. Owning a new metric (sudden-onset CoM excursion) is a long-term moat but needs a benchmark others adopt — propose it as a protocol (Digit impulse-perturbation style, already noted in vault memory) or it dies as a one-off.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the model-based-vs-learned and feedforward-vs-feedback pendulum replaying on humanoids. ALMA (2022) was the feedforward-wrench peak of the *quadruped* MPC+RL wave; the field then swung hard to end-to-end reactive RL (FALCON, Thor) because it deployed more robustly. The bet proposes swinging the feedforward idea *back* in, now as a *learned* residual rather than model-based.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The lineage is explicit in the citations: ALMA's model-based wrench predictor (2201.03871) → RAMBO's model-WBC+RL hybrid (2504.06662) → the bet's learned-forward-model-over-nominal-analytic residual. This is the same "keep the physics core, learn the residual" pattern that won in actuator nets (UAN 2502.10894) and disturbance observers (ADAPT 2606.16542).
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Every prior feedforward-anticipation win got eroded once reactive methods got fast/robust enough (the Dreamer-vs-model-free, pixel-vs-latent cycle). The durable version of this bet is *not* "anticipation beats reactive" (which reactive will eventually match) but "anticipation is the cheap residual over a nominal model that buys the first 100 ms while reactive catches up" — frame it as a hybrid, not a replacement, exactly as the Risks section already hedges (residual over nominal model, keep the curriculum).

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on whether whitespace is load-bearing*: Academic says the unknown-external + learned-forward-model + humanoid triple is unoccupied (true by citation). Skeptic says it is *thin* and likely yields a marginal/negative result because reactive (FALCON no-sensing, Door RGB-only) already covers the deployable envelope. Same facts, opposite verdicts on *value*.
   - *Practitioner vs the card's headline on the 100 ms metric*: The card stakes the headline on a first-100 ms CoM-excursion advantage; the Practitioner argues estimator lag (WT-UMI 68 ms even when *directly sensed*; ADAPT onset-only) consumes most of that window, so the very metric may be unmeasurable as designed on real hardware.
   - *Economist vs Academic on framing*: Academic wants the subtle transient metric as the contribution; Economist warns that metric has no leaderboard and loses the attention race to load-ceiling/SR numbers, urging a co-reported high-load number.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — its whitespace claim is directly verifiable against the table and the alphaxiv search (no cited or discovered paper forward-models an unknown-external wrench reaction on a humanoid), and it correctly identifies the contribution as a mechanism-transplant with a pre-registered falsifier.
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — directionally right about incentives but least decision-relevant: it shapes *framing/marketing*, not whether the bet's physics holds. Its advice (co-report a load number) is cheap insurance, not a go/no-go.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction (Skeptic vs Academic on value).**
   > On a real humanoid, at matched step-load magnitude, does a *well-tuned reactive* estimate-then-compensate controller (ADAPT/Unified-Force-Position class) leave a CoM-excursion gap in the first ~100 ms that is (a) statistically distinguishable from sensor/actuation noise and (b) large enough that a learned forward model — fed by a 30–70 ms-lagging wrench estimate — can actually close it? If no, the whitespace is real but empty.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   - The *novelty claim is factually intact*: no cited or alphaxiv-surfaced paper estimates an *unknown external* wrench and *forward-models* its whole-body reaction on a *humanoid*. All five accept the gap exists; they disagree only on whether filling it produces a strong result.
   - The bet must be framed as a *learned residual over a nominal analytic model* (hybrid), not a from-scratch forward model — Practitioner, Historian, and the card's own Risks all converge here, and UAN/ADAPT precedent supports it.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by NONE).**
   - *What happens when the wrench estimate is wrong in direction, not just magnitude.* Every perspective debated lag and effect size; none addressed the *failure mode of mispredicted anticipation*: a feedforward leg pre-compensation against a wrongly-estimated wrench actively *destabilizes* (you lean into a push that isn't there). Reactive control degrades gracefully toward "do nothing"; anticipatory control can degrade *catastrophically* toward "fall the wrong way." The safety asymmetry between reactive and anticipatory failure modes is the unexamined core risk, and it is exactly what C2's certified filter is supposed to bound — but the C1 card never quantifies the mis-prediction downside.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** The C1 bet — anticipate an unknown external hand wrench and forward-model its whole-body reaction on a humanoid — sits on *genuine, citation-verified whitespace*: ALMA (2201.03871) proved anticipatory-feedforward beats reactive (208%) but only for the self-induced *known* wrench on a quadruped, and every humanoid force paper in the field (FALCON, SplitAdapter, Thor, ADAPT, WT-UMI, Unified-Force-Position) is reactive. The bet is therefore a clean *mechanism-transplant* with a pre-written falsifier, which is its strength. But it is also *thin and high-variance*: the contribution is one mechanism crossed with two scope-extensions, the headline 100 ms transient metric is threatened by a 30–70 ms estimator lag that exists even with direct tactile sensing, and reactive methods already cover the deployable load envelope with no force term at all. This is a DE-RISK, not a clean COMMIT: worth doing only if a cheap pilot shows the first-100 ms CoM-excursion gap is both real on hardware and closable despite estimator lag, and only if framed as a learned residual over a nominal model (hybrid) co-reported with a load-ceiling number.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The novelty is intact (highest reliability).* No cited paper, and none surfaced by the alphaxiv discover search (RAMBO, Safe-WBC 2603.02443, Terrain-Aware 2605.31343, HAIC 2602.11758, Bracing-for-Impact 2505.11495), forward-models an *unknown external* wrench reaction on a humanoid. Supported by ACADEMIC, accepted by all; only ECONOMIST adds that novelty alone does not win attention.
2. *The closest threat is ALMA, and it does not pre-empt (high).* ALMA is anticipatory-feedforward but explicitly self-induced-known, quadruped, model-based. Supported by ACADEMIC/HISTORIAN; no perspective contests it.
3. *Reactive may already suffice over the deployable envelope (high).* FALCON 0.37 with no sensing, Door 83% RGB-only. Supported by SKEPTIC, conceded by the card's own H4/Risks; challenged only in degree by ACADEMIC (the *transient* regime is where reactive provably lags per ALMA).
4. *The headline 100 ms metric is latency-threatened (medium-high).* WT-UMI 68 ms lag even when directly sensed; ADAPT onset-only on hardware. Raised by PRACTITIONER; not contradicted, but ACADEMIC notes the humanoid's narrow support polygon may enlarge the payoff.
5. *Anticipatory mis-prediction has a catastrophic-failure asymmetry (medium, and unexamined).* Surfaced as the blind spot; no card text or perspective quantifies the downside of leaning into a wrongly-estimated wrench. Lower reliability because it is an inference, not yet evidenced.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's latency budget and the Historian's pendulum are the *same* constraint viewed from two angles: the reason every prior feedforward-anticipation win eroded is that reactive methods got *fast enough* to collapse the latency window the feedforward term was exploiting. So the bet's durability is not "does anticipation beat reactive today" but "is there a *floor* on reactive latency (estimator + actuation) below which a learned forward-model residual is permanently cheaper than closing the loop faster." If that floor exists on humanoid hardware, the bet is durable; if reactive can be tuned under it, history says the advantage evaporates — which is exactly the Skeptic's resolving question.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher (DE-RISK, with concrete next experiment).** Do not COMMIT blind and do not DROP. DE-RISK with a *sim-first kill-test before any hardware*: implement the H1 head-to-head in sim only — (a) FALCON-class reactive curriculum, (b) ADAPT-class reactive estimate-then-compensate, (c) the bet's learned-forward-model residual over a nominal analytic model — apply matched *unknown* step-load impulses at the EE, and inject a realistic estimator lag (sweep 0 → 70 ms) and direction-error noise into the wrench estimate. The single decisive plot: first-100 ms CoM-excursion gap (c vs best of a/b) *as a function of estimator lag*. Kill criteria: if the gap closes to noise at >40 ms lag (achievable on real G1-class hardware), or if direction-error injection flips the anticipatory policy to *worse-than-reactive* (the blind-spot failure mode), the bet does not survive contact with reality → keep the curriculum, drop the forward model. If the gap survives realistic lag *and* degrades gracefully under direction error, COMMIT and move to a quick-release real-load rig, framing the result as a hybrid (learned residual over nominal model) co-reported with a UAN-class 113 N load-ceiling number.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is there a hardware-imposed *reactive-latency floor* on humanoid force response (proprioceptive estimation + actuation bandwidth) below which feedforward anticipation is permanently the cheaper way to buy the first 100 ms — and can that floor be characterized as a *physical constant of the platform* rather than a tuning artifact? If yes, anticipatory whole-body force control becomes a durable sub-field with its own benchmark; if the floor is just "reactive isn't tuned yet," the entire bet is a transient that the next reactive paper erases.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded against the C1 card (Whole-Body.md L726-806) and the KH notes for 2201.03871 (ALMA), 2606.13232 (WT-UMI), 2606.16542 (ADAPT), plus a citation check on ALMA's identity and figures.*

**Verification done before reviewing.** ALMA (2201.03871) confirmed as Ma et al., RA-L 2022, "Combining Learning-based Locomotion Policy with Model-based Manipulation" — the 208% figure is real and is over "reactive or naive controllers" against a 150 N lateral push on the ALMA quadruped. WT-UMI's "68 ms" is confirmed as the **force-prediction-head temporal lag on human-demonstration data** (RMSE 1.05 N, 68 ms lag), *not* a deployed sensing-to-actuation latency. ADAPT confirmed as onset-and-relative-magnitude-only on real hardware (absolute magnitude only in sim), at 60 N / 4 kg, genuinely below FALCON/UAN load.

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

1. **Novelty is intact — 8/10.** Verified: no cited or discover-surfaced paper forward-models an *unknown external* wrench reaction on a humanoid. But the delta is narrower than the card's "clean mechanism-transplant" implies. ALMA already (a) feeds a *predicted wrench sequence as an anticipatory observation* to the leg policy and (b) trains with *unobserved* disturbances for robustness. So the genuine whitespace is the conjunction "unknown-external ∧ humanoid-support-polygon," not "anticipation itself." Real, but one notch less pristine than billed.

2. **ALMA does not pre-empt — 8/10.** Solid and uncontested. Self-induced-known, quadruped, model-based predictor — all confirmed in the KH note. The one caveat (above) is that the mechanistic distance to C1 is smaller than the prose dramatizes; it does not pre-empt, but it is a closer cousin than "transplant" suggests.

3. **Reactive may already suffice over the deployable envelope — 7/10.** The strongest skeptical point and the one the card honestly concedes (H4/Risks). FALCON 0.37 no-sensing and Door 83% RGB-only are real. The reason it is not higher: these are *steady-state / sustained-load* numbers; none of them measures the *first-100 ms transient* C1 actually claims, so "suffices" is asserted on the wrong regime. The claim is directionally right but evidentially under-matched to the bet's actual target metric.

4. **Headline 100 ms metric is latency-threatened — 5/10.** Directionally correct but built on a **misread number**. "WT-UMI 68 ms even when directly sensed" is used as evidence of an estimator-latency *floor*, but 68 ms is a learned prediction-head lag on demo data, not a hardware estimation-to-actuation latency. ADAPT's onset-only-on-hardware is the better evidence and survives. The latency threat is real (any estimate-then-act loop has lag), but the specific quantitative anchor the briefing leans on does not measure the quantity it is invoked for. Lower confidence than the briefing's "medium-high."

5. **Catastrophic mis-prediction asymmetry — 6/10.** Correctly self-labeled as inference, not evidence, which is intellectually honest. It is also the most decision-relevant unexamined risk: leaning into a wrongly-estimated wrench on a small support polygon is qualitatively worse than failing to lean (a reactive policy degrades to "stiff," an anticipatory one degrades to "actively wrong"). The 6 reflects that it is sound reasoning but entirely unquantified, and the proposed kill-test (direction-error injection) is the right instrument.

**Mean = 6.8. Lowest = 5 (finding 4).**

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 4 (the 100 ms latency threat as anchored on "WT-UMI 68 ms"). **What would verify it:** a single hardware measurement of the *actual* proprioceptive-wrench-estimate-to-leg-torque latency on a G1-class humanoid (momentum-observer settling time + policy inference + actuator bandwidth), reported as a distribution, not a point. ADAPT's real-hardware observer is the natural vehicle. Until that number exists, "100 ms transient advantage threatened by 30-70 ms lag" is an argument about two numbers measured in different frames (demo-prediction-head vs deployed-control-loop). The frontier question's "physical constant of the platform" hinges entirely on this measurement existing and being characterizable — which, to its credit, the DE-RISK plan's lag-sweep is designed to produce.

##### 3. Bias check

**ACADEMIC over-dominated, with PRACTITIONER as runner-up.** The synthesis is anchored on novelty-defense (findings 1-2 = 16 of the confidence mass) and on the ALMA mechanism-transplant narrative, which is an academic-taste framing. SKEPTIC is present and load-bearing (finding 3) but is repeatedly *answered* by ACADEMIC ("the transient regime is where reactive provably lags per ALMA") rather than left to bite — the skeptic is quoted, then rebutted, in the same breath. The genuinely balanced move (the hidden-connection in §3, unifying Practitioner-latency and Historian-pendulum) is the best part of the briefing and shows ECONOMIST/HISTORIAN were consulted. ECONOMIST is thin: "novelty alone does not win attention" appears once and never costs the bet anything. Net: **ACADEMIC-leaning**, not balanced.

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The HARDWARE / CONTROL-ENGINEER perspective** — the bandwidth and actuator dynamics of the platform itself. The entire bet reduces to "can a feedforward term buy the first 100 ms," yet nobody asks the prior question: on a G1-class humanoid, is the *actuator* the bottleneck, not the estimator? If series-elastic / current-loop bandwidth caps torque rise-time at, say, 50-80 ms regardless of how early the policy commands it, then a perfect feedforward prediction *cannot be physically realized in the first 100 ms* and the bet is dead on arrival for reasons unrelated to estimation or learning. This sixth angle would change the conclusion: it could move the verdict from "DE-RISK in sim" to "measure actuator step-response on real G1 FIRST, before any sim work," because a hardware bandwidth ceiling would make the sim kill-test irrelevant (sim has idealized actuators). The DE-RISK plan injects *estimator* lag but not *actuator* rise-time limits — a real gap.

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.** This is a well-constructed, intellectually honest briefing: the falsifier is pre-written, the topThreat (ALMA) is named and correctly characterized, the verdict is appropriately a DE-RISK rather than a COMMIT, and §3's cross-perspective synthesis is genuinely insightful. It loses the A-range on three things: (1) a load-bearing number is misframed, (2) the actual bet metric (first-100 ms transient) is never matched by the evidence used to question it (steady-state numbers), and (3) the most decisive physical question — actuator bandwidth — is absent.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Re-anchor the latency claim.** Drop "WT-UMI 68 ms" as the estimator-floor evidence; it is a demo-prediction-head lag, not a control-loop latency. Replace with a real G1-class momentum-observer settling-time + actuator-bandwidth measurement, reported as a distribution. This is the single most important fix — the entire frontier question depends on it.
2. **Add actuator rise-time to the kill-test.** The DE-RISK sweep injects estimator lag and direction-error noise but assumes ideal actuators. Add an actuator-bandwidth cap (sweep torque rise-time 0→80 ms) — if a perfect feedforward command cannot be realized inside 100 ms, kill before hardware.
3. **Match evidence to the claimed regime.** Stop using FALCON 0.37 / Door 83% (sustained-load, steady-state) to argue "reactive suffices" against a bet that lives in the *transient*. Either find a transient-regime reactive number or state explicitly that no prior work has measured the regime C1 targets — which would *strengthen* the whitespace claim while being more honest about finding 3.
4. **Quantify the asymmetry (finding 5) before COMMIT, not after.** A wrongly-estimated wrench leaned-into on a tight polygon is the catastrophic mode; make the direction-error kill-criterion the *first* gate, not a co-equal one.

### C2 (Direction review)

Topic: a *wrench-aware certified safety layer* for whole-body loco-manipulation — a barrier whose safe set carries the external-payload equilibrium term — that (a) holds collision-free single-leg balance under unknown payload where kinematics-only barriers ([[2505.11494|SHIELD-Humanoid]], [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]) violate the support polygon, and (b) in a certified-vs-reward head-to-head *under load* lifts OOD survival to [[2604.07457|CMP]]'s 86.7%/93.3% real where an equal-capacity reward-penalty / robustness-by-training policy ([[2503.00923|HWC]]-class) cannot.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys robot policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet is operationally sound exactly where it matters — a fall under load is unrecoverable and uninsurable, so a per-step O(1) check that bounds the hard constraint is worth the latency cost. But the binding reality is *force estimation*, not the barrier: a wrench-aware safe set is only as good as the wrench you can measure online, and on most humanoids there is no wrist F/T sensor.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2604.07457|CMP]] runs at 2.99 ms and [[2602.01515|RAPT]] at 1.63 ms — both inside a 50 Hz–1 kHz loop, so the per-step-check feasibility (H5) is already demonstrated, not speculative. [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]] holds collision-free under 20% mass mismatch on a *real* TOCABI, proving the model-mismatch tolerance the practitioner needs.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: FAME ([[2603.08961]]) already estimates the hand wrench *sensor-free* from joint torques + Jacobian (`F_ext = -(J^T)^† (τ − τ_g)`). Your wrench-aware barrier inherits that estimator's noise floor — so the real experiment is not "barrier vs reward," it is "does the barrier still certify when the wrench input is a noisy proprioceptive estimate?" Build the H7-style fidelity sweep on the *force* channel, not just the geometry channel.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The literature supports the *qualitative* claim that certified-as-cost beats reward shaping ([[2508.07611|End-to-End]]: 53% unsafe-time reduction, 60% vs 33% narrow-passage; [[2510.14959|CBF-RL]]: 99.0% vs 38.7%), but every existing certified-vs-reward instance is locomotion/obstacle-avoidance with *no wrench term*. The card's framing is honest about this — it explicitly concedes SHIELD/CBF-RL take the mechanism and leave the under-load axis.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The Related table is unusually well-mapped: [[2502.02858|p-SSA]] already demonstrates "safe single-leg balancing with hand control on real G1," [[2502.03132|SPARK]] gives the shared safety-cost substrate, and [[2603.02443|Safe-WBLM]] has the wrench-aware *governor* but on a quadruped. The whitespace is precisely the intersection none of them occupy: humanoid + support-polygon + external-wrench-parameterized safe set.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The "no one compares certified vs reward-shaping under load" claim is now *almost* falsified from one direction — [[2508.07611|End-to-End]] (P3O-CBF-as-cost) IS the certified-vs-reward head-to-head, it just lacks the load term. So H1 is not a new *protocol*, it is a one-variable extension (add payload OOD to an existing comparison). That makes H1 cheaper to run but also less novel; the load-bearing novelty must be H2 (the wrench term itself), not H1.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: FAME ([[2603.08961]]) is the falsifier walking in the front door: a pure robustness-by-training policy (latent force-context + curriculum + DR) that hits 73.84% standing success under randomized bimanual hand forces and stays upright on a real H12 under asymmetric *and* symmetric load. If reward/DR already buys balance under load, the certified guarantee may "buy nothing" — which is the card's own stated falsifier for H1/H2.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: FAME's mechanism is exactly the HWC-class baseline the card names, but *stronger and load-specific* — it conditions on the estimated wrench, so it is not even wrench-blind. [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s own paper warns the guarantee is "sensitive to the parameter ε" and needs "conservative tuning," i.e., the certified layer can over-constrain (the card's Risk-1) and a well-tuned adaptive policy may match it.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The bet quietly conflates two claims — "certified beats reward on *survival*" and "certified beats reward on *guarantee*." FAME can win survival without any guarantee. The only defensible novelty is the *certificate under unknown load*, so the experiment must report a metric reward shaping structurally cannot produce (a proven forward-invariance margin / worst-case balance bound vs payload magnitude), not just a survival percentage FAME can also climb.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: "Certified safety on humanoids" is the narrative that unlocks deployment capital and insurance/regulatory sign-off — it is the story every humanoid company (Unitree G1/H12, TOCABI) needs to sell co-bots near people, so the topic is incentive-aligned and citation-rich. But the same incentive floods the space: SPARK, Safety-Gymnasium, and HumanoidBench are racing to be the standard substrate, and benchmark-chasing rewards a clean "X% safer on benchmark Y" headline over a hard-to-package theorem.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The sheer density of 2025-2026 entries on this exact axis (SHIELD, CBF-RL, End-to-End, p-SSA, ISSf-CBF, Poisson-CBF, Adaptive Shielding, plus a *replication-and-stress-test* paper [[2605.19009]] already auditing SPARK filters) shows a crowded, fast-saturating field where the cheap cells fill within months.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The wrench-equilibrium safe set is the *least commoditizable* asset here — it cannot be cloned by swapping a benchmark seed, and it ports to a B2B selling point (certified payload rating: "this humanoid is guaranteed balance-safe up to N kg / M N·m hand wrench"). Frame the contribution as a *payload-certified envelope*, the thing FAME's "manipulation envelope" measures empirically but cannot guarantee — that is the defensible, fundable wedge.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the model-based-safety-over-learned-policy pendulum swinging back — the same arc as Dreamer-era model-based RL wrapping model-free policies, and the DR-vs-system-ID debate of the 2018 sim2real wave. Historically the "hard guarantee" side wins the *certification* argument but loses the *deployment* argument to whoever makes robustness-by-training cheap enough, then the two fuse (CBF-as-cost / internalized barriers).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The fusion is already visible: [[2510.14959|CBF-RL]] internalizes the CBF into the policy and [[2508.07611|End-to-End]] turns it into a CMDP cost — both abandoning the runtime filter the card's bet relies on. The DR wave's lesson (DR robustifies in expectation, certifies nothing) is exactly RAPT's "policies execute confidently in OOD states" finding, replayed.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Every prior "certified-over-learned" wave got absorbed into training within ~2 years. The runtime *wrench-aware barrier* is therefore a transitional artifact — its durable contribution is the *safe-set formulation* (the wrench-equilibrium constraint), which will outlive the filter because it can be reused as a CBF-as-cost term once someone internalizes it. Publish the safe set as the asset, not the filter.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Practitioner/Academic on whether the guarantee buys anything*: Skeptic says FAME's 73.84% load-robustness shows reward/DR already solves balance-under-load, so the certificate is redundant; Practitioner/Academic counter that FAME measures *survival in expectation* and certifies nothing — a fall it does not provably exclude is still a fall. The clash is the card's own H1/H2 falsifier, made concrete by a real 2026 paper.
   - *Academic vs the card's "no one runs the comparison under load" claim*: Academic notes [[2508.07611|End-to-End]] already runs certified-as-cost vs reward-shaping on a real G1 — the comparison exists; only the *load term* is missing. The card slightly over-states the whitespace of H1.
   - *Historian vs Practitioner on the runtime filter*: Historian says the runtime barrier is transitional and will be internalized (CBF-RL/End-to-End already did); Practitioner says the runtime O(1) filter is the *only* thing that gives a per-step guarantee under OOD the training-time methods cannot, because internalized barriers lose the certificate once payload drifts past training.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.** Strongest: **Academic** — every claim is anchored to a real metric in the table and it correctly locates the novelty in H2 (the wrench term) rather than H1 (a protocol that mostly exists). Weakest: **Economist** — directionally right about incentives and the "payload-certified envelope" framing is genuinely useful, but it is the least falsifiable and offers no experiment that could refute the bet.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.** *On identical real-robot single-leg-balance-under-stepped-payload episodes, does a wrench-aware certified barrier ever prevent a fall that FAME-class force-adaptive RL (matched capacity, same online wrench estimate) does not — and can it report a provable balance margin FAME structurally cannot?* If yes, the certificate buys something; if the fall sets coincide, the wrench term and the guarantee are redundant over robustness-by-training.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).** Balance under external load IS the unaddressed frontier (no current humanoid safety system carries a payload/wrench term in its safe set), the per-step check is *latency-feasible* (2.99 ms / 1.63 ms inside the control loop), and online *wrench estimation quality* — not the barrier algebra — is the real bottleneck for any wrench-aware guarantee.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by none).** *Multi-contact and the support-polygon's own validity.* Every paper and every hypothesis assumes a fixed, well-defined support polygon (flat-ground single/double stance). Under real loco-manipulation the robot leans on a surface, braces against the payload, or takes a recovery step — the contact set *changes*, so the "CoM inside support polygon" constraint the entire bet rests on is itself a function of the (uncertain) contact configuration. No perspective and no hypothesis interrogates whether the safe set is well-posed when contacts are being made and broken.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** C2 is a *real but narrowing* whitespace. The mechanism it correctly disowns (certified barrier over a learned humanoid policy) is taken — SHIELD, CBF-RL, and End-to-End all ship it, the last two even running the certified-vs-reward head-to-head the card wants for H1, just without a load term. What genuinely remains unoccupied is the *wrench-aware safe set* (H2): a humanoid balance barrier whose support-polygon constraint is parameterized by the external payload equilibrium. That cell is empty across the literature checked. The threat is not pre-emption of the certificate but *erosion of its value*: FAME ([[2603.08961]]), published this quarter, hits 73.84% standing success under randomized bimanual hand forces with pure force-adaptive RL on a real humanoid, sensor-free — exactly the robustness-by-training baseline the card stakes H1/H2 against, and stronger than the HWC-class strawman the card names. Commit only if the contribution is reframed around the *certificate FAME cannot produce* (a provable payload-parameterized balance envelope), not a survival percentage FAME can also climb.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The certified-vs-reward head-to-head already exists for locomotion; H1 is a one-variable extension, not a new protocol.* [[2508.07611|End-to-End]] (P3O-CBF-as-cost, 53% unsafe-time cut, real G1) and [[2510.14959|CBF-RL]] (99.0% vs 38.7%) are the comparison; only the load term is missing. Supported by Academic, Historian; unchallenged. (Highest reliability — direct metrics.)
2. *Latency is not the binding constraint; the per-step check is loop-feasible.* CMP 2.99 ms, RAPT 1.63 ms (H5 effectively pre-confirmed). Supported by Practitioner, Academic; challenged by none.
3. *The wrench-equilibrium safe set (H2) is the genuine, least-commoditizable whitespace.* No checked paper (SHIELD, ISSf-CBF, p-SSA, Safe WBLM, Poisson-CBF, FAME) carries an external-payload term in a humanoid balance barrier. Supported by Academic, Economist, Historian; the Skeptic agrees it is *novel* but questions whether it is *valuable*.
4. *Robustness-by-training may match the certified layer on survival, threatening H1/H2's value.* FAME's 73.84% / real H12 load-robustness is the live falsifier. Supported by Skeptic; challenged by Practitioner/Academic (survival ≠ guarantee). (Reliability moderate — the threat is to *value*, not *existence*.)
5. *Online wrench-estimation fidelity gates the whole bet — a degraded force input makes the certified check unsafe (a force-channel H7).* FAME's `F_ext = -(J^T)^† (τ−τ_g)` estimator defines the noise floor any sensor-free wrench-aware barrier inherits. Supported by Practitioner; latent in Academic; addressed by no current hypothesis. (Lower reliability — extrapolated, but high-leverage.)

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Skeptic's threat (FAME) and the Practitioner's caveat (sensor-free wrench estimation) are the *same fact* viewed twice: FAME is simultaneously the robustness-by-training baseline that erodes the certificate's survival advantage AND the source of the noisy wrench estimate the certificate must consume. The certified layer cannot escape FAME — it must either *beat* FAME's estimator or *use* it. That collapses H2 and the missing force-channel-H7 into one experiment: certify the balance margin *using FAME's own wrench estimate as input* and show it still holds (or fails gracefully) where FAME-the-policy silently falls.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to H2 only.** Drop H1 as a headline (it largely exists in [[2508.07611|End-to-End]]; keep it as a baseline cell, extended with payload OOD). Reframe the contribution as a *payload-certified balance envelope* — the certificate FAME's empirically-measured "manipulation envelope" cannot guarantee. Concrete next experiment: take [[2605.25546|ISSf-CBF-WBC]]'s ISSf-CBF safe set, add the external-wrench equilibrium term to the support-polygon constraint, and run real-robot (or matched-sim) single-leg-balance under *stepped* end-effector payload, feeding the barrier FAME's sensor-free wrench estimate; against an equal-capacity FAME-class force-adaptive RL baseline, report on identical episodes (i) fall/collision rate per payload level, (ii) the *provable* balance margin vs payload magnitude (the metric FAME cannot emit), and (iii) graceful-degradation behavior as the wrench estimate is noised. Kill-criterion: if FAME's fall set ⊆ the barrier's fall set across the payload sweep, the wrench term buys nothing — abandon.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Is the support polygon even a well-posed safe-set boundary under real loco-manipulation, where the contact set is made/broken (bracing, leaning, recovery steps) — or does a payload-certified guarantee require lifting the safe set from "CoM-in-fixed-polygon" to a *contact-and-wrench-conditioned* invariant over the changing contact manifold? If the latter, the durable contribution is not a filter but a new safe-set *formulation*, the asset the Historian says will outlive every runtime barrier.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical robotics professor. The briefing is well-constructed and unusually honest about its own narrowing whitespace; my job is to find where it over-claims and where a single unverified fact carries too much weight.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

1. **Finding 1 — "Certified-vs-reward head-to-head already exists for locomotion; H1 is a one-variable extension." — 9/10.**
   Directly grounded. End-to-End (2508.07611) row confirms P3O-CBF-as-cost vs PPO-reward-shaping, 53% unsafe-zone cut, real G1; CBF-RL (2510.14959) confirms 99.0% vs 38.7%. Both verified in the card table (lines 851, 832). The deduction that H1 is therefore an *extension* not a *new protocol* is sound and self-consistent. The half-point off: "one-variable" is rhetorical — adding a payload OOD axis to a locomotion-only CBF requires a new wrench term in the safe set (that is H2), so H1 and H2 are not cleanly separable, and calling H1 "one variable" quietly imports H2's hard part.

2. **Finding 2 — "Latency is not binding; per-step check is loop-feasible." — 8/10.**
   CMP 2.99 ms / RAPT 1.63 ms are real card numbers (lines 814, 837) and inside a 50 Hz–1 kHz loop. Solid. Docked two points because the claim quietly generalizes from *these two specific shields* to *a wrench-aware barrier that does not yet exist*. A payload-parameterized CBF-QP with an online wrench estimator in the loop is not the same compute object as CMP's learned O(1) projection or RAPT's detector; Poisson-CBF Humanoid (2508.11129, line 847) already shows a formal humanoid CBF costing 20–100 ms PDE solves. H5 is "pre-confirmed" only for the *existing* rows, not for the proposed method.

3. **Finding 3 — "The wrench-equilibrium safe set (H2) is the genuine, least-commoditizable whitespace." — 7/10.**
   The novelty claim is well-supported: no checked row (SHIELD, ISSf-CBF, p-SSA, Safe WBLM, Poisson-CBF, FAME) carries an external-payload term in a *humanoid* balance barrier. But the briefing under-weights its own card's line-833 entry: **Safe WBLM (2603.02443) already builds a wrench-aware certified governor** — the only thing it lacks is the humanoid + support-polygon substrate (it is on a quadruped). So the whitespace is not "wrench-aware certified safe set" in the abstract; it is the narrower "wrench-aware *support-polygon* term on a *humanoid*." That is real but a notch less white than "empty across the literature checked" implies. The "least-commoditizable" tag is an Economist assertion, not a verified fact.

4. **Finding 4 — "Robustness-by-training (FAME) may match the certified layer on survival." — 6/10.**
   FAME's 73.84% / 29.44% / 51.40% and sensor-free real-H12 deployment are verified verbatim in KH 2603.08961. The threat-to-*value* framing (survival ≠ guarantee) is the correct rebuttal and is properly logged. **The real weakness the briefing hides: FAME is standing-only.** The card's own line-139 caveat states FAME "conditions on the load to *stand still*, not a forward model of the arm→base reaction during a reach (standing-only)." The C2 bet is *single-leg balance under a stepped end-effector reach* — a harder, different task. FAME is therefore not a clean head-to-head falsifier; it is a falsifier for a *standing* sub-case the C2 experiment does not centrally test. The briefing treats FAME as "the robustness-by-training baseline the card stakes H1/H2 against" without flagging this task mismatch, which inflates the threat. The kill-criterion ("FAME's fall set ⊆ barrier's fall set") is only well-defined if both run the *same* single-leg-reach episodes — which FAME-as-published does not.

5. **Finding 5 — "Online wrench-estimation fidelity gates the bet; force-channel H7." — 4/10.**
   The *insight* is correct and high-leverage (a certified check fed a noisy proprioceptive wrench inherits that noise floor). But the briefing's load-bearing artifact — FAME's estimator written as `F_ext = -(J^T)^† (τ − τ_g)` — **does not appear anywhere in the vault outside the review parts.** FAME's KH note says only "estimates wrist interaction forces from proprioceptive joint torque and kinematics" with no equation. The specific pseudo-inverse formula is an *extrapolation/reconstruction* presented as if quoted from FAME. It is a plausible reconstruction of a standard momentum-/torque-residual external-force estimator, but it is unverified against the paper and should not be cited as "FAME's estimator." This is the single most fabrication-exposed claim in the briefing.

**Mean of the five scores: (9+8+7+6+4)/5 = 6.8. Lowest: 4.**

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 5's estimator formula `F_ext = -(J^T)^† (τ − τ_g)`.** It is presented as FAME's own equation and is the literal "noise floor any sensor-free wrench-aware barrier inherits" — yet it is nowhere in FAME's KH note or anywhere in the vault. **What would verify it:** fetch FAME (2603.08961) full text (arxiv /abs/ or alphaxiv get_paper_content) and confirm (a) that this is the exact estimator form, and (b) crucially, whether FAME reports any *quantitative* wrench-estimation error (RMS Newtons / bias under bimanual load). Without (b) the "noise floor" framing has no number — Finding 5 asserts a gate whose magnitude is unmeasured. A secondary verification: confirm FAME is genuinely standing-only (Finding 4) so the task-mismatch caveat is firm before it is used to discount the falsifier.

##### 3. Bias check

**The Skeptic over-dominated, with the Economist supplying the reframe.** The entire CEO summary, the "hidden connection," and the actionable insight are organized around FAME-as-threat (Skeptic) and the payload-certified-envelope sell (Economist). FAME appears in 3 of 5 findings and is the spine of the synthesis. The Practitioner contributes one genuinely independent fact (the force-channel H7). The **Academic is under-credited relative to its reliability**: it produced the highest-confidence finding (H1 already exists) and correctly relocated the novelty to H2, yet the synthesis foregrounds the Skeptic's lower-confidence value-threat over the Academic's higher-confidence existence claim. The **Historian is decorative** — its "transitional artifact / publish the safe set" line is reused as the Frontier question but adds no constraint the Academic did not. Net: the briefing leans pessimistic-but-fundable (Skeptic+Economist) rather than evidence-weighted (Academic). The honest read is that the bet survives the Skeptic better than the synthesis tone suggests, *because* the Skeptic's headline falsifier (FAME) is task-mismatched.

##### 4. Missing perspective

**A control-theorist / formal-methods reviewer** would change the conclusion. The entire bet rests on "CoM-inside-support-polygon as a certified safe set with an external-wrench term," but nobody on the panel interrogates whether such a CBF is *constructible with a valid forward-invariance proof* under an *estimated* (not measured) wrench. A CBF certificate requires a known disturbance bound; a proprioceptive wrench estimate has unbounded worst-case error during contact transitions and torque-saturation, which can *void the forward-invariance guarantee entirely* — meaning the "provable balance margin" the briefing markets as the metric FAME cannot emit may itself be unprovable under sensor-free wrench input. This sixth angle collapses Findings 4 and 5 together in the briefing's *disfavor*: if the certificate cannot be proven under estimated wrench, then the certified layer's only claimed advantage over FAME (a guarantee) evaporates, and the briefing's own kill-criterion fires before the experiment runs. The blind-spot the contradiction map already noted (support-polygon validity under made/broken contact) is the *kinematic* half of this; the *disturbance-bound* half is missing and is more lethal.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**
A strong, intellectually honest briefing — it correctly disowns the taken mechanism, locates a real (if narrowing) whitespace, and proposes a falsifiable experiment with a kill-criterion. It loses the A tier on three things: (1) a fabricated-looking equation doing load-bearing work, (2) an unflagged task mismatch that inflates its central threat, and (3) a tone that over-weights the least-falsifiable perspectives.

**Top fixes, in order:**
1. **Verify or strike the `F_ext` formula.** Pull FAME's text; either cite the exact estimator and (critically) its reported wrench-error magnitude, or downgrade Finding 5 to "FAME estimates wrench sensor-free from torque residuals (error unreported)" and stop quoting an equation.
2. **Flag FAME as standing-only.** The kill-criterion compares fall sets on "the same single-leg-reach episodes," but FAME-as-published stands still. Either (a) re-train/adapt FAME to the reach task as a *built* baseline and say so, or (b) demote FAME from "the falsifier" to "the standing-case falsifier" and find/construct the reach-task robustness-by-training baseline (HEX 2604.07993 or FALCON 2505.06776 may be closer on-task).
3. **Add the formal-methods gate before the empirical one.** State explicitly whether a forward-invariance proof survives an *estimated* (bounded-how?) wrench. If the disturbance bound is unprovable, the "provable balance margin" deliverable is at risk — surface that as the first kill-criterion, ahead of the empirical fall-set comparison.
4. **Down-weight Safe WBLM's whitespace overlap honestly.** The wrench-aware certified governor already exists (quadruped); narrow the novelty claim to "humanoid support-polygon wrench term" rather than "wrench-aware certified safe set."
