### D (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence and central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster D — "Whole-Body Teleoperation & Human-Motion Retargeting" — whose framing is *"The whole-body data wall: demonstrations of coupled loco-manipulation are scarce, embodiment-mismatched, and per-platform. The four directions remove four different bricks from that wall"* (D1 retarget, D2 capture, D3 transfer, D4 synthesize). The reviewer's question is whether this grouping carves the problem at the right joint and whether the shared bet (the wall is the binding constraint, and each direction's surviving novel core is a controlled-isolation experiment nobody has run) is sound.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys humanoid policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The data wall is real and the four-brick decomposition matches how a lab actually sources data, but the cluster quietly assumes a *working whole-body controller already exists* underneath every brick — OmniRetarget rides on a G1 RL tracker, HumanoidMimicGen reuses the Homie locomotion controller, Any2Any needs a pretrained source WBT policy. The hard, unglamorous engineering is that controller, and the cluster treats it as substrate, not contribution.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: HumanoidMimicGen's hybrid action space is explicitly "a reinforcement-learned locomotion controller (Homie) + a joint-space controller for upper-body" (KH note); GRAIL trains policies "built upon a pretrained whole-body controller"; Any2Any's whole win is *not* retraining the 9,000-GPU-hour tracker. The +51 pp in EgoHumanoid (82% vs 31%) is also conditioned on a VR rig + alignment pipeline that costs real human-hours per demo.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Report **demos-per-real-robot-hour** and **the cost of the underlying controller** as first-class numbers. The cluster's "data wall" is half a *controller* wall — D4's synthesis only looks free because the locomotion controller it replays through was paid for once, off-budget. Before committing, build/borrow that controller and verify sim-to-real on *your* G1, because every cited 90% real number assumes one that already deploys.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The cluster is honest that the *systems* half is consensus (it repeatedly concedes "the capture interface is taken," "the seed route is multiply-instantiated," "interaction-preservation is consensus") and correctly relocates novelty to *controlled-isolation experiments* — the contact-fidelity→SR regression (D1 H2, D4 H4), the fixed-base-vs-whole-body subtraction (D2 H1), the transfer-vs-generalist crossover (D3 H5). That is the right scientific move, but the literature has crept closer than the card admits.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: alphaxiv surfaces 2510.02252 (GMR, "Retargeting Matters") which *already* isolates retargeting quality from training under one fixed pipeline across 4 retargeters — exactly D1 H2's protocol minus the regression slope; 2602.02960 (EAGLE) and 2602.05791 (XHugWBC cross-humanoid) are the D3 generalist comparators already built; 2505.12705 (DreamGen Bench) already reports >90% Pearson of a generation-quality score to downstream SR, which is D4 H4's claim for *video*. The surviving novelty is narrow: the *slope+R² regression itself* and the *whole-body-loco-MANIPULATION* qualifier (every transfer/scaling result — H-Zero, Embodiment Scaling Laws — stops at locomotion).
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The bets are real but *thin* — each is "the measured curve nobody plotted" on top of an effect everybody already believes. That is a workshop-paper-sized delta per direction unless you commit to the one qualifier the field genuinely hasn't crossed (manipulation-coupled, not locomotion) and make the *negative/non-monotone result* publishable too.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The "four bricks of one wall" framing is a taxonomy, not a thesis — and taxonomies don't publish. D1/D4 are nearly the *same* bet stated twice (contact-fidelity→trainability regression appears as D1 H2 *and* D4 H4), and D2/D4 are explicitly framed as the same axis ("the sharpest line is D2 vs D4"), which means the cluster's four directions are really ~two independent ideas wearing four hats.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: D1 H2 and D4 H4 are verbatim the same experiment (regress penetration/foot-skating → downstream SR) on retargeted vs synthesized data; the cluster intro itself collapses D2 and D4 onto one capture-vs-synthesis axis; D3 H5 ("cheap transfer beats one-time generalist below a crossover") is hedged into near-unfalsifiability — *of course* something wins below some crossover and loses above it, so the result is guaranteed regardless of which way it goes. The "whitespace" in D1 is conceded to be *only* the regression slope after GMR (2510.02252) already ran the controlled comparison.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The whitespace is real but *small and partially squatted*. The honest contribution is one paper — "a contact-fidelity→trainability law for humanoid whole-body data, measured identically across retargeted, captured, and synthesized sources" — *not* a four-direction cluster. Picking one brick and calling it a cluster inflates a single experiment into a research program.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money and incentives).**
- *Core position*: This cluster is shaped by who pays for compute. Every from-scratch generalist comparator (SONIC 21k GPU-h, Embodiment Scaling Laws 817 embodiments, Humanoid-GPT 2B frames, GR00T) is an *industry-lab* artifact; the cheap-transfer/PEFT side (Any2Any ~1%, MOTIF 1-shot) is the *academic-affordable* lane. D3's "transfer-vs-generalist crossover" is literally the academic-vs-industry compute divide dressed as a scientific question — and the academic is structurally incentivized to find the crossover *low*.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The anchor set is NVIDIA (HumanoidMimicGen, GRAIL, GR00T), Amazon FAR (OmniRetarget), LimX Dynamics (Any2Any), Shanghai AI Lab (EAGLE/XHugWBC) — hardware/foundation-model vendors whose narrative is "humanoid data is the bottleneck, here is our engine." The benchmark substrate (BiGym, TeleOpBench, SkillBench, HumanoidArena, AnyBody) is proliferating and *un-saturated*, which is itself a signal: no canonical leaderboard means no shared yardstick means the "controlled isolation" results won't be directly comparable to anyone.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The cheap-transfer bet (D3) and the regression-law bet (D1/D4) are the *only* two a solo academic can actually run at the industry-comparator scale required — but only if you can borrow the pretrained controller (Practitioner's point) for free. If you can't, you're funding NVIDIA's compute moat by proxy. Pick the bet whose comparator you can *download*, not *retrain*.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the imitation-learning data-bottleneck cycle replaying on humanoids — the exact arc of manipulation 2020-2023 (RoboMimic → MimicGen → DexMimicGen → BridgeData/OXE → RT-X generalists). Every brick here has a manipulation-era ancestor: D1=retargeting/correspondence, D2=teleop rigs (ALOHA lineage), D3=cross-embodiment (OXE/Octo), D4=MimicGen (the cluster even cites SkillMimicGen/DexMimicGen as "the mimicgen ancestor").
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The cluster's own lineage notes — DexMimicGen 2410.24185 → HumanoidMimicGen; Octo/GR00T as generalist comparators; the capture-vs-synthesis tension is RoboMimic-vs-MimicGen verbatim. In manipulation, the "controlled isolation" papers (e.g., what-makes-demos-good studies) landed *after* the systems, were modestly cited, and were quickly overtaken by scale (RT-X). The pixel-vs-latent and modular-vs-end-to-end swings suggest D3's transfer-vs-scale tension resolves *toward scale* once data accumulates.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: History says the *crossover moves*. Whatever crossover D3 measures in 2026 (cheap-transfer wins below X compute) will be obsolete by the next generalist drop — the manipulation analog (Octo vs fine-tuning) flipped within a year. Bet on the *invariant* (the phase-anchored structure, D3 H1/H2 — the localized dynamics-pathway lever) which is a durable mechanistic claim, not on the crossover *location*, which is a perishable operating point.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Where perspectives clash.**
   - *ACADEMIC vs SKEPTIC on whitespace size*: Academic says the bets are real-but-thin (the unplotted curve on a believed effect); Skeptic says they're so thin and so squatted (GMR ran D1's comparison; DreamGen Bench ran D4's correlation) that it's one paper, not a cluster. Specific claim in tension: is "the regression *slope+R²* across sources" a contribution, or a finishing touch on GMR/DreamGen?
   - *PRACTITIONER vs ECONOMIST on what's free*: Practitioner says the underlying whole-body controller is the real cost and is hidden off-budget; Economist says the cheap-transfer/regression bets are the only solo-affordable ones *precisely because* you borrow that controller. They clash on whether borrowing the controller de-risks the bet (Economist) or smuggles the hard part out of view so the result doesn't generalize to a lab that lacks one (Practitioner).
   - *HISTORIAN vs the card's D3 framing*: The card promotes "cheap transfer beats one-time generalist below a crossover" to the front-line falsifier; Historian says the crossover is perishable and the durable claim is the localized invariant (D3 H2). Direct contradiction on which D3 hypothesis is load-bearing.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — every claim is anchored to a specific real paper the card under-weights (GMR 2510.02252, DreamGen 2505.12705, EAGLE 2602.02960) and it correctly identifies the one genuinely-uncrossed qualifier (manipulation-coupled, not locomotion). It neither over-claims novelty nor dismisses it.
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — the manipulation-era analogy is illuminating but the "scale always wins" inference is exactly the prior the card's D3 H5 is *designed to test on loco-manip*, where the locomotion-only scaling results may not transfer; the historian assumes the conclusion. Useful for taste, weak as evidence.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction (Academic vs Skeptic on whitespace).**
   Does 2510.02252 (GMR)'s shared-suite retarget→tracking comparison already report, or trivially yield, a *monotone penetration/foot-skating → downstream-SR regression with slope and R²* across its 4 retargeters — or only rank-orders them? If GMR already gives the curve, D1's surviving core collapses to D4's and the cluster is one experiment; if GMR only ranks, the regression-law-across-sources is a real (if narrow) contribution.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   The whole-body data wall is genuinely binding, the systems-level routes (retarget/capture/transfer/synthesize) are *already consensus and multiply-instantiated*, and the only defensible novelty left is the *controlled-isolation / measured-curve* layer on top — specifically anchored to the one qualifier the field has not crossed: whole-body loco-**MANIPULATION** rather than locomotion. No perspective disputes that the bricks exist or that the systems are built; all agree the contribution must be a measurement, not a system.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by none).**
   **Evaluation validity and reward/contact specification.** Every direction's payoff is "downstream RL/IL success rate," but no perspective questions whether SR on these proliferating, un-saturated, mutually-incomparable benchmarks (BiGym, SkillBench, HumanoidArena, AnyBody, DreamGen Bench) is a *stable* dependent variable — HumanoidArena's own result is that cross-GMT-tracker transfer drops 39.9% with 0% retention on many tasks, meaning the same policy scores wildly differently by execution backend. If SR is backend-dependent, every "monotone fidelity→SR regression" is measuring the tracker as much as the data, and the contact-graph specification (which surfaces touch) that all of D1/D4 presuppose is itself an unmodeled, hand-annotated input.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.**
   Cluster D carves the humanoid whole-body data problem at a *defensible* joint — the data wall is real and the four "bricks" (retarget/capture/transfer/synthesize) genuinely partition how a lab sources coupled demonstrations. But the cluster's own honesty is its verdict: it concedes that every *systems* route is already consensus and multiply-instantiated, relocating novelty to a thin, scientifically-correct layer of controlled-isolation experiments — measured curves and subtractions nobody bothered to plot. Two of those (D1's and D4's contact-fidelity→trainability regression) are nearly the same experiment, and one (D3's transfer-vs-generalist crossover) is a perishable operating point dressed as a law. The single durable, genuinely-uncrossed opening is the *manipulation-coupled* qualifier: every transfer and scaling result in the literature stops at locomotion. The bet survives as whitespace, but as **one tight measurement paper, not a four-direction program** — and it is hostage to a hidden dependency (a working whole-body controller) and an unstable dependent variable (backend-dependent SR).

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   - **F1 (highest): The data wall is binding and the systems-level routes are already consensus.** The cluster repeatedly concedes this in-text and alphaxiv confirms saturation (OmniRetarget, Human2Humanoid, MeshMimic for D1; HMI/HumanoidExo/EgoHumanoid for D2; Any2Any/XHugWBC/EAGLE for D3; HumanoidMimicGen/DemoHLM/GRAIL for D4). *Supported by all five; challenged by none.*
   - **F2: The only surviving novelty is the controlled-isolation / measured-curve layer, anchored to the loco-MANIPULATION (not locomotion) qualifier.** *Supported by Academic, Skeptic, Economist; nuanced by Historian (durable mechanism vs perishable crossover); Practitioner indifferent.*
   - **F3: D1 and D4 collapse onto one experiment (contact-fidelity→trainability regression); D2 and D4 are one capture-vs-synthesis axis.** Verbatim from the card (D1 H2 ≡ D4 H4; intro: "the sharpest line is D2 vs D4"). *Supported by Skeptic, Academic; unchallenged on the facts.*
   - **F4: The literature has crept into the claimed whitespace — GMR (2510.02252) ran D1's controlled comparison, DreamGen Bench (2505.12705) ran D4's fidelity→SR correlation, EAGLE/XHugWBC are D3's built generalist comparators.** *Asserted by Academic via alphaxiv; Skeptic amplifies; Practitioner/Economist neutral; resolvable only by reading GMR's actual regression (Prompt-2 Q3).*
   - **F5 (lowest, but highest-leverage if true): The dependent variable (downstream SR) is backend/tracker-dependent and the contact-graph input is hand-specified, threatening every regression's validity.** *Raised only by the synthesis (blind spot); evidenced by HumanoidArena's 39.9% cross-GMT drop; untested by any perspective.*

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.**
   Practitioner's "hidden controller" and Economist's "downloadable comparator" are the *same* lever as the blind spot's "backend-dependent SR." All three are the unmodeled *low-level whole-body tracker* — it is simultaneously the off-budget cost (Practitioner), the thing-you-must-borrow-not-retrain to make the bet solo-feasible (Economist), and the confound that makes SR an unstable measurement (HumanoidArena). Whoever you borrow the tracker from silently determines your cost, your comparability, and your regression's validity. The tracker is the real load-bearing object the cluster treats as wallpaper.

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK then COMMIT to a single fused direction (not the cluster).**
   - *Do not commit to D as a four-direction program* — it is two ideas (a fidelity→trainability law; a cheap-transfer-vs-scale operating point) in four hats.
   - *Commit instead to one paper*: **"A contact-fidelity → trainability law for humanoid whole-body data, measured identically across retargeted (D1), captured (D2), and synthesized (D4) sources, on loco-MANIPULATION."** This fuses the cluster's two real experiments and exploits the one uncrossed qualifier, while making the cross-*source* comparison (does the *same* slope hold for retargeted vs synthesized data?) the genuinely-new contribution GMR/DreamGen do not provide.
   - *De-risk first, two cheap gates before any training*: (a) **Resolve Prompt-2 Q3** — read GMR (2510.02252) and DreamGen Bench (2505.12705) in full to confirm neither already reports the slope+R² across sources; if either does, pivot to the cross-*source*-invariance framing only. (b) **Fix the tracker confound** — pick ONE frozen low-level tracker (the borrowable controller) and hold it constant across all data sources, then verify SR is stable to a tracker swap on 2-3 tasks; if SR moves >20% (HumanoidArena territory), the regression is invalid and you must report SR-conditioned-on-tracker, not SR.
   - *Concrete next experiment*: On one fixed tracker + one shared task suite (BiGym's whole-body-vs-bimanual switch gives the coupling knob), generate matched-count demo sets from OmniRetarget (retarget), HMI/Mobile-ALOHA (capture), and HumanoidMimicGen/GRAIL (synthesize), each at several contact-fidelity levels (vary penetration/foot-skating); regress downstream SR on contact violation *per source*; the headline result is whether the slope is **source-invariant** (one law) or source-dependent (synthesized feasibility ≠ retargeted feasibility). Falsifier: flat/non-monotone SR, or slope that swaps sign across sources, kills the law.

5. **The frontier question that would change everything.**
   Is "contact fidelity" even the right state variable, or is the load-bearing quantity the *closed-loop dynamic feasibility under the deployed policy's own action distribution* — i.e., does a kinematically contact-perfect demo still mis-train because the policy's rollout leaves the demonstrated contact manifold? If trainability is governed by on-policy reachable feasibility rather than off-policy reference fidelity, the entire cluster's metric (penetration/foot-skating on the *reference*) is measuring the wrong thing, and the data wall is actually a *closed-loop-feasibility* wall that no amount of cleaner references removes.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. The briefing is unusually honest and I want to reward that, but honesty about a weakness is not the same as fixing it. My job here is to find where the synthesis still over-reaches, where its confidence outruns its evidence, and where a load-bearing claim rests on the card quoting itself rather than on an independent check.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10 reliability, per key finding)

**F1 — The data wall is binding; the systems-level routes are consensus and multiply-instantiated. → 9/10.**
This is the most defensible claim in the briefing and the card earns it the hard way: every direction's "Today's answers" bullet explicitly concedes the systems route is taken (D1 line 914 "interaction-preservation is now *consensus*"; D2 line 997 "the robot-free whole-body capture *pipeline* is now consensus"; D3 line 1088 "the space has densely filled"; D4 line 1176 "multiply-instantiated, not a single-anchor frontier"). The instantiation lists are dense and specific (HMI / HumanoidExo / EgoHumanoid for capture; HumanoidMimicGen / DemoHLM / MoMaGen for synthesis), and DemoHLM is flagged as *predating* the D4 anchor — a tell that the author actually checked dates rather than asserting precedence. I dock one point only because "consensus" is being inferred from a curated KH pool, not a systematic survey: absence of a contrarian result in this vault is weaker evidence than the prose implies. But the direction of the claim is not in doubt.

**F2 — The only surviving novelty is the controlled-isolation / measured-curve layer, anchored to the loco-MANIPULATION qualifier. → 7/10.**
The decomposition is correct and the qualifier is genuinely the sharpest edge: D3's own text (line 1089, 1093) concedes H-Zero and the scaling-laws line "stop at locomotion," and the loco-manip restriction is the one place the whitespace is uncrossed by construction. My deduction is that "the *only* surviving novelty" is stated more absolutely than the evidence supports. A measured-curve / controlled-subtraction contribution is real but it is a *thin* contribution — referees at a top venue routinely reject "we plotted the regression nobody plotted" as incremental unless the curve overturns a belief. The briefing half-admits this ("a thin, scientifically-correct layer") but then leans on it as the load-bearing reason to commit. Thin-but-correct is a real paper; it is not obviously a strong one, and the briefing does not price that risk.

**F3 — D1 and D4 collapse onto one experiment; D2 and D4 are one capture-vs-synthesis axis. → 8/10.**
Strongly supported by the card's own text. D1's H2 (line 954–958: regress downstream RL SR on penetration/foot-skating across retargeting methods) and D4's H4 (line 1229–1233: regress downstream SR on synthesized penetration/foot-skating) are the *same dependent variable regressed on the same independent variable*, differing only in whether the data source is retargeted or generated. That is the briefing's strongest structural insight and it is verifiable line-by-line, not asserted. I withhold the top mark only because "collapse onto one experiment" slightly overstates it: the two regressions share a form but not a confound structure — retargeted-source penetration and generation-time-enforced penetration are produced by different mechanisms, so a single slope across both is a *hypothesis the briefing itself proposes as the contribution*, not an established identity. The collapse is the opportunity, not a defect; the wording blurs that.

**F4 — The literature has crept into the claimed whitespace (GMR ran D1's comparison; DreamGen Bench ran D4's correlation; EAGLE/XHugWBC are D3's built comparators). → 6/10.**
This is the finding most likely to be partly wrong, and it is internally inconsistent in a revealing way. For DreamGen Bench the card's own text (line 1207, 1231) reports ">90% Pearson correlation to downstream SR" — so the *correlation-to-trainability* idea is demonstrably already published, and the briefing is right to raise the alarm. But the card immediately qualifies that DreamGen scores *generated-video physics-plausibility*, not whole-body-trajectory penetration/foot-skating — i.e. it narrows rather than closes the gap. The briefing's headline ("DreamGen Bench ran D4's fidelity→SR correlation") drops that qualifier and so overstates the encroachment. For GMR the card is *explicit* that it runs the controlled retargeter comparison but does **not** regress fidelity→SR across methods (line 929, 932, 940 all repeat "never regresses contact-fidelity → SR"). So the briefing's claim that "GMR ran D1's controlled comparison" is true only for the *protocol*, not for the *regression that is D1's actual novelty* — the briefing conflates the substrate with the contribution. The finding is directionally useful (the whitespace is narrower than the card's framing admits) but two of its three instances are overstated relative to the card's own hedges. The de-risk gate the briefing proposes ("read GMR and DreamGen in full") is exactly right and is the honest tell that this finding is provisional.

**F5 — The dependent variable (downstream SR) is backend/tracker-dependent and the contact-graph input is hand-specified; both threaten every regression's validity. → 8/10.**
This is the highest-leverage finding and it is well-evidenced from inside the card. HumanoidArena's 39.9% cross-GMT drop (TWIST2→SONIC) with 0.0% retention on many tasks (line 1026) is a *measured* instability in exactly the dependent variable every D1/D2/D4 regression uses, and the card surfaces it only in D2 — never connecting it to the contact-fidelity→SR regressions in D1 and D4 that it most threatens. The hand-specified-contact-graph risk is also in-card (D1 Risk-1, line 981: "the interaction-mesh objective requires knowing which surfaces touch"). The reason this is 8 and not 9 is that the *magnitude* of the threat is inferred, not measured for this experiment: HumanoidArena's 39.9% is a policy-tracker transfer drop, not a demonstration that *a fixed-tracker fidelity→SR slope* is unstable. The briefing correctly proposes the falsifying test (swap the tracker on 2–3 tasks, see if SR moves >20%) — which is an admission the threat's severity is currently a conjecture, however a well-motivated one.

**Mean of the five: (9 + 7 + 8 + 6 + 8) / 5 = 7.6. Lowest: 6 (F4).**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

The weakest claim is **F4's GMR encroachment** — specifically the assertion that GMR (2510.02252) "ran D1's controlled comparison." The card's own three independent restatements (lines 929, 932, 940) say GMR supplies the shared-suite protocol but *never regresses contact-fidelity → downstream-SR across methods*, which is the actual D1 contribution. So the briefing's headline is in tension with the card it is reviewing.

*Specific information needed to verify:* read GMR's paper in full and answer one question — **does GMR report a numeric slope and R² of downstream tracking/RL SR against penetration or foot-skating across its four compared retargeters, or only a per-retargeter SR table?** If the former, D1's H2 is already published and the cluster's headline contribution collapses to the *cross-source* (retargeted-vs-synthesized) invariance question only. If the latter, D1's regression survives as novel and F4 is overstated. This single fact decides whether the recommended paper has one new contribution or zero, so it is the correct first de-risk gate — and the briefing names it as such, which is to its credit. (Symmetrically, the second gate — reading DreamGen Bench to see whether its >90% Pearson is computed over *whole-body trajectory* penetration or only generated-video plausibility — would resolve the D4 half.)

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Skeptic over-dominated; the synthesis is structurally a teardown.** The CEO summary, the five findings, and the actionable insight all converge on the same move: concede consensus, relocate novelty to a thin layer, then attack that layer's durability (F2's "perishable operating point," F3's "collapse onto one experiment," F5's "unstable measurement"). Four of the five findings are deflationary; only F1 is affirmative, and F1 is itself a concession (the routes are *taken*). The Academic perspective is present but subordinated — it supplies the encroachment citations (F4) that the Skeptic then amplifies to deflate further. The Practitioner is described in the briefing's own words as "indifferent," and the Economist appears only as a single lever (the downloadable comparator) that gets folded into the Skeptic's blind-spot argument via the "hidden cross-perspective connection." The Historian's one genuinely additive move — distinguishing D3's *durable mechanism* (phase-anchored localization) from its *perishable crossover* (transfer-vs-generalist operating point) — is the most valuable non-Skeptic contribution and it is underweighted: it is the reason D3 is not simply dismissable, yet the actionable insight discards D3's crossover wholesale rather than preserving the localization claim. A balanced synthesis would have given the Historian's mechanism-vs-operating-point distinction equal billing with the Skeptic's collapse argument, because it is the one lever that could make the program more than a single measurement paper.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — the 6th angle

**The Reviewer-2 / publishability angle (the "so what does the field believe differently" test).** Every one of the five perspectives evaluates whether the *experiment is novel and correct*. None evaluates whether the *result, whichever way it comes out, changes what the field does*. This is the angle that would most change the conclusion, because the cluster's surviving contribution is a measurement, and measurements are only publishable when at least one outcome overturns a held belief. Run the test on the recommended paper: if the contact-fidelity→trainability slope is monotone and source-invariant, the field's prior ("cleaner references train better, and this holds whether you retarget or synthesize") is *confirmed*, not overturned — a null-of-the-interesting-kind that strong venues reject. The paper only becomes a top-tier result if the slope is *source-dependent* (the same penetration costs more SR when synthesized than when retargeted) or *non-monotone*, i.e. only on the falsifier branch. A sixth perspective focused on belief-change would therefore reframe the entire recommendation: do not sell the paper as "we measured the law," sell it as "we tested whether the law is source-invariant and it is not" — and would demand the author pre-commit to the source-dependent outcome being the headline, because the source-invariant outcome is a weak paper no matter how cleanly measured. This connects to and sharpens the Frontier Question: if the load-bearing quantity is closed-loop on-policy feasibility rather than off-policy reference fidelity, then *both* the confirming and the falsifying branches of the current experiment are measuring a proxy, and the belief-change perspective would push the whole program toward the closed-loop-feasibility framing before any data is collected.

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing that correctly carves the cluster, correctly identifies that the systems routes are saturated, and correctly locates the one genuinely-uncrossed qualifier (loco-MANIPULATION). The structural insight that D1-H2 and D4-H4 are the same regression on different sources (F3) is a real contribution and the fusion recommendation that follows from it is sound. The reason it is not an A is twofold: (i) the load-bearing encroachment finding (F4) is internally inconsistent with the card it reviews and overstates two of its three instances, and the briefing builds its central "commit to one paper not four" recommendation partly on that finding without first running the gate that would validate it; (ii) the briefing under-prices the publishability risk — it treats "a correct measurement nobody plotted" as a sufficient contribution when the more likely outcome (a confirming, source-invariant slope) is a weak result, and it does not pre-commit to the falsifier branch being the headline.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Resolve F4 before committing.** Read GMR (2510.02252) and DreamGen Bench (2505.12705) in full and answer the single question in §2 — does either already report the slope+R² across sources? The briefing's own gate (a) is correct; elevate it from "de-risk step" to *precondition*. The recommendation to "commit to one paper" is not yet earned until this returns negative.
2. **Reframe the contribution around belief-change, not measurement.** Make the *cross-source invariance* question (does the same slope hold for retargeted vs synthesized data?) the headline and pre-commit to the source-*dependent* outcome being the result that matters. The source-invariant outcome should be explicitly labelled in advance as the weak/null branch so the paper is not hostage to a confirming result.
3. **Promote the tracker confound (F5) from blind-spot to design constraint.** The hidden-cross-perspective connection is correct: the unmodeled low-level tracker is simultaneously the off-budget cost, the borrowed comparator, and the SR-instability. Fix one frozen tracker, report SR-conditioned-on-tracker, and run the >20% swap test on 2–3 tasks *before* any training — exactly as gate (b) proposes. This is non-negotiable: a 39.9% backend swing (HumanoidArena) is larger than any fidelity→SR slope the experiment is likely to find, so an unfrozen tracker would simply drown the signal.
4. **Take the Frontier Question seriously enough to test cheaply.** Before committing to penetration/foot-skating as the state variable, run a one-task check of whether a kinematically contact-perfect demo still mis-trains because the deployed policy's rollout leaves the demonstrated contact manifold. If on-policy reachable feasibility dominates off-policy reference fidelity, the entire cluster's metric is the wrong axis and no amount of cleaner references fixes it — this is a cheap experiment with the power to invalidate the whole program, so it should run first, not last.
5. **Preserve D3's durable mechanism even while discarding its perishable crossover.** The Historian's distinction is right: drop the transfer-vs-generalist operating point (it is a perishable number) but keep the phase-anchored-pathway-localization claim (Any2Any's PEFT-into-dynamics-sensitive-modules, line 1089, 1133–1137), which is a mechanism, not an operating point, and is the one D3 asset the fusion recommendation currently discards by accident.

---

### D1 (Direction review)

Topic: the FP bet that across retargeting methods (OmniRetarget / Human2Humanoid / NMR / DynaRetarget / ULTRA), the penetration/foot-skating → downstream-RL-SR regression is *monotone with a measurable slope and R²* on a shared task suite, so contact-fidelity is a *designed-against quantitative predictor* (not a correlate), and a per-contact-term ablation (object-only vs object+scene at *fixed* joint accuracy) isolates the load-bearing contact relationship.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER** (builds/deploys policies daily)
- *Core position*: The bet's deliverable is a regression curve, not a robot skill — and the single most expensive, brittle input to that curve (the shared downstream-RL pipeline run per method) is exactly what academics underestimate. Each point on the H2 curve is a full RL training run + sim eval for a *different* retargeter, and retargeting cost itself ranges from milliseconds (closed-form solver [[2602.01632\|2602.01632]], 0.587 ms) to per-reference RL loops ([[2605.06593\|ReActor]]) that do not scale.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: GMR ([[2510.02252\|2510.02252]]) already paid this cost once — it ran PHC/ProtoMotions/Unitree/GMR through one fixed BeyondMimic pipeline and found dynamic motions ('Dance 1', 'Run stop&go') hit **0% SR** under bad retargeting. That 0%-vs-90% cliff is the practitioner's warning: SR is likely *threshold/cliff-shaped*, not the smooth monotone slope H2 wants to regress.
- *Only this perspective says*: Budget the experiment by RL runs, not papers. 7 methods × multiple task classes × seeds is dozens of multi-GPU-day runs; if you cannot re-retarget the *same* motion set through all 7 (several are closed pipelines or need per-method tuning), the "shared task suite" is a fiction and the slope is confounded by motion-set differences, not contact fidelity.

**2. ACADEMIC** (knows the peer-reviewed literature)
- *Core position*: The card is honest that interaction-preservation is *consensus* and correctly narrows novelty to the *quantitative predictor* + *per-term isolation*. But the literature already strongly implies the answer to H2's existence ("does fidelity predict trainability?") — GMR and Stability-Aware ([[2510.04353\|2510.04353]], margin→resilience **R=0.69**) make the directional claim — so the contribution rests entirely on *monotonicity + slope + R² + which term*, a methodologically thin wedge for a top-venue paper.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: No card paper isolates *which* contact term at fixed joint accuracy. OmniRetarget bundles object+scene+foot in one interaction-mesh Laplacian; Human2Humanoid ([[2606.03476\|2606.03476]]) is end-effector-only (88.5% SR); TopoRetarget ([[2606.16272\|2606.16272]]) is hand-object-only (87.5%, 7.71 mm). They *span* the contact faces but none runs the controlled A/B at matched joint error. That gap is real.
- *Only this perspective says*: The falsifier is the strongest part of the card (H1/H2 falsifiers are crisp), but H2's covariate-control via MDS ([[2512.07248\|2512.07248]] Motion Difficulty Score) is the load-bearing methodological move — without it the slope is confounded by intrinsic motion hardness, and reviewers will attack exactly there.

**3. SKEPTIC** (thinks it's wrong or already solved)
- *Core position*: "Contact-fidelity predicts trainability" is so close to already-established that the residual is a measurement exercise, and the *monotone* form is probably false — GMR's 0%-vs-success cliff says the relationship is a step function (below a contact-violation threshold the policy simply can't learn), which makes "slope + R²" the wrong model and risks a flat-or-non-monotone falsifier firing on the headline hypothesis.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card itself concedes every cited method "never regresses contact-fidelity → SR across methods" — i.e. nobody did it because the directional result is assumed obvious and the *quantitative* version may be uninteresting once you have it. DynaRetarget ([[2602.06827\|2602.06827]]) already shows feasible→downstream gain (97.09% vs 79.41% raw) — a 2-point fidelity→SR relation exists in a *single* paper; the bet just multiplies points.
- *Only this perspective says*: The convenient omission is confound structure. Higher-fidelity retargeters (OmniRetarget, DynaRetarget) also produce *smoother, more feasible* references (NMR's −54% self-collisions, zero joint-jumps) — so any fidelity→SR slope may be a feasibility/smoothness→SR slope mislabeled as *contact*. Without holding feasibility fixed while varying *only* contact, H2's slope may not be a contact slope at all.

**4. ECONOMIST** (follows money/incentives)
- *Core position*: The retargeting-quality narrative is funded by the humanoid teleop/data-engine gold rush — Amazon FAR (OmniRetarget), Stanford (GMR), CMU/Meta FAIR (SPIDER [[2511.09484\|2511.09484]]) all need a clean "better references → cheaper RL" story to justify human-motion-capture spend. A *predictor* that lets you screen retargeters offline (before paying for RL) is directly monetizable, which is why this whitespace is attractive but also why a big lab may publish it first as a byproduct.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The benchmark substrate is consolidating fast (Mimicking-Bench [[2412.17730\|2412.17730]], TeleOpBench [[2505.12748\|2505.12748]], GMR's shared-suite protocol) — benchmark-builders profit when their suite becomes the axis everyone regresses against, so GMR's protocol becoming the H2 substrate is an incentive-aligned land-grab the card is downstream of, not upstream of.
- *Only this perspective says*: This is a "diagnostics" paper, not a "method" paper — academically lower-prestige, harder to defend against "so what, just use OmniRetarget." Frame it as a *retargeter-selection rule* (offline fidelity screen that saves N RL runs) to give it economic teeth, or it reads as a measurement note.

**5. HISTORIAN** (has seen prior waves)
- *Core position*: This is the motion-capture/animation field's "retargeting" problem (Gleicher 1998, interaction meshes — Ho et al. 2010) re-imported into robot RL, and the "fidelity predicts learnability" claim rhymes with the imitation-learning lineage where demonstration *quality/optimality* was repeatedly shown to gate downstream performance (DAgger-era covariate shift, BC-on-noisy-demos). The wave usually ends with the diagnostic absorbed into the method (people just bake the best objective in and stop measuring the predictor).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The interaction-mesh itself (OmniRetarget's Laplacian, TopoRetarget's Delaunay reuse) is a 15-year-old graphics tool; the *novel* robot-era twist is tying it to downstream RL SR — exactly the kind of "old representation, new trainability claim" that defined the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycles. Those cycles rewarded whoever *quantified* the tradeoff cleanly, then were forgotten once one objective won.
- *Only this perspective says*: Predictors have a short half-life. The moment a dominant retargeter (likely OmniRetarget's recipe + GMR's protocol) wins, the slope/R² becomes trivia. The durable contribution is the *which-contact-term-is-load-bearing* result (H4), because that informs objective *design* and survives the method consolidation; the slope (H2) does not.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes**:
   - *SKEPTIC vs ACADEMIC on the model form*: Skeptic says GMR's 0%-vs-success cliff makes the relationship a *step/threshold*, so "monotone slope + R²" is the wrong model and H2's own falsifier ("non-monotone → decorative") may fire on the headline. Academic treats monotone-with-slope as a defensible, reviewable claim given MDS covariate control. Specific clash: is fidelity→SR *smooth-monotone* or *threshold*?
   - *SKEPTIC vs CARD on what the slope measures*: Skeptic says higher-fidelity retargeters are confounded with smoother/more-feasible references (NMR −54% self-collisions, zero joint-jumps), so any slope is feasibility→SR mislabeled as contact→SR. The card's H5 actually *concedes* feasibility and interaction are separable mechanisms — which means H2's cross-method slope, run over methods that differ in BOTH, is confounded unless feasibility is held fixed.
   - *PRACTITIONER vs ECONOMIST on framing*: Practitioner says the deliverable is an expensive curve of limited deploy value; Economist says re-framed as an offline retargeter-selection rule it has direct cost-saving value. Clash on whether the output is a robot capability or a screening diagnostic.

2. **Strongest vs weakest**:
   - *Strongest*: SKEPTIC — it lands a confound (contact vs feasibility/smoothness) that the card's *own* H5 admits is a real mechanistic split, plus a model-form attack grounded in GMR's measured 0% cliff. Both are specific and evidence-bound.
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — insightful on half-life but the "old graphics tool" framing doesn't threaten the bet's validity, only its prestige; its strongest point (H4 is the durable contribution) is constructive, not a refutation.

3. **Resolving question**: When you regress downstream SR on contact-violation *across methods*, does the slope survive after partialling out feasibility/smoothness (self-collision rate, joint-jump count) and intrinsic motion difficulty (MDS) — i.e. is there a *contact-specific* slope, or only a generic reference-quality slope?

4. **Universal agreement** (likely true): Reference/contact fidelity *does* gate downstream RL — bad retargeting can drive SR to 0% on dynamic tasks (GMR), and better references cut reward engineering (OmniRetarget, GMR, DynaRetarget). The *directional* claim is safe; only its *functional form* and *contact-specificity* are contested.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed)**: Real-robot validation of the predictor. Every cited fidelity→SR signal is in *simulation* downstream RL; nobody asks whether the contact-fidelity → *sim2real-transfer* SR relationship holds, where the predictor would actually earn deploy value. The slope could be real in sim and vanish (or invert) under the sim2real gap that OmniRetarget/TopoRetarget cross zero-shot but never tie back to a fidelity metric.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** D1's central scientific bet is unusually well-targeted: it concedes the crowded half (interaction-preservation is consensus, owned by OmniRetarget/Human2Humanoid/MeshMimic/TopoRetarget) and stakes novelty only on what no cited paper measures — a cross-method, *monotone, slope+R²* contact-fidelity → downstream-RL-SR predictor on a shared suite, plus a fixed-joint-accuracy ablation isolating *which* contact term is load-bearing. A literature check (alphaxiv discover, two queries) returns only the papers already in the card plus HDMI and a sim-to-online-RL design study (2602.20220) on a different axis: nobody has run this regression or this controlled per-term isolation, so the whitespace is real. The risk is not novelty but *form and confound*: GMR's measured 0%-vs-success cliff suggests the relationship may be a threshold rather than a smooth monotone slope, and because high-fidelity retargeters are simultaneously smoother and more feasible, any cross-method slope may be a generic reference-quality slope mislabeled as contact-specific. The bet is committable, but only if reframed as a controlled, confound-partialled diagnostic with a real-robot anchor — not a method-beating result.

**5 key findings (ranked by reliability):**
1. *Directional claim is safe; the field already believes fidelity gates trainability* — GMR (0% SR cliff), DynaRetarget (97.09% vs 79.41%), Stability-Aware (R=0.69). [Supported: all five perspectives. Challenged by: none on direction — Skeptic only on *form*.]
2. *The precise bet (cross-method monotone slope/R² + per-contact-term isolation) is genuinely unclaimed* — alphaxiv returns only in-card papers + HDMI; GMR isolates retargeting-from-training but reports no slope/R²; no paper ablates contact term at fixed joint accuracy. [Supported: Academic, alphaxiv evidence. Challenged by: Skeptic — "residual is a measurement exercise."]
3. *The monotone functional form is the most likely failure mode* — GMR's 0%-vs-success cliff implies a threshold, and H2's own falsifier fires on non-monotonicity. [Supported: Skeptic, Practitioner. Challenged by: Academic — defensible with MDS covariate control.]
4. *The slope may be confounded: contact-fidelity is entangled with feasibility/smoothness across methods* — NMR's −54% self-collisions and the card's own H5 admit interaction and feasibility are separable mechanisms. [Supported: Skeptic. Challenged by: card's H4/H5 design intent to separate them — but only if run at fixed feasibility.]
5. *The experiment is RL-run-bound and the "shared suite" is fragile* — several retargeters are closed or need per-method tuning; re-retargeting one motion set through all 7 is the true cost and precondition. [Supported: Practitioner, Economist. Challenged by: none.]

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner (GMR's 0% cliff = expensive, threshold-shaped) and Skeptic (monotone form is wrong) and Economist (this is a *selection diagnostic*, not a method) converge on the same redesign: the valuable, defensible object is not a continuous slope but a *thresholded retargeter-selection rule* — "below this contact-violation level, expect trainable; above, expect collapse" — which is both honest about the cliff and economically framed as an offline screen that saves RL runs.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to a narrowed version.** Do not commit to the headline "monotone slope + R²" as the primary claim. Concrete next experiment (the cheapest decisive de-risk): take *one* fixed motion set, retarget it with exactly two methods that match on feasibility/smoothness but differ in a *single* contact term (object-only vs object+scene — OmniRetarget's scene term ablated vs full), hold joint-tracking error matched, run the *same* downstream RL on Mimicking-Bench scene-tasks vs tabletop tasks (this is H4 + the H1 matched-joint-accuracy control fused). If the object+scene retarget beats object-only on climbing/leaning with the gap near-zero on tabletop, you have the load-bearing, design-relevant, confound-controlled result (H4 — the historian's "durable" contribution) for a fraction of the 7-method curve's cost. Only after that lands should you expand to the full multi-method curve, and report it as a *thresholded selection rule with feasibility/MDS partialled out*, not a bare slope.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Does the contact-fidelity predictor hold against the *sim2real* gap — i.e. does lower retargeting contact-violation predict higher *real-robot zero-shot* SR (not just sim RL SR)? If yes, the predictor becomes a deployable retargeter-selection tool and the direction is a strong paper; if the slope lives only in sim and washes out on hardware (the universal blind spot), the bet is a simulator artifact regardless of how clean the regression looks.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. The briefing is unusually self-aware — it has already pre-empted most of the easy objections (confound, threshold form, RL-run cost). My job is to test whether the pre-emption is real or rhetorical, and whether the surviving claim is worth a paper.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

1. **Directional claim — fidelity gates trainability. Score: 9/10.**
   The field genuinely believes this and the card supplies three independent data points (GMR 0% cliff, DynaRetarget 97.09 vs 79.41, Stability-Aware R=0.69). The only reason this is not a 10 is that "the field already believes it" is exactly what makes it *unpublishable on its own* — high reliability, low novelty, and the briefing is honest about that.

2. **The precise bet (cross-method monotone slope/R² + per-term isolation) is unclaimed. Score: 6/10.**
   The whitespace is plausibly real, but the evidence is thin: a two-query alphaxiv discover sweep is a *weak* negative. Absence-of-evidence from two queries is not evidence-of-absence — GMR already runs the shared-suite protocol and reports per-sequence SR; turning that into a regression is a small step someone may have taken in a venue alphaxiv did not surface (RSS/CoRL workshop, a benchmark paper's appendix). The Skeptic's charge that the residual is a *measurement exercise* rather than a *discovery* is the deeper risk and it is not retired. Genuinely-unclaimed is believable; genuinely-*worth-claiming* is not established.

3. **Monotone form is the most likely failure mode. Score: 8/10.**
   This is the briefing's strongest analytical move. GMR's 0%-vs-success cliff is direct evidence the relationship is thresholded, not a smooth slope, and H2's own falsifier ("flat or non-monotone") fires on exactly this. The downgrade from 9 is that "threshold" and "monotone" are not mutually exclusive — a saturating monotone (sigmoid) is both, and the briefing slightly overstates the conflict to motivate its redesign.

4. **The slope may be confounded with feasibility/smoothness. Score: 8/10.**
   This is correct and important. High-fidelity retargeters are co-engineered to be smoother and more feasible (NMR's −54% self-collisions, the whole feasibility-by-distribution row), so a bare cross-method slope is almost certainly a generic reference-quality slope. The card's H4/H5 *intend* to separate these but, as the briefing notes, only the fixed-feasibility version is clean — and the card does not currently mandate matching on feasibility, only on joint accuracy. The confound is real and only partially addressed by the card as written.

5. **Experiment is RL-run-bound and the shared suite is fragile. Score: 8/10.**
   Correct and under-weighted in the card's own Risks block. Re-retargeting one motion set through 7 methods, several closed-source (Unitree) or needing per-method tuning, is the true cost and precondition, and it is a *throughput* risk (N RL training runs × M methods) that the card lists only as "RL-in-the-loop retargeter is costly" — which is a narrower point. The minus-2 is only because cost is an engineering risk, not a scientific-validity risk; it threatens feasibility, not correctness.

**Mean = 7.8. Min = 6.**

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 2 (score 6): **the bet is genuinely unclaimed.** The whole direction's novelty rests on a two-query alphaxiv discover returning only in-card papers + HDMI. That is the load-bearing claim and the weakest evidence in the briefing. To verify, I would want: (a) a targeted search for "retargeting quality predicts downstream RL success" / "reference fidelity downstream trainability regression" beyond alphaxiv — Semantic Scholar + the GMR and BeyondMimic citation graphs specifically, since GMR's shared-suite protocol is the obvious place someone fits this curve; (b) a check of the GMR paper's own appendix and any follow-on, since they already have the per-sequence SR table and the slope is one regression away; (c) confirmation that HDMI and 2602.20220 genuinely sit off-axis rather than being near-misses the briefing dismissed too quickly. Until (a)–(c) are done, "the whitespace is real" is asserted at confidence 6, not demonstrated.

##### 3. Bias check

**The Skeptic over-dominated** the synthesis. Findings 3, 4, and the entire "hidden cross-perspective connection" and "actionable insight" sections are Skeptic-led (form is wrong, slope is confounded, reframe as a threshold selection rule). The Practitioner and Economist are present but largely *recruited to ratify the Skeptic's redesign* — the briefing explicitly says all three "converge on the same redesign," which is convergence engineered by selection, not three independent perspectives arriving at one answer. The Academic is the *only* voice pushing back (defending monotone-with-MDS-control, defending the residual as a contribution) and it is consistently marked "Challenged by." A genuinely balanced synthesis would have given the Academic a real win somewhere; here it functions as a foil. Net: the analysis is sharp but tilted toward de-risking-into-a-screen, which conveniently is also the *least ambitious and most defensible* outcome — a Skeptic's natural attractor.

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The downstream-RL-algorithm / training-stochasticity perspective** — call it the Experimentalist. The entire bet treats "downstream-RL-SR" as a clean dependent variable, but RL success rate is notoriously high-variance across seeds, reward shaping, and PPO/architecture choices. A cross-method slope with R² is only meaningful if the SR-per-method noise floor (seed variance) is smaller than the cross-method fidelity signal. If re-running the *same* retarget with 5 seeds spans 60–90% SR, no regression across methods is identifiable regardless of how clean the retargeting metrics are. This changes conclusions: it means the cheapest decisive de-risk is *not* the two-method object-vs-scene experiment the briefing proposes, but a **single-method seed-variance study** — measure the SR confidence interval for one fixed retarget before committing to a regression whose effect size may live entirely inside the error bars. This perspective also reframes the Frontier question: the sim2real gap is one washout risk; seed/reward variance is a *more proximal* one that kills the regression before hardware is ever reached.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, self-critical briefing. It correctly identifies that the novelty is real-but-narrow, names the two killer confounds (threshold form, feasibility entanglement), and proposes a genuinely cheaper decisive experiment (the H4+H1 fused two-method object-vs-scene control at matched joint accuracy). That fused experiment is the best idea in the document and is correctly identified as the durable contribution. It loses the A-range for three reasons: the novelty claim rests on a weak two-query negative; the synthesis is Skeptic-tilted and manufactures cross-perspective "convergence"; and it misses that downstream-RL seed variance — not just sim2real — may be the variable that determines whether *any* regression is identifiable.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Harden the novelty check before committing.** Two alphaxiv queries is insufficient for a direction whose entire claim is "no one measured this." Search the GMR/BeyondMimic citation graph and benchmark-paper appendices specifically; confirm HDMI and 2602.20220 are off-axis, not near-misses.
2. **Add a seed-variance pilot as the *true* cheapest de-risk.** Before the two-method experiment, measure SR confidence intervals for one fixed retarget across seeds/rewards. If the noise floor swamps plausible cross-method deltas, the regression is dead on arrival and you have saved the entire 7-method curve.
3. **Mandate fixed-feasibility, not just fixed-joint-accuracy, in H1/H4.** The confound (Finding 4) is only neutralized if the two compared retargets match on feasibility/smoothness *and* joint accuracy while differing in a single contact term. The card currently fixes joint accuracy only; make matched feasibility an explicit precondition or the slope remains a generic-quality slope.
4. **Reframe the headline as a thresholded selection rule, not a bare monotone slope** (the briefing's own conclusion, which I endorse) — "below this contact-violation level, expect trainable; above, expect collapse" is honest about GMR's cliff and economically framed as an offline screen that saves RL runs.
5. **Promote the sim2real anchor from Frontier-question to a required claim.** A predictor that lives only in sim is a simulator artifact; at least one real-robot zero-shot point (TopoRetarget and ULTRA already report sim2real, so the hooks exist) should gate the paper's central claim.

### D2 (Direction review)

Topic: D2's first-principles bet — *whole-body-captured demos (simultaneous base+arm, via teleop or robot-free egocentric rig) beat fixed-base-arm-demos + a separate locomotion controller on coupled tasks, with the gap near-zero on fixed-base tasks (H1); and each capture modality captures a different half of the coupling — MoCap/exo the loco half, VR/vision the manip half, so no single interface dominates (H4)*. The load-bearing contribution is the **controlled isolation/subtraction** the system papers assume but never run.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The "interface determines whether the coupling is in the data" claim is operationally true and they have felt it — a fixed-base ACT/DP trained on arm-only demos genuinely cannot reposition the base mid-task. But the proposed contribution (a clean controlled subtraction on a legged humanoid) is a *measurement* paper, and the field already pays for whole-body capture rigs regardless of whether the subtraction is published.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2401.02117|Mobile-ALOHA]]'s Wipe-Wine 50%→95% with 50 demos via *static* co-training shows the practitioner reality — coupled teleop already works, and the lab-bound cognitive load (commanding base + two arms) is the real ceiling, not the absence of a subtraction proof. [[2510.08807|Humanoid-Everyday]]'s GR00T N1.5 at **0%** on hard loco-manip is the practitioner's blunt fact: even 10.3k teleop trajectories don't crack it, so data *quantity* via teleop is not the bottleneck the card implies.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The H1 baseline ("fixed-base arm demos + a separate locomotion controller") is hard to build *fairly* — a real separate locomotion controller (e.g. an RL walking policy) is itself a strong system, and [[2409.15095|MoMa-Teleop]] (base delegated to a pre-trained N²M² RL agent, operator commands EE only) shows the decoupled baseline can be *up to 45% faster* and 5-demo-transferable. If you under-build the baseline, the +51pp is an artifact of a weak controller, not of the coupling.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The qualitative H1 result is no longer whitespace — it has been shown on wheeled bases by multiple groups; what survives is the *legged-humanoid, stratified, scaled* version. The card's own framing concedes this ("the bet's capture-interface half is taken").
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2509.04443|EMMA]] (Georgia Tech) replaces 1 hr teleop with 1 hr egocentric human data and gets **82% vs 52%** Handover-Wine (+30pp) and **54%** generalization where Mobile ALOHA fully fails — that ablation *is* the controlled subtraction, on a wheeled mobile-manipulator. [[2509.03222]] (TU Darmstadt, *not in the card*) runs the cleanest version: 50 trajectories each from a coupled Whole-Body Controller vs a decoupled Separate-Body Controller on the *same* drawer task, same Diffusion Policy, reporting **SBC 0% vs WBC 80%** and explicitly attributing SBC failure to "the lack of base-arm coupling in the motion signals." [[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]]'s +51pp generalization (82% vs 31%) is the humanoid-VR datapoint.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The card cites EgoHumanoid's +51pp as *evidence the coupling is data-bound*, but EgoHumanoid co-trains human + robot data and ablates view/action *alignment*, not coupling-vs-no-coupling — its +51pp is partly a human-data-diversity effect, not a clean coupling subtraction. H2 (alignment, not raw demos, drives the +51pp) and H1 (coupling drives it) are *competing* explanations of the same number, and the card lists both as confirmatory.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: H1 is already solved qualitatively and the "controlled subtraction nobody runs" framing is wrong: [[2509.03222]] ran it (coupled 80% vs decoupled 0%), EMMA ran it on a wheeled base, and the card's own anchor [[2603.03243|HoMMI]] (Stanford/TRI) learns whole-body mobile manipulation from robot-free human demos. What's left is an incremental "do it on legs, stratify by base-travel" delta.
- *Strongest counterargument / ignored evidence*: The card never grapples with the *confound* in every existing +51pp: human/whole-body data is also *more diverse, more numerous, and cheaper-per-demo*, so a positive H1 result is over-determined — you cannot attribute it to the coupling unless you hold scene diversity and demo count fixed, which none of the cited rows do. The falsifier ("gap near-zero on fixed-base tasks") is the only thing that makes it a coupling claim rather than a data-volume claim, and that stratum is exactly where the card has *no* cited result.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: If [[2509.03222]]'s 0%-vs-80% (N=5, "not statistically representative") already convinces reviewers the coupling is data-bound, your legged-humanoid replication is a confirmation, not a discovery — the novelty must live entirely in **H4** (which-modality-captures-which-half), which *no* paper has run.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The whole-body-data narrative is driven by humanoid-company capital (AgiBot, Unitree, X-Humanoid, NVIDIA, Midea) that needs a *scalable data story* to justify valuations; "robot-free egocentric capture" is the narrative that says data scales like human hours, not robot hours. A controlled-subtraction paper has low commercial value (it doesn't ship a rig) but high *academic-differentiation* value precisely because everyone else is racing to ship rigs.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card lists ~30 capture-system papers in ~12 months ([[2602.06643|HMI]], [[2510.03022|HumanoidExo]], [[2511.02832|TWIST2]], [[2603.14327|OmniClone]], [[2502.13013|HOMIE]] at *~$500*, [[2503.10554|NuExo]] *5.2kg portable*) — a classic rig-arms-race where vendors compete on hardware throughput (HMI's 3× teleop throughput, HumanoidExo's 195-demos-=-200-real), not on the scientific question of whether the coupling is data-bound. The benchmark saturation signal is real: [[2606.17833|HumanoidArena]] shows cross-GMT transfer drops **39.9%** with 0% retention — i.e. the field is already chasing a moving benchmark target.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: A measurement paper that proves "no single modality dominates" (H4) is *adversarial to every vendor's marketing* (each claims their rig is best), which is why it stays un-run and is therefore genuinely defensible whitespace — but also why it will get little industry uptake or co-authorship.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the imitation-learning re-run of the end-to-end-vs-modular swing and the "data-in-the-trajectory" lesson from RL. Whole-body-coupled-capture vs fixed-base+controller is structurally identical to monolithic-policy vs hierarchy debates; history says *both* win in different regimes and the interesting result is always the *crossover*, not a single +51pp.
- *Strongest evidence*: The Mobile ALOHA → OmniH2O → TWIST2 → OmniClone lineage ([[2406.08858|OmniH2O]] as the root) mirrors the DAgger→teleop→shadowing progression; [[2406.10454|HumanPlus]] (single-RGB shadowing) and [[2503.13441|PH2D]] (~27k egocentric, ~100% relative OOD gain) recapitulate the "human video is the cheapest demo" wave that recurs every few years and *always* hits the same wall: missing proprioception/force (the card's Risk-3).
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Prior waves show the "missing half" framing (H4) usually resolves not as "two modalities each own a half" but as "one modality plus a cheap refiner subsumes the other" — exactly the [[2605.20373|SUGAR]] physics-refiner / [[2509.16757|HDMI]] interaction-reward pattern (H5). H4's clean trade-off is the *hypothesis history most often falsifies*.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Academic vs Skeptic on whitespace*: Academic says the legged-humanoid + stratified version survives; Skeptic says [[2509.03222]]'s 80%-vs-0% + EMMA's +30pp already settle H1 qualitatively, leaving only H4. Specific clash: is "do the subtraction on legs, stratified by base-travel" a *discovery* or a *replication*?
   - *Practitioner vs Card on the H1 baseline*: Card treats "fixed-base + separate locomotion controller" as obviously inferior; Practitioner cites [[2409.15095|MoMa-Teleop]] (base-delegated-to-RL, EE-only operator) as *faster* and competitive — if the decoupled baseline is built strong, H1's gap may collapse on many tasks.
   - *H1 vs H2 (internal contradiction)*: Card lists EgoHumanoid's +51pp as evidence for *both* "coupling is data-bound" (H1) and "alignment drives the +51pp" (H2). These are competing causal attributions of one number; both cannot be the primary mechanism.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — every claim ties to a real cited metric (EMMA +30pp, EgoHumanoid +51pp, [[2509.03222]] 80-vs-0) and it correctly locates the surviving whitespace without overclaiming.
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — illuminating on pattern but its "crossover, not +51pp" and "modality+refiner subsumes" predictions are analogical, not yet grounded in a D2-specific metric; useful as a risk lens, not as evidence.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   - *On a legged humanoid, with scene diversity and demo count held fixed, does the whole-body-vs-fixed-base+controller gap go to near-zero on tasks requiring no in-task base travel?* If yes, the result is a genuine coupling claim (not a data-volume artifact) and survives the Skeptic; if the gap persists on fixed-base tasks, the +51pp is data-diversity, not coupling, and the bet is mis-attributed.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   - A fixed-base arm-only interface structurally cannot *demonstrate* in-task base-arm coupling, and whole-body/coupled capture data trains policies that reposition the base mid-task where decoupled data does not — corroborated independently by [[2509.04443|EMMA]] (wheeled), [[2509.03222]] (wheeled, coupled 80% vs decoupled 0%), and [[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]] (humanoid). The *qualitative* coupling-is-data-bound claim is essentially settled.

5. **The blind spot (no perspective in the card addressed).**
   - **Sim2real / proprioception-force closure of the egocentric route.** Every robot-free egocentric rig omits the robot's internal state (force, joint torque, contact) — the card flags it as Risk-3 but treats co-training as the fix. None of H1–H6 *measures* how much of the +51pp survives once the policy must close the loop on real contact/force on a *balancing* humanoid (vs a statically-stable wheeled base). The wheeled→legged transfer of these results is assumed, not tested, and balance is exactly where egocentric human data has no proprioceptive analog.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** D2's first principle (coupling lives in the interface, not the policy) is correct and now *multiply corroborated* — but the corroboration has overtaken the card's "controlled subtraction nobody runs" framing. [[2509.03222]] (TU Darmstadt, not yet in the vault) ran a small coupled-vs-decoupled subtraction (80% vs 0%), and [[2509.04443|EMMA]] ran the human-replaces-teleop version on a wheeled base (+30pp, +54pp generalization). The genuinely un-run, defensible contribution is therefore *not* "prove the coupling is data-bound" (largely done) but the **legged-humanoid stratified subtraction** (gap must vanish on fixed-base tasks to rule out the data-volume confound) plus the **H4 modality decomposition** (which-interface-captures-which-half), which no paper has measured and which is adversarial to every vendor's rig-marketing. Commit only if you re-center the novelty on H4 + the stratified-falsifier-on-legs; the bare H1 is now a replication.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *(Highest)* The coupling-is-data-bound claim (H1, qualitative) is real and reproduced on wheeled bases. Supported by Academic, Practitioner, Historian; *challenged as already-solved* by Skeptic. Evidence: [[2509.03222]] 80%-vs-0%, [[2509.04443|EMMA]] +30pp, [[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]] +51pp.
2. The robot-free whole-body *capture pipeline* is consensus, not contribution. Supported by all five; the card itself concedes it. Evidence: [[2602.06643|HMI]], [[2510.03022|HumanoidExo]], [[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]], [[2603.03243|HoMMI]].
3. The +51pp is confounded by scene diversity + demo count; only the *near-zero-gap-on-fixed-base* stratum makes it a coupling claim. Supported by Skeptic, Academic; the card's falsifier already encodes this but cites no result on that stratum. (Moderate reliability — it's a design requirement, not yet a measured fact.)
4. H4 (modality captures different halves) is the true whitespace and is un-run. Supported by Economist, Academic; *challenged* by Historian (predicts "one modality + refiner subsumes the other," cf. H5/[[2605.20373|SUGAR]], [[2509.16757|HDMI]]). Evidence: no paper decomposes [[2505.12748|TeleOpBench]]'s 4 modalities by loco-vs-manip half; [[2509.03222]] varies controller-coupling but not capture-modality.
5. *(Lowest)* The H1 baseline strength is decisive and currently under-specified. Raised by Practitioner; a base-delegated-RL baseline ([[2409.15095|MoMa-Teleop]], up to 45% faster) could collapse H1's gap on low-base-travel tasks, so a weak controller would inflate the result.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "no-single-modality-dominates result is adversarial to every vendor" and the Historian's "modality+refiner subsumes the other half" converge: the reason H4 is un-run is *both* that it embarrasses rig-vendors *and* that the field's reflex is to bolt a refiner onto one modality (SUGAR's physics refiner, HDMI's interaction reward) rather than admit a modality owns only half — so H4's clean trade-off may be a hypothesis the field is structurally disinclined to test *and* historically prone to falsify. That makes H4 simultaneously the most novel and the most at-risk sub-bet.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to a narrowed bet.** Do *not* commit to the bare H1 (it is now a wheeled-base replication via EMMA + [[2509.03222]]). Re-center on two un-run pieces: (a) the **stratified legged-humanoid subtraction** where the headline result is the *falsifier* — coupled-capture beats fixed-base+controller on high-base-travel tasks but ties on zero-base-travel tasks, ruling out the data-volume confound; and (b) **H4** as the lead contribution. Concrete next experiment: on [[2407.07788|BiGym]]'s 40 mobile-bimanual tasks (its whole-body-vs-bimanual action-mode switch is the coupling-on/off knob), collect matched-count demos from *two* modalities (one loco-strong: exoskeleton/[[2510.03022|HumanoidExo]]-style or MoCap; one manip-strong: VR/[[2602.10106|EgoHumanoid]]-style), train identical policies, and report a 2×2 surface: {loco-modality, manip-modality} × {high, low base-travel} SR. Pre-register the *baseline*: the fixed-base arm + a *strong* RL locomotion controller ([[2409.15095|MoMa-Teleop]]-grade), not a toy one, so a positive H1 cannot be dismissed as a weak-baseline artifact. Before any of this, ingest [[2509.03222]] into the KH and cite it as the direct prior — the card currently omits the single closest published instantiation of H1.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Does whole-body-captured *kinematic* data transfer the coupling to a *balancing* humanoid that must close the loop on contact force and proprioception — or is the +51pp a property of statically-stable (wheeled) bases that collapses once the legs must absorb the manipulation wrench? If the egocentric route loses the loco half precisely on the balance-critical tasks (squat, lean, push), then H4's "MoCap/exo capture the loco half" is not a modality choice but a hard ceiling, and the entire robot-free-egocentric narrative for *legged* whole-body manipulation needs a force/proprioception channel D2 currently routes around via co-training.
