#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. I read D2 (Whole-Body.md L986-1076), the sibling analyst synthesis (015-D2-1-analyst.md), and independently checked the load-bearing external claim (2509.03222) against its arXiv abstract. My headline disagreement with the briefing is below; the per-finding scores follow.*

**Headline finding before scoring.** The briefing's entire pivot — "the bare H1 is now a replication," "prove the coupling is data-bound (largely done)" — rests on two pillars: EMMA (2509.04443) and 2509.03222. The first pillar is solid (EMMA's +30pp / +54% generalization are confirmed in KH and the card, on a wheeled Aria-glasses mobile-manipulator). **The second pillar is materially mischaracterized.** 2509.03222 (Bianchi Moyen, Krohn, Peters, Prasad, Chalvatzaki — TU Darmstadt, confirmed) is titled "The Role of Embodiment in Intuitive Whole-Body Teleoperation for Mobile Manipulation," and per its own abstract is *primarily a teleoperation / embodiment / visual-feedback operator-workload user-study*. Its coupled-vs-decoupled imitation-learning comparison is, verbatim, a *"preliminary experiment"* — the analyst's own Prompt-1 (line 14) flags it as N=5 and "not statistically representative," and the abstract reports **no quantitative success rate at all**. So the "80% vs 0%" that the CEO summary promotes to "the cleanest version" of the H1 subtraction is an underpowered side-finding inside a workload paper, not a published head-to-head that settles H1. That single overstatement is what tips Finding #1 from "promising corroboration" to "largely done," and it propagates into the actionable insight ("do NOT commit to the bare H1") and the grade.

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability)**

1. **Coupling-is-data-bound (H1, qualitative) is real and reproduced on wheeled bases — 6/10.** The *direction* of the claim is well supported (EMMA verified; the first-principles argument that a fixed-base interface cannot emit a coupled trajectory is sound and conceded across perspectives). But the briefing's strength rating ("Highest," "reproduced") over-leans on 2509.03222's preliminary N=5 result, which the abstract does not even quantify, and on EgoHumanoid's +51pp — which (the analyst itself notes, line 15) is a co-training + alignment ablation, *not* a coupling-vs-no-coupling subtraction. One genuine wheeled datapoint (EMMA) + one preliminary side-finding + one confounded humanoid number is "promising," not "settled." Docking 2-3 points for the replication overclaim.

2. **Robot-free whole-body capture pipeline is consensus, not contribution — 9/10.** Strongest finding. The card concedes it explicitly (L997: "The bet's *capture interface* half is taken"), all five perspectives agree, and the rig roster (HMI, HumanoidExo, EgoHumanoid, HoMMI, plus ~25 more rows) is real and verified in-card. Little to challenge.

3. **The +51pp is confounded by scene-diversity + demo-count; only the near-zero-gap-on-fixed-base stratum makes it a coupling claim — 8/10.** The most intellectually valuable finding and correctly self-rated "moderate." The confound is real (whole-body/human data is also more diverse, more numerous, cheaper-per-demo), the falsifier is the right instrument, and the briefing is honest that no cited result occupies that stratum. I'd rate it higher except it is, by its own admission, a design requirement, not a measured fact — so its reliability *as a finding about the world* is intrinsically capped.

4. **H4 (modality captures different halves) is the true whitespace and un-run — 7/10.** I agree the decomposition is genuinely un-run (no paper splits TeleOpBench's 4 modalities by loco-vs-manip half), and the Economist/Historian convergence is the sharpest insight in the briefing. The 7 reflects a real hazard the briefing under-weights: H4 as stated may be *ill-posed*, not merely un-run. "Modality X captures the loco half better" presumes loco and manip are separable axes of a demonstration; if the coupling is the *interaction term* (base motion that extends reach, balance during a manipulation wrench), then no modality "owns" a clean half and the 2x2 surface has no clean main effects to report. The Historian's "one modality + refiner subsumes the other" is one failure mode; ill-posedness is a second the briefing doesn't name.

5. **H1 baseline strength is decisive and under-specified — 8/10.** Correctly rated "Lowest" but it is one of the *best-grounded* cautions in the document. MoMa-Teleop (2409.15095, verified in KH, base delegated to a pre-trained N²M² RL agent, operator commands EE only, up to 45% faster) is a concrete, published, *strong* decoupled baseline that could collapse H1's gap on low-base-travel tasks. The briefing's pre-registration fix (a MoMa-Teleop-grade controller, not a toy) is exactly right. The only reason it is not 9 is that "decisive" is asserted, not demonstrated — we don't yet know the gap is baseline-sensitive, we know it *could* be.

*Mean of the five scores: (6 + 9 + 8 + 7 + 8) / 5 = 7.6. Lowest: 6 (Finding 1).*

**2. WEAKEST LINK.** Finding #1, specifically the sub-claim that 2509.03222 "ran the cleanest version" of the controlled subtraction and that H1 is therefore "largely done." What would verify it: the actual N, task count, and reported success rates of 2509.03222's imitation-learning experiment (the abstract supplies none and labels it "preliminary"; the analyst cites N=5, "not statistically representative"). Until someone reads the paper's IL section and confirms a *powered* coupled-vs-decoupled SR comparison on a shared task with matched demo count, the correct statement is "H1 has one wheeled-base datapoint (EMMA) plus a preliminary, underpowered side-result," not "H1 is a replication." This is not a nitpick: it is the hinge the whole "re-center off H1" recommendation swings on. (Secondary weak link: the briefing treats the wheeled→legged transfer of EMMA's result as the *only* open piece, but never establishes that EMMA's +30pp would even hold on a balancing humanoid — see Missing Perspective.)

**3. BIAS CHECK — which perspective over-dominated.** The **Skeptic over-dominated**, with the **Academic** as its enabler. The synthesis adopts the Skeptic's "H1 is already solved" verdict as the organizing frame (CEO summary, Finding #1's "challenged as already-solved," the entire "DE-RISK, do NOT commit to bare H1" actionable), and it does so by accepting the Academic's citation of 2509.03222 at a strength the source does not bear. The Practitioner (baseline-strength), Economist (incentives), and Historian (crossover/refiner) are present but subordinated to "H1 is a replication, pivot to H4." The tell: Finding #1 is rated *Highest reliability* yet is the finding most damaged by the source-verification above — the briefing's confidence ranking is inverted relative to its evidential robustness. A balanced synthesis would have rated the *capture-is-consensus* finding (Finding 2) highest and held H1's "done-ness" as contested, since exactly one of its three cited supports is a clean, powered, on-claim result.

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE — a 6th angle that would change the conclusion.** A **Controls / dynamicist (legged-locomotion) perspective.** Every perspective in the synthesis treats the demonstration as *kinematic data* and asks "is the coupling in the data?" None asks the dynamicist's question: *the coupling that matters on a balancing humanoid is a force/momentum coupling (the manipulation wrench perturbs the centroidal dynamics; the legs must counter it), and egocentric/teleop capture records kinematics, not the contact wrench or the proprioceptive correction.* This reframes the whole card: on a wheeled, statically-stable base (EMMA, Mobile ALOHA, MoMa-Teleop — note all the verified H1 evidence is wheeled) the loco-manip coupling is largely *kinematic reach extension*, which whole-body kinematic capture genuinely supplies. On legs, the load-bearing coupling is *dynamic balance under a manipulation wrench*, which kinematic capture structurally cannot demonstrate — co-training on robot proprioception (the card's Risk-3 fix) is then not a patch but the actual mechanism, and the "robot-free egocentric" narrative quietly does not apply to the balance-critical stratum. This perspective converts the briefing's "frontier question" from a closing flourish into the *central risk that should re-rank the findings*: it would demote H1-on-legs (the proposed contribution) from "the defensible un-run delta" to "possibly un-runnable as a robot-free capture claim," and promote a force/proprioception channel from a side-risk to a prerequisite. The briefing gestures at this (the Frontier question, the analyst's blind-spot #5) but never lets it touch the recommendation.

**5. OVERALL GRADE — B.**

A strong, well-organized synthesis with one genuinely excellent move (the falsifier-as-headline reframing in Finding 3 and the Economist×Historian convergence) and one disqualifying-from-an-A flaw: it builds its central pivot ("H1 is a replication") on a source it overstates, and it ranks that overstated finding as its *most* reliable. A Stanford committee would not fail it — the direction is real, the capture-consensus call is correct, the H4 whitespace is correctly located, and the baseline caution is sharp — but it would send it back for revision.

*Top things to fix, in priority order:*
1. **Correct the 2509.03222 claim.** Downgrade it from "the cleanest version of the subtraction (80% vs 0%)" to "a preliminary, underpowered (N=5, not statistically representative) IL side-finding inside an operator-workload study," and re-rate Finding #1 accordingly (it is *not* the highest-reliability finding). Still ingest it into KH and cite it — it is a legitimate prior — but as supporting, not settling, evidence.
2. **Stop calling H1 "a replication."** With exactly one clean, powered, on-claim datapoint (EMMA, wheeled), H1's *qualitative* direction is corroborated but its *legged, stratified, baseline-controlled* form is unrun and non-trivial. The honest framing is "H1's wheeled-kinematic form is supported; its legged-dynamic form is the open contribution," which is *also a stronger pitch* than "pivot away from H1."
3. **Add the dynamicist lens and let it re-rank.** Make the force/momentum coupling the explicit reason the legged stratum is hard, and treat the proprioception/force channel as a prerequisite the design must include, not a co-training afterthought. This also sharpens H4: state whether you expect clean modality *main effects* or whether the coupling is an interaction term no modality cleanly owns (the ill-posedness risk).
4. **Pre-register the strong baseline** (MoMa-Teleop-grade) and the matched scene-diversity + demo-count controls — the briefing already says this; elevate it from "actionable insight" to a gating precondition, since without it neither H1 nor H4 produces an attributable result.

### D3 (Direction review)

Topic: Cross-embodiment transfer of a whole-body **loco-MANIPULATION** policy across humanoid morphologies via a phase-anchored / kinematically-aligned shared representation, at [[2605.23733|Any2Any]]'s ~1%-of-from-scratch compute, that **beats a one-time-trained generalist** ([[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] / [[2602.02960|EAGLE]]) below a measurable crossover, with the transferable structure **localized to the dynamics-sensitive pathway**.

The bet (verbatim from First-principles): *"On whole-body loco-MANIPULATION (not locomotion), a cross-embodiment adapter transfers a pretrained policy at Any2Any's ~1% of from-scratch compute/data across 4 humanoids via a PHASOR-class phase-anchored representation (90.3% R@1, 1.62 mm next-frame), and — the promoted front-line falsifier — beats a one-time-trained generalist (XHugWBC ~85% of specialists / EAGLE) on cost below a measurable crossover, with the transferable structure localized to the dynamics-sensitive pathway."*

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys humanoid policies).**
- *Core position*: The "~1% compute" headline is a sim-tracking number, not a deployment number. The bet conflates whole-body **tracking** transfer (Any2Any, PHASOR, VENOM all measure MPJPE/RMSE on retargeted motion) with whole-body **loco-manipulation**, where the hard part is contact, payload-induced balance disturbance, and grasp stability — none of which a phase manifold or a kinematic-alignment matrix touches.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2606.03297|SplitAdapter]] (Samsung, single G1) shows that the moment you add an object, a *frozen* loco-manip policy degrades hard with payload (base policy 16/27 real full-task at mixed loads, 6kg = OOD); robustness came from a **load-factorized** adapter (FiLM into the frozen policy), not from a morphology-invariant representation. [[2605.23733|Any2Any]]'s own ablation says LoRA had to go into the *actor backbone + I/O projections* (the dynamics pathway) — i.e. the transferable thing is body dynamics, and dynamics is exactly what changes most under contact load.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Before committing, run the cheapest possible falsifier: take the Any2Any source tracker, add a 4kg box task, transfer to one new body, and see whether the ~1% still holds *with the object in the loop*. If it doesn't, the whole loco-manip premise is dead and you saved a year.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card is honest that the locomotion version is **consensus-solved** ([[2512.00971|H-Zero]] 81% unseen, [[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] 100% survival/19 sim+7 real, [[2505.05753|Embodiment-Scaling-Laws]] 817 bodies), and correctly identifies that the literature *stops at locomotion*. The genuinely open contributions are narrow: (i) the loco-manip extension, and (ii) the transfer-vs-generalist **crossover curve**, which no cited paper plots.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2606.16696|VENOM]] already pushes cross-embodiment *whole-body tracking past locomotion* across 5 humanoids (RMSE 0.42-0.58 rad) but via a learned shared trunk, no manipulation coupling and no cost-vs-distance study; [[2412.15166|Human-Humanoid]] (UDH) already does cross-body whole-body *loco-manip* transfer (5.81h→0.97h, box-carrying UDH→NAVIAI/H1) but adversarial-IL, not phase-anchored, and no crossover. So each *half* of the bet exists; the **conjunction** does not.
- *Only-this-perspective*: H1 (phase beats joint-space) and H4 (phase is loco-biased, weak on manipulation) are in tension with each other inside the same card — if H4 is true, phase is the *wrong* invariant for the manipulation half, which is precisely where the novelty is claimed to live. The card half-admits this by routing non-periodic skills to [[2604.19734|UniT]]. That is a contradiction, not a hedge.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The "whitespace" is an artifact of slicing the axis finely enough to exclude every neighbor. Cross-embodiment loco-manip transfer is *substantively* done by [[2412.15166|Human-Humanoid]]; the transfer-vs-scale question is *answered in spirit* by [[2511.07820|SONIC]] (99.6% OOD by scaling from scratch) and [[2602.05791|XHugWBC]] (a fine-tune of the generalist already gives +10% over specialists) — which suggests the crossover favors the generalist, the opposite of the bet.
- *Evidence the card under-weights*: XHugWBC's own result — *"fine-tuning the generalist improved sample efficiency and gave up to 10% higher peak vs from-scratch specialists"* — means the generalist is ALSO a cheap-transfer substrate. The bet's clean dichotomy (cheap-PEFT-transfer vs frozen-generalist) is false: the strongest baseline is *fine-tune the generalist*, which combines both and likely dominates. If true, H5 falsifies the bet.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The real experiment isn't "PEFT-transfer vs frozen generalist," it's "PEFT a *specialist* source vs PEFT the *generalist* source." If PEFT-the-generalist wins (very plausible), the contribution collapses to "fine-tuning works," which is not publishable as a thesis.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The cheap-transfer narrative is structurally favored by *academic* labs (no 9,000-GPU-hour budget, can't train SONIC's 21k GPU-h or 817-body scaling runs), while the generalist-scale narrative is owned by industry (NVIDIA GR00T, Shanghai AI Lab's XHugWBC/H-Zero, the LimX team behind Any2Any). The bet is the academic-affordable side of a field-structuring tension — which is *exactly why it's attractive and why it may be a treadmill*.
- *Strongest evidence*: The vendor-platform list is telling — Any2Any transfers across **LimX Oli/Luna** (LimX's own robots) + G1/H1; the benchmarks are vendor-coupled (no graded morphology-distance suite exists for bipedal loco-manip; [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]] only covers 3 closely-related Unitree bodies, [[2505.14986|AnyBody]] is manipulation-only). Whoever defines the morphology-distance benchmark for loco-manip captures the citation surface — that benchmark, not the method, is the durable asset.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The highest-ROI move is to *publish the missing benchmark* (graded morphology-distance × loco-manip), not the method. The method will be beaten in 6 months by the next foundation model; the benchmark gets cited by both camps for years.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the modular-vs-end-to-end / adapt-vs-scale cycle replaying on humanoids. Every "cheap structured transfer beats brute scale" bet (hand-designed features vs deep nets; modular SLAM vs end-to-end driving; PEFT/adapters vs full-finetune in NLP) won *briefly on a compute budget* and then lost to scale once the foundation model arrived.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own table already shows the arc in miniature — [[2606.01851|PHASOR]] (engineered phase invariant) vs [[2606.03985|Humanoid-GPT]] (2B frames, transfer *emergent from scale*) vs [[2511.07820|SONIC]] (100M frames, *scale is the transfer*). PEFT-in-NLP rhymes exactly: LoRA was indispensable at GPT-3 scale for budget-bound labs, never beat full-finetune on quality, and the "structure" (low-rank) turned out to be a compute story, not a knowledge story.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Frame the contribution as *the crossover law itself* — "below X GPU-hours and Y morphology distance, localized transfer dominates; above it, scale wins" — because that statement survives the next foundation model, whereas "our adapter beats today's generalist" expires the day GR00T-N2 ships.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic ⟂ card (H5)*: card says cheap PEFT-transfer beats a *frozen* one-time generalist below a crossover; skeptic says the right baseline is *fine-tuning the generalist*, and XHugWBC's own +10% fine-tune result implies the generalist-as-source wins — flipping H5's verdict.
   - *Academic internal (H1 ⟂ H4)*: H1 stakes the thesis on phase being THE invariant; H4 concedes phase is loco-biased and weak on the manipulation half — but manipulation is where the novelty is. The invariant that carries the claimed-novel half is admitted to be the weak one.
   - *Practitioner ⟂ Academic*: practitioner says tracking-transfer numbers (MPJPE/R@1) don't transfer to contact loco-manip; academic treats VENOM/PHASOR tracking metrics as evidence for the loco-manip bet. SplitAdapter is the wedge — it shows contact/load needs a *different* (load-factorized) adapter than a morphology invariant.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: HISTORIAN + ECONOMIST converge — reframe the deliverable as a *crossover law / benchmark*, not a method, because both the prior-wave pattern and the incentive structure say the method expires and the law/benchmark endures. Most decision-useful.
   - *Weakest*: a naive optimist reading of H1 in isolation (phase is the universal invariant) — directly undercut by the card's own H4 and by Humanoid-GPT/SONIC showing transfer emerges from scale without any engineered phase.

3. **Resolving question.** *At equal adaptation budget on a contact-rich loco-manip task with a held-out body, does PEFT-ing a specialist source (Any2Any-style) beat PEFT-ing/fine-tuning the XHugWBC generalist source?* If the generalist-source wins, H5 falsifies and the thesis collapses to "fine-tuning works"; if the specialist-source wins below a crossover, the bet survives.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective agrees the **loco-MANIPULATION + cost-vs-morphology-distance crossover** is genuinely unmeasured — no cited or discovered paper plots transfer cost vs morphology distance on contact-rich whole-body loco-manip ([[2412.15166|Human-Humanoid]] does the transfer but no crossover; [[2606.03297|SplitAdapter]] does load-adaptation but single-body; [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]/[[2505.14986|AnyBody]] lack a graded loco-manip distance suite). The *empirical curve* is real whitespace even if the *method framing* is contested.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** **Actuation/torque-density and control-frequency heterogeneity.** Every transfer cell assumes kinematic/phase alignment suffices, but humanoids differ most in *actuator bandwidth, torque limits, and contact-stiffness* — a phase schedule or a LoRA residual cannot synthesize torque a target body physically cannot produce. No hypothesis tests whether the "morphology distance" that matters for loco-manip is *kinematic* (what AnyBody measures) or *dynamic/actuation* (what nobody measures). The cliff in H3 may be an actuation cliff mislabeled as a kinematic one.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** D3 bets that whole-body loco-manipulation has a morphology-invariant structure (phase + end-effector goals) that a cheap adapter can transfer across humanoids at ~1% compute and that this beats a one-time generalist below a crossover. The locomotion version of this is *consensus-solved* and the card knows it; the two genuinely open pieces are the **loco-manipulation extension** and the **transfer-vs-generalist crossover curve**, neither landed by any single existing paper. But the bet has two structural weaknesses: its headline evidence (Any2Any/PHASOR/VENOM) is whole-body *tracking*, not contact loco-manip, and its falsifier (H5) is framed against a *frozen* generalist when the strongest real baseline is a *fine-tuned* generalist that fuses both regimes. This is a DE-RISK, not a clean COMMIT: the empirical whitespace is real, but the method framing must change from "our adapter wins" to "here is the crossover law and the benchmark that measures it."

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The loco-manip + cost-vs-morphology-distance crossover is genuinely unmeasured.* (All five support; none challenges.) [[2412.15166|Human-Humanoid]] transfers loco-manip but plots no crossover; [[2606.03297|SplitAdapter]] is single-body; no graded loco-manip distance benchmark exists.
2. *Each half of the bet already exists separately; the conjunction does not.* (Academic, Skeptic support; Practitioner neutral.) VENOM = cross-body whole-body tracking past locomotion; Human-Humanoid = cross-body loco-manip transfer; neither is phase-anchored + localized + crossover-instrumented together.
3. *The strongest baseline is "fine-tune the generalist," and it may beat cheap-transfer.* (Skeptic, Economist, Historian support; card's H5 challenges.) XHugWBC fine-tune gives +10% over specialists — the generalist is itself a cheap-transfer substrate, threatening the bet's clean dichotomy.
4. *Tracking metrics (MPJPE/R@1) overstate loco-manip transferability.* (Practitioner strong; Academic supports; card challenges by citing them as evidence.) SplitAdapter shows contact/payload needs a load-factorized adapter, orthogonal to a morphology invariant.
5. *The durable asset is the crossover law / benchmark, not the method.* (Historian, Economist strong; Practitioner sympathetic.) The method expires at the next foundation model; the graded morphology-distance × loco-manip benchmark gets cited by both camps.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Historian's PEFT-in-NLP analogy and the Economist's compute-budget framing are the *same* observation: LoRA/adapters win precisely and only in the budget-bound regime, and "structure" is really a compute story. This unifies with the Skeptic's "fine-tune-the-generalist" baseline — all three say the bet is a *transient-regime* claim. That is not fatal: it tells you the contribution must BE the regime boundary (the crossover), because the boundary is the part that doesn't expire.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then commit narrowly.** Do NOT commit to "phase-anchored adapter beats generalist" as the headline. (a) First-week cheap falsifier (Practitioner): take the Any2Any source tracker, add a single contact loco-manip task (box-carry, 4kg), transfer to ONE held-out body — does the ~1% hold *with the object in the loop*? If no, drop. (b) If yes, run the resolving experiment (H5-real): PEFT a *specialist* source vs fine-tune the *XHugWBC generalist* source at matched budget on contact loco-manip with a held-out body. (c) Reframe the deliverable as the **crossover law + the missing benchmark**: a graded morphology-distance × loco-manip suite (extend SkillBench beyond 3 Unitree bodies; borrow AnyBody's interpolation/extrapolation/composition splits onto bipedal loco-manip), reporting transfer cost vs distance vs the generalist baseline. Concrete next experiment = (b), because it resolves the biggest contradiction and is cheap relative to a full method paper.

**(5) Frontier question.** Is the "morphology distance" that governs the loco-manip transfer cliff *kinematic* (joint topology, what AnyBody measures) or *dynamic/actuation* (torque density, bandwidth, contact stiffness — what nobody measures)? If it is actuation-dominated, then phase/kinematic invariants are anchored to the wrong axis, and the entire transfer-vs-scale framing should be re-cast on an actuation-distance metric — which would change which method, and which camp, wins.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded against `Capability/Whole-Body.md` lines 1077-1164 (D3). All 11 spot-checked IDs resolve in `_KnowledgeHub_/`; the briefing's structural reading of the card is faithful.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Loco-manip + cost-vs-morphology-distance crossover is genuinely unmeasured | **9** | Strongest claim. The card itself concedes it everywhere: H3/H5 are flagged "open contribution," SkillBench is "composition-instrumented, not transfer-instrumented" with "only 3 closely-related Unitree morphologies (no graded distance gradient)," AnyBody is "manipulation-only, no bipedal whole-body body." No single row plots transfer-cost-vs-distance on loco-manip. Verifiable from the card; I deducted 1 because "no benchmark exists" is an absence-of-evidence claim that a fast-moving 2606 literature could already be falsifying. |
| 2 | Each half exists separately; the conjunction does not | **8** | Well-supported and the card supplies the receipts: VENOM (2606.16696) = "whole-body tracking past locomotion" but "no manipulation-coupling and no transfer-cost-vs-morphology-distance study"; Human-Humanoid (2412.15166) lands the loco-manip *core* (5.81h→0.97h, box-carry) but is adversarial-IL on a digital-human prototype, not phase-anchored + localized + crossover-instrumented. The triple-conjunction (phase-anchored AND pathway-localized AND crossover-instrumented) genuinely has no occupant. Minus 2: "phase-anchored" is one specific instantiation of a broad invariant class, so the whitespace is partly a definitional artifact of how narrowly the conjunction is drawn. |
| 3 | The strongest baseline is "fine-tune the generalist," and it may beat cheap-transfer | **8** | This is the sharpest and most damaging finding, and the card *under-plays it against itself*. XHugWBC (2602.05791) reports "fine-tune +10% peak" in the card's own table — i.e. the generalist is already a cheap-transfer substrate. H5 as written falsifies against a *frozen/one-time* generalist, the weaker strawman. The fine-tuned-generalist baseline is the real null hypothesis and it is not the one H5 tests. Minus 2: "+10% peak" is a single number whose adaptation budget is unstated, so whether it actually beats Any2Any-PEFT *at matched budget on loco-manip* is unproven in either direction. |
| 4 | Tracking metrics (MPJPE/R@1) overstate loco-manip transferability | **7** | Correct in spirit and the card half-admits it: PHASOR's caveat is "weaker for non-periodic fine manipulation," H4 explicitly hedges phase vs manipulation. The "SplitAdapter shows contact/payload needs a load-factorized adapter, orthogonal to a morphology invariant" link is the strongest single piece of corroboration: 2606.03297 factorizes load context from dynamics, a regime morphology-invariance does not address. Minus 3: the claim that retrieval/MPJPE *overstate transferability* is asserted, not measured. R@1 collapsing on contact tasks is plausible but no cited row demonstrates a tracking-metric-vs-task-success divergence; it is the reviewer's prior, not a result. |
| 5 | The durable asset is the crossover law / benchmark, not the method | **6** | The most rhetorically attractive and the least falsifiable. "The method expires at the next foundation model" is a narrative prior (PEFT-in-NLP analogy), not a finding about D3. Benchmarks also expire (HumanoidBench is already being superseded in this very vault), and a *graded morphology-distance loco-manip suite* is a large, expensive, contested-design artifact that may never reach community consensus. The Historian/Economist framing is internally consistent but self-reinforcing across three perspectives that share one premise (see Bias Check). Defensible strategic advice, weak as a "finding." |

**Mean confidence: 7.6. Lowest: 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 5 ("the durable asset is the benchmark, not the method," confidence 6) is the least reliable, but it is *advice* dressed as a finding. The weakest **factual** claim load-bearing for the whole bet is the H5 core in Finding 3: that **cheap localized PEFT beats a fine-tuned generalist at matched budget on whole-body loco-manipulation.** The card asserts a crossover exists; the briefing asserts the fine-tuned generalist may win; neither has the datum.

What would verify it: a single matched-budget run — Any2Any-style PEFT of a specialist source vs fine-tuning the XHugWBC generalist source — on *one* contact loco-manip task (box-carry) with *one* held-out body, reporting final SR and total adaptation cost for both arms. This is exactly the briefing's experiment (b), and it is correctly identified as the resolving experiment. Until that number exists, the entire "transfer-vs-generalist crossover" contribution rests on an unmeasured inequality, and the sign could go either way. Note also: XHugWBC's "+10% fine-tune" is on whole-body *tracking/locomotion*, not loco-manip — so even the one number cited in support of the threat is off-axis from the task the bet lives on.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**Over-dominated: the Historian + Economist axis (a "compute-regime" coalition).** The briefing's own "hidden cross-perspective connection" admits it: the Historian's PEFT-in-NLP analogy and the Economist's compute-budget framing "are the same observation," and both fuse with the Skeptic's fine-tune-the-generalist baseline. That is three of five perspectives converging on one premise — "structure is really a compute story, the method is transient." Presented as triangulation, this is actually a single argument counted three times. It drives Findings 3 and 5 and the entire "reframe as the crossover law" thesis.

The **Practitioner** is the genuinely independent voice and is correctly weighted (the first-week falsifier in (a) is the best part of the briefing). The **Academic** is the most muted: there is little engagement with whether a *phase manifold* is the right scientific object at all, versus the briefing's frontier question (actuation vs kinematic distance) which quietly concedes the Academic's central concern was under-explored. Net: not balanced; the synthesis leans compute-economic and should discount the apparent 3-way agreement to roughly 1.5 independent votes.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The hardware/controls engineer (sim-to-real and actuation realist).** Every cited transfer result that matters for the bet — Any2Any, PHASOR, VENOM, H-Zero — is dominated by simulation or motion-retrieval metrics (R@1, MPJPE, MRR, tracking SR). The bet is about *contact loco-manipulation*, where the binding constraint is not kinematic alignment but torque density, actuator bandwidth, contact stiffness, and the sim-to-real gap under load. This perspective changes the conclusion in two ways:

1. It promotes the briefing's own **frontier question** from a footnote to the central risk: if morphology distance is actuation-dominated (torque/bandwidth/stiffness) rather than kinematic (joint topology, what AnyBody measures), then phase/kinematic invariants are anchored to the wrong axis and the *entire* transfer-vs-scale framing is mis-cast. The briefing names this but does not let it reorder the recommendation — it should. The actuation-distance metric is plausibly the real contribution, not the kinematic one.
2. It reframes Finding 4: a ~1% transfer that holds in sim with the object "in the loop" may collapse on hardware precisely because the held-out body's actuators saturate differently under the same 4 kg payload. The Practitioner's first-week falsifier (a) is run in sim; a hardware engineer would insist the falsifier is only meaningful *on metal*, which changes the cost and timeline of the de-risk.

This sixth angle does not kill the direction, but it would move the "concrete next experiment" from (b) the sim crossover toward an actuation-distance characterization, and it suggests the durable asset (Finding 5) might be an *actuation-distance* benchmark, not a kinematic one.

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: A−.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing. It correctly separates the consensus-solved locomotion case from the two genuinely open pieces, it is faithful to the card (verified: anchors are tracking/retrieval not contact loco-manip, exactly as charged), and the DE-RISK-not-COMMIT recommendation with a first-week falsifier is precisely the right posture for a direction whose headline evidence is off-axis from its claim. The reason it is not an A: the synthesis lets a single compute-regime premise masquerade as three-perspective agreement (Bias Check), and it states the most decision-relevant idea — the actuation-vs-kinematic distance question — as a closing "frontier question" rather than as the risk that should reorder the plan.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**

1. **Rewrite H5's falsifier against the FINE-TUNED generalist, not the frozen one.** The card's own XHugWBC row says "fine-tune +10%." The real null hypothesis is "fine-tune the generalist at matched budget," and the current H5 tests a strawman. This is a one-line change to the card with large consequences for what the bet claims.
2. **Promote the actuation-vs-kinematic-distance question into H3.** H3 currently orders bodies by AnyBody's *kinematic* distance. Add the competing hypothesis that the transfer cliff is actuation-dominated (torque density/bandwidth/contact stiffness) and make the distance-axis itself a measured outcome, not an assumption. If actuation wins, the method and the benchmark both change.
3. **Make the deliverable the benchmark, and say so in the Thesis.** The briefing's conclusion (the durable asset is the crossover law + graded loco-manip suite) is sound but lives only in the synthesis. The card's Thesis still reads "our adapter wins." Reframe it to "here is the crossover law and the benchmark that measures it," extending SkillBench beyond its 3 Unitree bodies with AnyBody-style interpolation/extrapolation/composition splits onto bipedal loco-manip.
4. **Gate the whole direction on experiment (a).** The first-week falsifier — Any2Any source tracker + one 4kg box-carry task + one held-out body, does ~1% hold with the object in the loop — should be stated as a kill-switch in the card's Risks callout, ideally with a hardware check, not only a sim check.

---

### D4 (Direction review)

Topic: D4 — Whole-Body Synthetic Data Generation. The bet (verbatim from First-principles): "(i) From-scratch generation ([[2606.05160|GRAIL]] 81.4% SR, synthetic-only 90% real) breaks the *diversity ceiling* a single-seed route ([[2605.27724|HumanoidMimicGen]] 0.89 PSR, [[2510.11258|DemoHLM]]) hits ... and (ii) enforced synthesized contact-fidelity → downstream-SR is *monotone* ([[2606.05160|GRAIL]]'s 0.90% penetration / 88.9% tracking), so feasibility-enforcement during generation is a quantitative trainability predictor, with co-training lifting real SR 0.51→0.71 (+20%)."

Novelty stress-test (alphaxiv `discover_papers` worked): the "whole-body feasibility is the lever" first principle is settled (the card concedes this). The two surviving prongs were probed. H4 (feasibility→trainability) is **partially pre-empted**: [[2509.26633|OmniRetarget]] already shows across four retargeting methods that penetration/foot-skating/contact-preservation predict downstream RL SR on whole-body loco-manip (OmniRetarget 82.2% vs VideoMimic 3.85% robot-object; GMR's 0.99 contact-preservation but high penetration → lower SR), and [[2505.12705|DreamGen]] reports >90% Pearson generation-quality→SR. H1 (seed-vs-from-scratch diversity ceiling) is **still whitespace**: no paper runs the matched-budget plateau comparison.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The win that actually ships is co-training, not the diversity-ceiling theory. HumanoidMimicGen's 0.51→0.71 real-world lift and GRAIL's 90%/84%/80% real G1 are the only numbers a deployment cares about; the seed-vs-from-scratch curve is a sim-side ablation that doesn't change what you put on the robot.
- *Strongest evidence*: HumanoidMimicGen co-train 0.51→0.71 (+20% over real-only); GRAIL synthetic-only real G1 90% stair-climb; DemoHLM zero-shot G1 5/5 LiftBox. Every real-robot number in the card comes from a seed-route or asset-route system that already exists and is reproducible.
- *Only-this-perspective*: A from-scratch generator (GRAIL) replaces teleop cost with a pipeline that needs 3D assets + a video foundation model + a 4D-HOI reconstruction stack + a pretrained whole-body controller. That engineering burden is invisible in the card's "removes the per-demo human" framing — the per-demo human is gone, but a 5-stage asset/video pipeline now has to be built and maintained per task family. Measure generator-build hours, not just demos-per-seed.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's H4 ("enforced contact-fidelity predicts trainability") is not whitespace — it is the explicit, already-published result of OmniRetarget, and the >90% Pearson correlation of DreamGen Bench. The genuinely open question is narrower than the card states: whether a *continuously swept* generation-time contact-enforcement knob traces a *monotone* curve on *whole-body coupled-trajectory* penetration (vs the 4-point cross-method comparison OmniRetarget ran).
- *Strongest evidence*: OmniRetarget Table II: across PHC/GMR/VideoMimic/OmniRetarget, lower penetration + zero foot-skating → higher RL SR (82.2% vs 3.85% robot-object; 94.73% vs 51.75% robot-terrain), and GMR's 0.99 contact-preservation-but-high-penetration → lower SR is exactly the "contact alone is insufficient, feasibility must be enforced as whole-body dynamics" point the card credits to HumanoidMimicGen's 0.33→0.89.
- *Only-this-perspective*: H1 is the only prong the literature has *not* answered. Re-frame the bet around H1 (the seed-diversity plateau) and demote H4 to a calibration baseline citing OmniRetarget + DreamGen — otherwise a reviewer who knows OmniRetarget will reject H4 as re-derived.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or solved).**
- *Core position*: "From-scratch breaks the seed's diversity ceiling" smuggles in an unfair comparison: GRAIL's diversity comes from its *asset pool + video-foundation-model prior*, which is itself a captured-data dependency, not a true from-scratch free lunch. The "ceiling" may just be the seed route having a smaller input corpus, not a structural property of seeding.
- *Strongest evidence*: GRAIL's own "what's missing" cell: "generation fidelity is bounded by the asset pool + video-prior quality." Swap a 20,000-asset pool for a 1-seed input and the comparison is apples-to-oranges. CLAIMS (2602.21599) already shows a *seed-free* generator (text-conditioned diffusion + difficulty curriculum) pushing past a fixed manifold (OOD failure 41.7%→23.1%, VLM difficulty rising monotonically) — so "an automated generator escapes a fixed diversity manifold" is already demonstrated, just not as a head-to-head vs a seed route.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The falsifier the card writes ("if the seed route's diversity does not plateau below from-scratch") is the *likely* outcome once you equalize input-corpus size. The honest test isn't "from-scratch vs seed," it's "diversity-SR vs input-information-content," holding the *information injected* constant — and that may show no architectural advantage to from-scratch at all.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
- *Core position*: This entire sub-field is an NVIDIA-anchored narrative: HumanoidMimicGen, GRAIL, DreamGen, and Genie Sim 3.0 all carry Fan/Zhu/NVIDIA authorship, and the "synthesize, don't teleoperate" thesis directly sells GPU-simulation compute and the Isaac/Genie stack. The benchmark substrate (Genie Sim 3.0, HumanoidBench, DreamGen Bench) is vendor-supplied, so "synthetic data wins" is measured on yardsticks the vendor controls.
- *Strongest evidence*: Anchor/target papers cluster on the same lab (HumanoidMimicGen authors Lin/Mandlekar/Fan/Zhu; GRAIL authors incl. Fan/Zhu/Kautz; DreamGen incl. Fan/Zhu/Fox). Genie Sim 3.0 supplies "10,000+ h synthetic data, R²=0.94" — the simulation substrate the whole thesis runs inside.
- *Only-this-perspective*: An academic group betting on D4 is competing on the *one axis* (raw synthetic-data scale + sim fidelity) where a GPU vendor has a structural moat. The defensible academic wedge is the *measurement* nobody is incentivized to publish — the diversity-ceiling / information-content curve (H1) — because it could show synthetic scale has diminishing returns, which is anti-narrative for the compute sellers.

**5. HISTORIAN (seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the MimicGen → DexMimicGen → HumanoidMimicGen lineage re-running the 2017-era domain-randomization and procedural-generation playbook one rung up the DoF ladder, and it will hit the same wall every synthetic-data wave hits: the generator's prior *is* the diversity ceiling, and "more synthetic data" plateaus exactly where the generator's coverage stops.
- *Strongest evidence*: MimicGen (2310.17596) → DexMimicGen (90% from 40 demos vs 0% from 4) → HumanoidMimicGen (0.89 vs DexMimicGen+ 0.33) is a clean three-step escalation; each step the "valid demo" yield (SkillMimicGen 65% vs 14%) is the recurring bottleneck. The pixel-vs-latent and DR-vs-real history says the asset/video-prior route (GRAIL) is this wave's "render-more-variation," and it saturates against real-world contact statistics the generator never saw.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The card's two prongs are the *same* historical question asked twice — H1 (does the seed cap diversity?) and H4 (does feasibility cap trainability?) are both "where does the generator's prior run out?" Prior waves resolved this not with a plateau plot but by showing co-training with a *little* real data dominates *any* amount of synthetic (the GRAIL synthetic-only-vs-co-trained tension). Bet on characterizing the real-data crossover (H2), not the synthetic ceiling.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs the card's H1 (and Academic backs the skeptic)*: card claims from-scratch *structurally* breaks the seed ceiling; skeptic says the "ceiling" is just smaller input-corpus information (GRAIL's 20k-asset pool vs 1 seed), so the comparison is rigged and likely shows no architectural advantage once input-information is equalized.
   - *Academic vs the card's H4-as-novelty*: card lists H4 as an un-run "feasibility→trainability curve"; academic says OmniRetarget (penetration→SR across 4 methods) + DreamGen (>90% Pearson) already establish it directionally, so only the *swept-knob continuous-monotone* version is open.
   - *Practitioner vs Economist/Historian*: practitioner says co-training real-robot lifts (0.51→0.71, 90% real G1) are the deliverable; economist/historian say those numbers live on vendor benchmarks and re-run a saturating wave, so the real-robot SR is the *narrative*, not the *finding*.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only perspective grounded in a specific contradicting result (OmniRetarget Table II) that directly refutes the card's H4-novelty wording, and it survives checking against the paper content.
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — the authorship-concentration observation is real and useful for positioning, but it argues about *who benefits*, not about whether the bet is *true*; it cannot by itself sink or save the direction.

3. **Resolving question (biggest contradiction = is H1 real whitespace?)**: *When you equalize the total information injected into the generator (seed-route input-bits vs from-scratch asset/video-prior input-bits), does generalization-SR-vs-diversity still show the seed route plateauing below from-scratch — or do both routes lie on one input-information curve with no architectural advantage?* This single experiment decides whether H1 is a structural finding or an artifact of unequal inputs.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective accepts that the per-demo human is removable and that whole-body feasibility-enforcement (not contact-only) is what makes a synthesized coupled demo trainable — HumanoidMimicGen 0.33→0.89, DemoHLM thousands-from-one-seed, GRAIL synthetic-only real G1, and OmniRetarget's penetration→SR all converge on this. The first principle is settled; the bet must live downstream of it.

5. **Blind spot (no perspective addressed).** None addressed **failure-mode / safety distribution of synthesized demos**: a feasibility-enforced generator optimizes for *valid* trajectories, which systematically under-samples the near-failure, recovery, and contact-loss regimes a deployed policy most needs — synthetic corpora may be *too clean*, training brittle policies that never saw the unstable states real teleop accidentally captures. No row, hypothesis, or risk measures the recovery/edge-case coverage of synthesized data.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** D4's first principle (a demo is just a physically-feasible coupled trajectory, and feasibility is enforceable not human-exclusive) is correct and already settled by three independent groups, which the card honestly concedes — so the direction's value rests entirely on its two downstream prongs. Of those, H4 (enforced contact-fidelity predicts trainability) is *partially pre-empted*: OmniRetarget already shows penetration/foot-skating/contact-preservation rank-order downstream RL success on whole-body loco-manip, and DreamGen reports >90% Pearson, leaving only the continuous swept-knob monotone-curve version genuinely open. H1 (from-scratch breaks the seed's diversity ceiling) is real un-run whitespace, but its headline framing is fragile: the comparison is confounded by unequal input-information (GRAIL's asset+video pool vs one seed), and CLAIMS already shows a seed-free generator escaping a fixed manifold. The committable nugget is narrow and measurement-shaped — characterize the diversity-SR curve *as a function of injected information* and the real-data crossover — not the headline "from-scratch wins."

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The per-demo human is removable and whole-body (not contact-only) feasibility is the trainability lever.* (Support: all five perspectives; evidence HumanoidMimicGen 0.33→0.89, DemoHLM, GRAIL, OmniRetarget. Challenged by: none — this is the settled control, not the bet.)
2. *H4 is already established directionally by OmniRetarget + DreamGen.* (Support: Academic, Historian; OmniRetarget 82.2% vs 3.85% with penetration as the discriminator, DreamGen >90% Pearson. Challenged by: card framing, which lists H4 as un-run — the swept-continuous-monotone version is the only defensible residue.)
3. *Co-training a little real data with synthetic dominates synthetic-only on contact-precision tasks (H2).* (Support: Practitioner, Historian; HumanoidMimicGen 0.51→0.71, GRAIL synthetic-only holds for dynamics-dominated stair-climb. Challenged by: the from-scratch-purist reading of H1, which wants synthetic-only to suffice.)
4. *H1's "from-scratch beats seed" is confounded by unequal input-information and partly pre-shown by CLAIMS.* (Support: Skeptic, Economist; GRAIL's own asset-pool caveat, CLAIMS 41.7%→23.1% manifold-escape. Challenged by: card, which treats the seed plateau as structural.)
5. *The synthetic-data thesis is vendor-anchored (NVIDIA: Fan/Zhu/Kautz across HumanoidMimicGen/GRAIL/DreamGen/Genie Sim) and measured on vendor benchmarks.* (Support: Economist, Historian. Challenged by: Practitioner — the real G1 transfers are still real regardless of who funded them.)

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Skeptic's "equalize the input-information" critique and the Economist's "compute-sellers want synthetic scale to look unboundedly useful" point are the same finding from two angles: if diversity-SR is governed by *injected information content* rather than *generation architecture*, then the entire "synthesize more" narrative has a hidden diminishing-returns ceiling — and the academic wedge is precisely to *measure that ceiling*, which is the one result the vendor incentive structure suppresses. H1, re-framed as an information-content curve, is simultaneously the novel science and the anti-narrative result.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK (re-frame), do not commit as written.** Do not commit to the headline "from-scratch breaks the seed ceiling" — it is confounded and H4 is half-claimed by OmniRetarget. Instead, **commit to the measurement**: (a) demote H4 to a *calibration baseline* explicitly citing OmniRetarget Table II + DreamGen Bench, and make the contribution the *continuous swept-knob monotone curve on whole-body coupled-trajectory penetration* (which neither ran). (b) Re-pose H1 as the **diversity-SR-vs-injected-information curve**: the concrete next experiment is to match *information budget* (not just demo count) between a seed route (HumanoidMimicGen/DemoHLM, sweep #seeds) and an asset route (GRAIL/CLAW, sweep asset-pool breadth), plot generalization SR on DreamGen Bench's held-out GR1 splits (22 novel behaviors, 10 unseen-env), and locate whether both lie on one input-information curve or genuinely diverge. (c) Add the **recovery/near-failure coverage** axis (the blind spot) as a third measured quantity — synthesized-corpus failure-state coverage vs teleop-corpus — since that is the unguarded place a reviewer or a real deployment will break the "synthetic suffices" claim.

**(5) Frontier question that changes everything.** Is generalization a function of the *information injected into the generator* (seed-bits ⊕ asset/video-prior-bits ⊕ enforced-feasibility-constraints) rather than of the generation architecture — and if so, what is the information-per-demo exchange rate that sets the synthetic-data plateau? Answering it converts D4 from a "which generator wins" race (vendor-dominated, saturating) into a scaling-law for synthetic robot data (architecture-agnostic, academically defensible, and directly predictive of where co-training real data becomes mandatory).

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. The briefing under review is a de-risking re-frame of card D4 (Whole-Body Synthetic Data Generation), Whole-Body.md lines 1165-1251. My job is to find the soft claims, not to ratify the re-frame.*

The briefing's core instinct is right and the re-frame is the correct call: D4's first principle is settled, so the value lives in the two downstream prongs, and both prongs are softer than the card's headline framing admits. But the briefing inherits two unverified external numbers and one un-flagged internal inconsistency that a committee would catch.

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, per key finding)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Per-demo human is removable; whole-body (not contact-only) feasibility is the trainability lever. | **9** | The 0.33→0.89 manipulation-only-vs-whole-body gap (HumanoidMimicGen) plus DemoHLM's independent sim-to-real replication is the strongest leg; this is genuinely the settled control, not a bet. Only docked for resting on two NVIDIA-adjacent sources for the headline number. |
| 2 | H4 already established directionally by OmniRetarget + DreamGen; only the swept-continuous-monotone version is open. | **6** | The OmniRetarget half is verified in-vault (82.20-94.73% downstream RL coincident with near-zero penetration / zero foot-skating, across *discrete* baselines) — that is a rank-order, not a curve, so the calibration is sound. But the DreamGen ">90% Pearson" number is the weak rivet (see Weakest Link), and the briefing imports OmniRetarget's authority without noticing it contradicts D1's own bet. |
| 3 | Co-training a little real data with synthetic dominates synthetic-only on contact-precision tasks (H2). | **6** | HumanoidMimicGen's 0.51→0.71 is one data point at one mix ratio on one system; "dominates on contact-precision tasks" is an extrapolation across a task-property axis the card asserts but no cited paper actually sweeps. Directionally plausible, quantitatively unestablished. |
| 4 | H1's "from-scratch beats seed" is confounded by unequal input-information and partly pre-shown by CLAIMS. | **8** | The confound is real and decisive: GRAIL's asset+video pool is strictly more input information than one seed, so any breadth win is uninterpretable as a generation-architecture result. CLAIMS' 41.7%→23.1% manifold-escape (in-card, verified line 1211) genuinely demonstrates a generator escaping a fixed manifold. Strong critique; docked only because CLAIMS synthesizes tracking/control motion, not object-coupled loco-manip, so it is a near-pre-emption, not an exact one. |
| 5 | The synthetic-data thesis is vendor-anchored (NVIDIA across HumanoidMimicGen/GRAIL/DreamGen/Genie Sim) and measured on vendor benchmarks. | **7** | Provenance claim is checkable and substantially correct; the incentive-structure inference (vendors suppress the diminishing-returns result) is a reasonable hypothesis but is sociology, not evidence, and the Practitioner's rebuttal (real G1 transfers are real regardless of funder) is undersold in the ranking. |

**Mean = 7.2. Min = 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**The claim:** "DreamGen reports >90% Pearson [correlation of generation-quality scores to downstream SR]" — load-bearing because it is half of the evidence that H4 is "already established directionally," and the briefing proposes to *demote H4 to a calibration baseline explicitly citing DreamGen Bench*. If that number is softer than stated, the calibration baseline is softer than stated.

**Why it is weakest:** the in-vault KH note for DreamGen (2505.12705) states only that *"a strong correlation exists between the quality of generated robot videos ... and downstream robot policy performance."* The specific ">90% Pearson" figure appears **only in the card**, not in the extracted note — it is an un-traced number. Pearson r is also fragile to the n of conditions correlated and to whether it is computed over tasks, checkpoints, or runs; "strong correlation" and "r > 0.90" are not interchangeable.

**What would verify it:** pull the DreamGen paper's actual Pearson figure, the exact axis it is computed over (number of points, IF-score and Physics-Alignment-score separately or pooled), and confirm it is whole-trajectory-conditioned rather than a per-frame video-quality proxy. Until then, cite it as "strong reported correlation (exact r unverified in-vault)," not ">90%."

**Runner-up weak link (structural, not numeric):** the briefing treats OmniRetarget as pre-empting D4's H4, but OmniRetarget is the **anchor of D1**, and D1's *own* bet (lines 919-920) explicitly claims the contact-fidelity→trainability regression is "unmeasured" and "the surviving D1 core." So either D1 over-claims its own whitespace or D4's H4 is more pre-empted than D4 admits — the two cards cannot both be right. The briefing correctly senses H4 is half-claimed but does not surface that the other claimant is *this vault's own sibling card*. That internal collision is the single most useful thing to resolve, and it is verification-cheap (re-read D1's bet against OmniRetarget's rank-order result).

---

##### 3. Bias check — dominant perspective

**The Skeptic over-dominated, with the Economist as a tightly-coupled second** (the briefing itself admits in section 3 that these two collapse into one finding). The entire actionable insight is a de-risk/measurement re-frame — demote, re-pose, add a coverage axis — which is the Skeptic's native move. The Practitioner is repeatedly *named* as the dissent but never allowed to win a ranking slot: real sim-to-real G1 transfers (GRAIL 90%/84%/80%, DemoHLM 5/5 LiftBox, DexMimicGen 90%-from-40) are the hardest evidence in the whole card, and they survive every information-content and vendor-incentive critique intact, yet they are filed under "Challenged by: Practitioner" rather than driving a finding. The synthesis would be more honest if "synthetic-only already crosses the sim-to-real gap on real hardware for several whole-body tasks" were finding #2, ahead of the contested H4 calibration. As written, the measurement-purist lens has crowded out the deployment-evidence lens.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — the 6th angle

**The training-dynamics / distribution-coverage perspective (an ML-systems learning-theorist, not a roboticist).** Every finding here treats a demo corpus as a *count* with a *feasibility flag*, and treats "diversity" as a scalar that rises and plateaus. But what actually governs downstream SR is the **support and density of the state-action distribution the policy is trained on** — and synthetic generators have a structural failure mode the briefing's information-content framing cannot see: they oversample the easy interior of the manifold and undersample the boundary/recovery/near-failure states, *regardless of how many bits of asset-prior you inject*. This reframes the briefing's own section-4(c) blind spot from an afterthought into the governing variable: the synthetic-data plateau may be set not by total injected information but by **coverage of the low-density, high-consequence tail** (contact transitions, near-falls, regrasps). If true, the "information-per-demo exchange rate" frontier question (section 5) is mis-specified — the relevant currency is not bits-per-demo but *coverage-of-the-failure-tail-per-demo*, and two generators with identical injected information can land on opposite sides of the co-training-becomes-mandatory line purely from how their sampling concentrates. This perspective would change the conclusion: it predicts H2 (real anchor needed for contact-precision) is not a task-property boundary but a *distribution-tail* boundary, and it makes the recovery-coverage axis the primary measurement, not the third one.

---

##### 5. Overall grade

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest re-frame that correctly refuses to rubber-stamp the card's headline and lands the one critique that matters most (H1's input-information confound, which genuinely invalidates "from-scratch wins" as stated). The information-content scaling-law reframing is a real and defensible academic wedge. It loses the A-range on three fixable defects: an un-traced external number presented as fact, an unspotted D1/D4 internal contradiction, and a synthesis that lets the Skeptic crowd out the hardest deployment evidence.

**Top things to fix, in priority order:**
1. **Trace or soften the DreamGen ">90% Pearson" number** before making DreamGen Bench a named calibration baseline. The KH note only supports "strong correlation." Do not cite a figure the vault cannot reproduce.
2. **Resolve the D1/D4 collision over OmniRetarget.** Decide whether OmniRetarget's discrete-baseline rank-order pre-empts D4's H4 *and* D1's bet, or neither. Both cards currently claim the same whitespace from opposite sides; one must yield. This is the cheapest high-value fix.
3. **Promote the deployment evidence.** Make "synthetic-only already crosses sim-to-real on real G1 for several whole-body tasks" a top-ranked finding; right now the Practitioner's strongest card is buried as a perpetual "challenged-by." Balance the Skeptic.
4. **Re-specify the frontier question's currency.** "Information-per-demo" should be stress-tested against "failure-tail-coverage-per-demo" (the missing 6th perspective). If coverage, not raw bits, sets the plateau, sections 4(c) and 5 need to be rebuilt around it.
5. **Caveat the vendor-suppression inference as a hypothesis, not a finding.** It is a good reason to do the measurement; it is not itself evidence and should not be ranked alongside the empirical findings.
